| [052](ADR-052-shell-registered-tool-dispatch-scope-correction.md) | Does this codebase have a shell command dispatcher that invokes a registered `Tool` while bypassing 006 §3's real capability/approval/provenance pipeline, reachable from real agent execution? | **Judged.** The bypass CODE is real (2026-08-10 gap audit #13, re-verified) — `shell_dispatch.cpp`'s `dispatch_command()` calls `resolved.tool->invoke(argv, ctx)` directly, no gates at all — but it has never been reachable: `command_registry.hpp`'s own pre-existing `RegisteredTool` comment already states this is "the minimum type-erased shape needed to make CommandRegistry's three-way lookup real and testable... NOT an implementation of 006's ten-step pipeline," and `shell_dispatch.cpp` (this session's own earlier ADR-056 already confirmed by name) has zero production call sites — only two test files include it. The REAL, live native-jail shell path (`mediated_shell_dispatch.cpp`) has zero "tool"-related code at all, confirmed by direct search — it doesn't dispatch registered Tools by name today, so there is no bypass to close there either. The audit's other named symptoms (a null-`tool_bridge` crash, collapsed capability/approval denials, no real `call_index` source) don't exist anywhere in this file — they describe requirements for a real integration that has never been built, not a fix to existing code. No functional change: a warning comment lands at the exact `resolved.tool->invoke()` call site and on `RegisteredTool`'s own definition, both stating that any REAL future Tool-dispatch integration (spike or production) must route through `invoke_tool()`, never this closure directly — closing the actual risk (a future maintainer copying the anti-pattern) without inventing unscoped feature work this gap never asked for. `test_shell_runner_proof.cpp` re-verified passing unchanged. Full suite green. |
| [051](ADR-051-workflow-scheduling-shuffle-test.md) | Does 014 §8 G3's own scheduling-shuffle test (shuffling intra-round executor scheduling across 10³ seeds produces identical output; an order-dependent executor is caught) exist anywhere in this codebase? | **Judged.** No (2026-08-10 gap audit #22, re-verified) — confirmed directly, no file matched "shuffle" in the workflow-scheduling sense before this ADR; the M6 breakdown doc's own decisions 5/8 only ever DESIGNED this test. Found and fixed a real, narrower documentation gap along the way: that same breakdown doc's own Phase J completion section silently dropped G3 between "delivered" (only 014 §8 G1/030 §7 G1 claimed) and "explicitly deferred" (G3 absent from that list too) — corrected in the same pass with an honest "Update" note rather than silently rewritten. Built `test_rt_workflow_supervisor_scheduling_shuffle.cpp`, reusing `test_rt_workflow_supervisor_patterns.cpp`'s own FI-1/FI-2 fan-out/fan-in shape (which already proved a narrower, single-hand-picked-delay version of this exact claim) broadened to a real 1000-seed sweep of independently randomized per-branch delays, proven against the CURRENT (post-ADR-037) `rt::ThreadPool`-backed fan-out mechanism, not the historical, now-deleted Quark one the original design prose describes. Self-red-team: a positive-only proof would be vacuous without a real negative control, so a second graph with a deliberately order-dependent executor (leaking its own real completion position into its output via a shared atomic counter) is run across a 200-seed subset and shown to actually diverge — proving the positive result is a real check, not something that would pass regardless. One `WorkflowSupervisor`/`ThreadPool` built once, re-run sequentially across all seeds (never spawned per-seed, matching decision 8's own machine-safety framing). Full suite green (~19s added, an accepted, previously-anticipated cost). |
| [050](ADR-050-usage-usagedetails-canonical-name-reconciliation.md) | Which of 027's vocabulary table and the shipped `Usage` struct is the one that actually drifted? | **Judged.** The CODE, per the audit's own framing (2026-08-10 gap #9) — but re-reading 003 §6 and 004 directly (not the audit's paraphrase) shows both already, consistently, normatively spell it `Usage`, never `UsageDetails`; 027's own table row was the one that disagreed with its own cited source. `tools/naming_lint.py` confirmed this structurally: `Usage` needed an `ae-naming-lint: allow` suppression to avoid being flagged unlisted, exactly the signature a genuinely-drifted name would carry — except the "spec" it drifted from was itself the outlier. `ADR-025` had already explicitly deferred this exact question to its own ADR rather than address it inline. Fixed via one table-row correction (027 §2: `UsageDetails` → `Usage`, marked **ours** with an honest divergence rationale per 027 §1's own required discipline) plus removing the now-unnecessary suppression comment on `core/content.hpp`'s `struct Usage` — no code renamed, since the code was already right. Verified with a real before/after naming-lint run: 96 → 95 suppressed findings, the SAME 202 unlisted names (proving this didn't silently absorb any of ADR-025's own deliberately-deferred bulk-reconciliation backlog). |
| [049](ADR-049-cross-provider-reasoning-exclusion.md) | Does a `Reasoning` content item ever get excluded from a turn's assembled context when it was produced by a DIFFERENT backend than the one currently bound (003 §8 Q2)? | **Judged.** No such check existed anywhere (2026-08-10 gap audit #20, re-verified) — `Reasoning` carried no producer field at all, so the comparison Q2 already resolved (2026-08-04: "included only when it originated from the ChatClientId currently bound... exclusion recorded in the trace") was structurally unimplementable. The audit's own cited blocker (`FailoverChatClient`'s ambiguous identity) is now moot — that type was removed (ADR-036 amendment); the audit's own "real production call site was missed" note is correct in spirit but names the wrong file — `assemble_context()`'s `ContextDrop` mechanism is unused by `AgentSession` at all (confirmed directly against `context_assembly.hpp`'s own top comment), the real site is `run_rounds()`'s direct call to `history_provider_.on_context()`. Fixed: `Reasoning` gains `producer_chat_client_id` (appended last), stamped by `AnthropicChatClient` (the only real producer in this codebase — confirmed OpenAI's backend has no reasoning-trace field on a response message at all) at its own single choke point (`StreamingUpdateAccumulator::release()`), consumed via a new optional `HasProducerChatClientId` concept (ADR-044's `has_agent_description` precedent) so a `ChatClientT` without it gets no filtering rather than a compile error. Self-red-team: threading the agent's compile-time `ChatClientId<...>` policy tag through was considered and rejected in favor of deriving identity from the bound backend's own real construction state (more correct, no concept-breaking plumbing); filtering runs at content-ITEM granularity, not whole-message (proven: a message with both a cross-provider Reasoning item and a Text item keeps the Text, drops only the Reasoning); an empty/unstamped provenance defaults to EXCLUDED (Q2's own allowlist wording), covering `text_derived` leak-scan items and pre-existing records alike. First real producer of `run_event_kind::policy_decision` (013 §1's own long-dormant vocabulary). New test file + Anthropic translation extension, full suite green. |
| [048](ADR-048-outbound-media-capability-gate.md) | When a `ChatRequest`'s history carries `Media` content (image/audio/video/file) the bound backend can't encode, does the caller find out? | **Judged.** No (2026-08-10 gap audit #19, Phase 1 only — Phase 2 real wire encoding stays blocked on RFC 019's blob-store seam, confirmed absent from the tree) — both real backends' outbound `translate_message()` silently drop `Media` unconditionally, regardless of what `ChatClientCapabilities` declares, since neither ever checks the capability bit before not-encoding it. Fixed with `validate_outbound_media_capabilities()` (`core/chat_client.hpp`), wired into `AgentSession::run_model_call()` before any backend branch — a request carrying ungrantable Media now fails closed with a real, attributable error naming the offending message/media_type, and the backend's own `chat()`/`chat_stream()` is never invoked at all. Recurses into `ToolResult::content` too, closing the gap's own named "Media nested inside ToolResult::content... unaddressed by either phase" residual as a direct, low-cost consequence of writing the walk correctly. Scoped strictly to the four `multimodal_in_*` bits — proven that a Reasoning item never trips this gate, and `Citation`/`Custom` are correctly out of scope (003 §1's Citation-as-annotation resolution; Custom has no capability bit at all). Two new test files (pure-function + end-to-end through a real AgentSession run), full suite green. |
| [047](ADR-047-memory-ranking-product-formula.md) | Does `rank_memory_items()` compute 029 §5's own literal ranking formula, a product of salience, recency, and tag/keyword overlap? | **Judged.** No (2026-08-10 gap audit #18, re-verified) — the formula was additive (`salience + keyword_overlap_score`) and never used recency at all; the audit's own first-pass alternative (a full commit-history scan per item) doesn't work against this codebase's actual worktree model (a `Ref` carries no parent/history chain, confirmed directly). Fixed with a real recency signal instead: `MemoryItem::write_seq`, stamped from the memory ref's own append-log `SeqNo` (`rt::AppendLogStore::last_seq`, ADR-037) at write time — O(1), no history scan. The formula is a genuine product of three non-degenerate factors, not the raw signals directly: self-red-team found (before any test ran) that a literal product over raw values is actively broken two ways this codebase genuinely hits — `keyword_overlap_score` is 0 on the ordinary turn (a raw multiplicative 0 would zero out every item's score on most turns), and `MemoryProvider::on_turn_end()` never sets `salience` on extracted items (a raw multiplicative salience factor would make every such item permanently rank-zero). Both got small positive floors instead. Recency is normalized against the current retrieval batch's own maximum `write_seq`, bounded to `[1,2]` — an unnormalized raw SeqNo would eventually let mere recency dominate the whole product regardless of relevance, also caught before testing. New test file (`test_memory_ranking_formula.cpp`) proves recency participates, is bounded (an old-but-far-more-salient item still beats a barely-relevant newer one), a zero-salience item isn't structurally rank-zero, plus determinism and a legacy-record (`write_seq==0`) no-division-by-zero control. Every pre-existing ranking-outcome assertion in `test_memory_provider.cpp`/`test_memory_retrieval_determinism.cpp` re-verified unchanged under the new formula; `examples/08_memory.cpp`'s exact-content-match assertion corrected alongside ADR-046 (same pass). Full suite green. Floor/normalization constants are named as reasoned engineering defaults, not evidence-tuned. |
| [046](ADR-046-memory-confidence-labels-and-system-message-separator.md) | Can the model (or a human) tell a `UserStated` memory fact from a `ModelInferred` guess once it's injected into a turn? | **Judged.** No (2026-08-10 gap audit #17, re-verified) — every `MemoryItem` rendered as bare `item.content` regardless of `origin.source`, even though 029 §3 calls `MemorySource` "a trust signal, not decoration." Re-grounding found the audit's own recommended approach named a real, adjacent bug worth fixing first: `protocol/anthropic/chat_client.hpp`'s `split_system_messages()` concatenated every system message's text with ZERO separator (confirmed directly — `out.system_text += t->text`, no boundary at all; its own pre-existing test only "worked" because its fixture happened to supply a leading space), which would have made a bare label prefix bleed into whatever text followed it. Fixed both: a real `"\n\n"` separator between concatenated system-text fragments (never as a leading/trailing pad, so a lone message's own text is unchanged byte-for-byte), and a confidence label (`⟦memory:user-stated, high confidence⟧` etc.) prepended to every rendered memory item, in BOTH places one reaches an agent as text — default injection AND the `recall(query)` tool's reply, not just one. The label is derived only from the trusted, structured `origin.source` field, never from content. Self-red-team named the adjacent forgery surface the audit itself flagged: `on_turn_end()`'s `ModelInferred` extraction is itself model-generated text, so an item's own content could embed literal label text to impersonate a higher-trust item once concatenated — closed by `neutralize_forged_memory_labels()`, which breaks any literal occurrence of the marker's open token inside retrieved content with a zero-width space before a real label is ever prepended, proven directly (the real marker appears exactly once across two items' rendered text, the genuine one's own label, never twice, even when the hostile item's content contains the exact marker string). Does not touch `ContentItem::tainted`/`origin` (029 §6's actual enforcement, unchanged, still proven by `test_memory_no_authority_laundering.cpp`) and does not address gap #21's broader raw-`ContentItem::text` claim. Full suite green. |
| [045](ADR-045-json-schema-validator-subset.md) | Does this codebase have any way to check a JSON instance against an arbitrary JSON Schema document, closing 006 §8 G2 and 015 §7 G2? | **Judged.** No (2026-08-10 gap audit #3, re-verified) — only schema-generation-from-C++-type machinery existed. Fixed with a deliberately scoped subset validator (`type`/`enum`/`const`/`required`/`properties`/`additionalProperties`/`items`/length+range keywords/local `$ref`), NOT the full 2020-12 vocabulary — `pattern` (regex) is deliberately never implemented, structurally avoiding the whole ReDoS risk category rather than trying to bound one (the codebase has zero `std::regex` usage anywhere, confirmed, so this isn't fixing an existing bug, it's not creating one). Real, reachable value scoped honestly: native tools already have stronger type-directed checking via `schema::from_json<Args>()`; this validator's actual consumer is the non-native (WASM/MCP/A2A) tool-invocation path gap #4's registry design targets — not built yet, so no production call site exists to wire this to today. `validate_schema_well_formed()` does real DFS $ref-cycle detection (015 §7 G2, path-scoped not globally-visited, so a legitimate DAG-shaped schema isn't falsely flagged); `validate_instance()` needs no separate cycle check since a finite instance structurally bounds it — proven directly, not just asserted. Self-red-team caught two real bugs before/via testing: `max_violations` alone doesn't bound total work for a huge-but-valid instance (added `max_nodes_visited`), and a latch-ordering bug where the "budget exceeded" flag silently blocked the violation reporting it (found by the test failing, fixed by reordering). Also fixed a real UB risk (an int64_t round-trip on adversarial numeric input) before it ever reached a test. 22-check new test file, full suite green. |
| [044](ADR-044-agent-workflow-description-version.md) | Do `AgentMetadata`/`Workflow` carry the `description`/`version` identity fields 002 §1 has always specified? | **Judged.** No (2026-08-10 gap audit #2, re-verified). Not new surface — 002 §1/§7 named `{agent_id, description, version}` as agent identity from the start; three independent compilers (A2A Agent Card, Agent YAML, Workflow YAML) hit the identical missing-field gap independently, each honestly recording it rather than working around it. Fixed by adding the fields to both structs and wiring the 4 real call sites (not 5 — MCP's tool listing was never a wiring point). `register_agent<A>()` detects the new statics via `requires`-based `has_agent_description`/`has_agent_version` concepts rather than requiring them, avoiding a breaking migration across every existing `Agent<Derived,...>` conformer. Named, not silently ignored: `Tool<Derived,...>`'s own CRTP `description` convention shares the member name with a different meaning — no actual compile collision (unrelated CRTP bases), but a real "could be confused" risk. Checked, not assumed: grepped for positional `AgentMetadata{...}`/`Workflow{...}` aggregate-init call sites before landing fields inserted mid-struct (zero found — every real site is named-field assignment or a fluent builder). I6 test extended (`test_agent_yaml_compiler.cpp`/`test_workflow_yaml_compiler.cpp`), full suite green. Does not close the A2A modality-declaration gap (still no home for `default_input_modes`/`default_output_modes`) or the `Agent`/`Tool` naming overlap beyond flagging it. |
| [043](ADR-043-ack-durability-policy.md) | Does `rt::AgentSession` acknowledge a turn only after its effects are durable, and if not, is there a real opt-in? | **Judged.** No opt-in existed (2026-08-10 gap audit #15, re-verified). The audit's own cited blocker was wrong on both counts: a `SessionStore` seam already exists and is already wired (it was never meant to be type-erased — a `concept`, matching `ChatClient`/`SecretStore` precedent), and 005 §2 already specifies the exact policy vocabulary (`at_most_once_ack`) the audit reinvented names for. Fixed via two new free functions, `start_run_with_ack_policy()`/`resolve_interaction_with_ack_policy()`, that sequence Store writes around the existing `start_run()`/`resolve_interaction()` calls WITHOUT touching `AgentSession` itself — preserving the file's own already-stated "AgentSession has no ambient Store access, I2" boundary. `require_durable` durably writes both a new per-turn `TurnDeltaRecord` (005 §2 says "history delta," not full history — reuses `rt/message_codec.hpp`'s already-proven Message↔JSON codec, making REAL conversation durability tractable now rather than blocked on `AgentSessionRecord`'s separate, larger, not-yet-built full-history gap) and the existing session bookkeeping record, fail-closed on either failing. Surfaced and fixed a real, latent ADL ambiguity in `message_codec.hpp`'s own unqualified internal calls (collided with `chat_recording.hpp`'s separately-maintained same-named functions the moment one test file included both). Self-red-team named a real residual: delta capture is race-free for sequential single-caller usage (005 §2's own G2 gate) but not for concurrent overlapping calls on one session — would need a `run_rounds()`-internal change deliberately avoided here. New test file (T1-T3) proves at_most_once is a store-free no-op, require_durable's delta round-trips byte-correct through JSON, and durability failure fails closed. Full suite green. Does NOT serialize the whole conversation history or wire a restore path — named, not claimed. |
| [042](ADR-042-context-instructions-taint-channel.md) | Does `ContextContribution.instructions` actually reach the model, and is the channel it rides taint-checked? | **Judged.** No, on both counts (2026-08-10 gap audit #16/#21, re-verified against current code). The real drop site is one level later than the audit implied: `assemble_context()` reads `.instructions` correctly, but `rt::AgentSession::run_rounds()` builds `ChatRequest` without ever consulting it, and `ChatRequest` had no instructions slot to put it in. Separately, `Tainted<T>`/`TaintedText` (Judged as a type, 007 §9 G2) was never used as an actual content-model field anywhere. Fixed by retyping `ContextContribution::instructions` to `TaintedText` (forcing every contributing provider to make an explicit trust decision at construction), then wiring the one real declassification site into `run_rounds()`, which synthesizes a `role::system` Message and prepends it. Self-red-team named two real residuals rather than overclaiming: the type system can't stop a careless/bad-faith wrap (matches `Tainted<T>`'s own documented contract everywhere else it's used), and the new channel has no token-budget enforcement. New tests (`test_context_assembly.cpp` B3-I1-I3, new `test_rt_agent_session_instructions.cpp` T1-T3) prove multi-provider concatenation, correct message shape/position, and that the no-instructions path is byte-identical to before this fix. One pre-existing test (`test_memory_retrieval_determinism.cpp`) broke on `Tainted<T>`'s deliberate lack of `operator==` and was fixed to declassify explicitly before comparing. Full suite 176/176 green. Does NOT close gap #21's broader scope (`ContentItem::text` and friends stay raw/ungated at `cli_chat.cpp`/`mcp/server.hpp`/`tool_bridge.hpp`) or wire `AgentMetadata::agent_instructions` (separately-scoped, already-named gap). |
| [041](ADR-041-appcontainer-ace-leak-accepted-residual.md) | Does Windows `native-jail` leak curated host files (`win.ini`, `hosts`) via inherited AppContainer ACEs in a way any per-app fix could close, and is it currently unmitigated? | **Judged.** Real (ADR-004 §5.3/§6.1), but not fixable the way the 2026-08-10 gap audit #11 assumed: its cited blocker, `CreateProcessAsUser`/`CreateProcessWithTokenW` privilege, is used nowhere in this codebase and was never proposed in ADR-004 — the real launch mechanism (`CreateProcessW` + `SECURITY_CAPABILITIES`) already runs under a standard non-admin token per ADR-004 §5. The real, ADR-004-evidenced obstacle is different: writing a DENY ACE onto `win.ini`/`hosts` (both `SYSTEM`/`TrustedInstaller`-owned) would hit the same `WRITE_DAC`-denied failure ADR-004 §5.4 already found for a different case. Root cause is Windows' own default DACL on a curated OS file set, independent of anything this backend grants. Ships no new code: ADR-004 §6.1/008 §1b already specify the accepted fix (interpreter-level `open()` mediation as the PRIMARY filesystem boundary, AppContainer ACL as backstop only) and it is already real, Judged, shipped code (`mediated_python_runner.cpp`'s `Internal_open`, `MediatedFileSystemAdapter`) — a request for `win.ini` never reaches the leaky ACL check because the mount-boundary check rejects it first. The residual only matters for native code bypassing Python-level mediation entirely, which AppContainer's ACL is explicitly a backstop for, not a primary boundary against. Evidence that the leak is real but bounded already exists and already passes: `tests/test_native_jail_abuse_corpus_windows.cpp` Case 4 pairs an arbitrary non-curated secret (`ESCAPE_DENIED`) against `win.ini` (`ESCAPE_OK`, documented) in the same run — this test predates this ADR (`M2 Phase C task C3`, commit `b39f5ea`); ADR-004 §11 item 1 asked for exactly this and was never marked done, a real instance of doc-drift corrected in the same pass. |
| [056](ADR-056-fs-quota-capability-gate-fix.md) | Did every `cap::FsRead`/`cap::FsWrite` check in `mediated_shell_dispatch.cpp`/`mediated_python_runner.cpp` correctly accept a quota-capped grant with headroom and deny a write once real usage already exceeds it? | **Judged.** No, on both counts (2026-08-10 gap audit #12, re-verified against current code). Every FsRead/FsWrite check built a synthetic `nullopt`-ceiling request and checked it via `contains()`, which `cap_covers()` rejects against any quota-capped grant as a widening attempt — a quota-capped grant was unconditionally unusable, the identical bug Milestone 3 Phase G4 already fixed once for the Python write path but never carried to the shell dispatcher (or to either file's FsRead side, which the original audit explicitly deferred but this ADR folds in). Separately, `MediatedFileSystemAdapter::write_file` tracked no usage at all, so the only grants actually usable before this fix (uncapped ones) enforced zero live quota — the audit's own "gate fix and live usage enforcement together" requirement. Fixed by adding `CapabilitySet::find_fs_read()` (the read-side twin of the already-Judged `find_fs_write()`) and a non-pure `FileSystemAdapter::usage()` seam, then applying both at every real call site. Self-red-team before implementation changed the design three ways: `usage()` unavailable fails closed rather than skipping the check; `rm` is never quota-gated (deletion only frees space); `mv` and `cp` share a call site but only `cp` is quota-checked (rename adds no usage) — proven directly by a regression pair, not merely asserted. New test block (E3-Q0-Q5, `test_mediated_shell_runner_smoke.cpp`) against a byte-exact-known mount; full suite 175/175 green. Named, not solved: `find_fs_write`/`find_fs_read`'s first-matching-grant-wins semantics (inherited from the already-Judged precedent, not new); `mount_root_usage()`'s full-rescan cost per quota-checked write (same precedent); the `mediated_python_runner.cpp` half is a mechanical pattern-match fix not locally build/test-verified this session (`AGENTENGINE_BUILD_PYTHON_RUNNER=OFF` in this environment). |
| [055](ADR-055-conflict-evidence-materialization.md) | When `merge_branch_into_parent()` reports a genuine conflict, does this codebase durably retain "both versions" the way 025 §4 requires, or does it vanish the moment the caller's local result goes out of scope? | **Judged.** It vanished (2026-08-10 gap audit #14) — `MergeConflict` was real and correctly computed, but nothing ever wrote it anywhere durable; `merge_branch_into_parent()` itself already, correctly, never touches the parent on failure (re-verified, a real invariant, not the audit's own hypothesized risk). Re-grounding found this bigger than "pick a storage location": `merge_branch_into_parent()` has zero production call sites anywhere (confirmed by grep) — `worktree_scoping.hpp`'s own top comment already says merge-on-join isn't wired for real yet — so this ADR designs what happens to conflict evidence ONCE a real call site exists, not the call site itself. Fixed: `conflicts_ref_name(parent_ref_name)` (pure, deterministic, modeled on `memory_ref_name`'s own pattern — two parents can never collide) and `materialize_merge_conflicts(...)`, a NEW function called by the merge's CALLER (never by `merge_branch_into_parent()` itself, preserving its existing contract unchanged) that writes `ours`'s and `theirs`'s ALREADY-STORED digests (no fresh blob copy needed — same content-addressed store) at `<path>.<ours_agent_id>`/`<path>.<branch.name>` into a genuinely separate `:conflicts` Ref, proven to leave the parent untouched. `ours_agent_id` is a REQUIRED explicit caller-supplied parameter (not inferable from the merge inputs alone — the audit's own "misattributes ours/theirs" finding, closed structurally). Self-red-team (inherited from the draft): writing evidence from inside `merge_branch_into_parent()` itself was considered and rejected (couples two separate concerns, breaks an already-relied-upon contract); a single shared `:conflicts` Ref per parent accumulates across every failed attempt (proven directly — an earlier attempt's evidence survives a later one), a deliberate choice with a named, unsolved retention/pruning residual. One correction to the draft found while implementing: the draft's own "SubWorktree::child_name" doesn't exist as a literal field — the real one is `SubWorktree::name`. New test file (`test_worktree_conflict_evidence.cpp`, M1-M6), full suite green. Does not build merge-on-join wiring, does not design the `/conflicts` mount's host-policy wiring, does not solve retention/pruning, does not change `merge_branch_into_parent()`'s own signature or behavior at all. **Amendment (2026-08-14):** real merge-on-join wiring -- `rt::WorkflowSupervisor` gains a `MergeOnJoinHook` (same no-ambient-authority callback shape as `checkpoint_hook_`), fired from the existing per-executor fold loop for every branch-mode executor whose reply this round was ok (025 §4: "merges back when its agent completes", NOT tied to `edge_kind::fan_in`'s own, different routing-join concept); `worktree_scoping.hpp` gains `make_merge_on_join_hook()` supplying the actual mechanics via `retry_merge_branch_into_parent()`/`materialize_merge_conflicts()`, returned as a plain `std::function` so `workflow/` still never depends on `rt/`. A new `workflow_status::merge_conflict` outcome reuses `executor_failed`/`routing_failed`'s own shape. New test file (J1-J3) is the first in this codebase to drive a branch-mode executor through the supervisor to completion, including a real end-to-end conflict with durable evidence. Full suite green. **Amendment 2 (2026-08-14):** `conflicts_mount_id()`/`conflicts_mount()` (`core/worktree.hpp`) build a real, guest-visible `Mount` for a parent's conflicts ref, mirroring `memory_mount()`'s own split -- pure binding, no capability, host still decides who gets access. Writing the FIRST test that ever read materialized evidence back through the ordinary `mount_read()` path caught a real, previously undetected bug: `materialize_merge_conflicts()` built one flat `Tree` whose entry names embedded `/` verbatim (both nested `MergeConflict::path`s and a workflow executor's own branch name, e.g. "session:s-20/agents/writer") -- silently unreachable through `mount_read()`'s segment-walk, for exactly the realistic workflow-driven case Amendment 1 just wired up. Fixed by rebuilding it with real nested-`Tree` construction (`detail::set_entry_at_path`, the same primitive `mount_write()` already uses) plus `/`-to-`_` agent-id sanitization. Test file extended (M7-M9), full suite green. |
| [054](ADR-054-tool-registry-name-keyed-resolution.md) | Given a bare string in a declarative document's `spec.tools`, does this codebase have any way to resolve it to a real `ToolDescriptor`? | **Judged.** No (2026-08-10 gap audit #4, and gap #5's name-keyed half) — 006 has zero occurrences of "registry"; 015 §1 asserts the reference exists with no mechanics behind it. Fixed with `ToolRegistry` (`core/tool_registry.hpp`), host-curated, never auto-discovered — mirrors `ChatClientRegistry`'s own shape. Capability-ceiling trust reuses the existing `check_capability_ceiling()`/`.contains()` shape at a different layer: native stays trusted by construction (the compiler is the trust boundary); `wasm_plugin`/`mcp_server`/`a2a_agent` self-reported ceilings are validated per-entry against a caller-supplied `outer_grant` (that provenance's own already-host-decided authority), excluding ONE bad tool, never a whole batch, never silently widened. `ToolTable::from_names(names, registry)` (gap #5's remaining half) is a thin consequence, delegating to the already-real `from_descriptors()`. `compile_agent_document()` gains an additive `ToolRegistry const* registry = nullptr` (Milestone 5 Phase B6's own `ChatClientRegistry const*` precedent) — `registry == nullptr` preserves the pre-existing "tools stays honestly empty" behavior byte-for-byte, re-proven, not just assumed; a supplied registry resolves plain-string `spec.tools` entries and correctly skips `{handoff: ...}` entries (014's vocabulary, out of scope). Self-red-team's own MUST-NAME 1 (a rejected tool must stay diagnosable, not indistinguishable from "never existed") closed via `exclusion_reason()`. Two new test files (`test_tool_registry.cpp` T1-T8, `test_agent_yaml_compiler.cpp` F-6), full suite green. Does not build any provider-specific ingestion path (MCP/WASM/A2A → `register_tool()` calls), does not resolve `spec.capabilities` YAML parsing, does not provide live-update/re-resolution for a stale registry snapshot, does not decide registry scope/lifetime. |
| [053](ADR-053-schedule-wakeup-standing-effect.md) | Does `rt::AgentSession` have a working `StandingEffect` producer for 019 §2's "Timer/schedule" wake row, the way it already does for background-task completion? | **Judged.** No (2026-08-10 gap audit #7) — bigger than the audit's own framing assumed: ADR-037 removed Quark's `ReminderService` entirely and `rt::` has never had ANY timer/delay primitive, not just an unwrapped one. The design draft's own §3(b) proposed a NEW CRTP policy tag, `Schedule<max_horizon_ms>` — found wrong once implementation touched real code: 007 §3 already normatively names `Schedule<max_horizon, max_active>` as a CAPABILITY, and `cap::Schedule`/`cap::decl::Schedule<...>`/`to_capability()`/`subsumes_payload()` already fully existed, simply never wired to a producer — only `CapabilitySet::find_schedule()` (mirroring `find_background()`) needed adding. Fixed: `AgentSession::schedule_wakeup(delay, label, now)` (PLAIN, UNLOCKED, same asymmetry as `start_background_task()`; `now` is REQUIRED caller-supplied, never ambient, I5) fails closed three ways (not granted / horizon exceeded / `max_active` at capacity, the `Background<max_concurrent>`/G9 precedent applied to `Schedule`) and emits `state_changed` on success (006 §6b's own normative event — NOT `tool_call_started`, a second correction to the draft's own hedged wording); `due_standing_effects(now)` is the host-polled introspection seam the draft named as "missing," read-only, mirrors `open_interactions()`. Self-red-team (inherited from the draft, unchanged): a self-firing `std::jthread` timer was considered and rejected — reintroduces ADR-037's own removed ambient-background-activity shape and a use-after-free class ADR-035 already caught once. New test file (`test_rt_agent_session_schedule_wakeup.cpp`, S1-S6), full suite green. Does not resurrect a self-firing timer, design the host poller or the resumption call's shape, solve `StandingEffect` durability generally, close `watch_resource`, or expose this as a model-callable declared tool — all named, not attempted. **Amendment (2026-08-14):** `schedule_wakeup` is now ALSO a real, model-callable tool (006 §6b/019 §2's own spec'd intent, previously unmet) -- injected into `contribution->tools` inside `run_rounds()` itself, capturing `this` directly (no existing mechanism was reusable: no reserved-tool-name interception anywhere, and ADR-028's own session-scoped-tool pattern only ever captures a `ContextProvider`, never `AgentSession`), offered only when a `cap::Schedule` grant is held. New test file (G1-G6), full suite green. |
| [057](ADR-057-agent-ask-suspend-without-deadlock.md) | Can `agent.ask()` — called from Python code running *inside* an already-dispatched `execute_code` — genuinely pause for a human answer and resume the SAME running script, without the naive port of ADR-029's suspend mechanism deadlocking `session_mutex_` against itself? | **Proposed** (design → red-team → prove complete for Design B; awaiting explicit user "Judged"). Traced the real call path (`invoke_tool()` runs at `rt/agent_session.hpp:999` while the coroutine still holds `session_mutex_`) and showed the literal ADR-029 port deadlocks by construction. Independent red-team (real source, no probe needed) **defeated two of three sketched designs on evidence already sitting in the tree**: Design A (drop the lock, block, re-acquire to signal) deadlocks anyway — `cli_chat.cpp`'s own production driver runs everything on a ONE-worker `ThreadPool` that its own header says cannot park and resume a coroutine cross-thread, so there is no second thread left to deliver the answer, on the very first outstanding ask. Design C (background thread, GIL released) requires cross-thread CPython access this embedding has never been hardened for and says so in its own comments (a real, previously-reproduced crash class); it also turned out NOT to reuse ADR-028's `Backgroundable` machinery as claimed — `ExecuteCodeTool` sets `captures_session_state = true`, which that machinery's own guard refuses to background by design. **Design B (abort-and-replay) survived red-team and was then implemented and proven for real**: a new `interaction_reason::codeact_ask`, `ExecOutcome::ask_pending`/`ask_prompt`, a `PendingCodeActAsk` suspend/resolve/replay path in `AgentSession`, and `agent.ask()` codegen wired through `_ae_internal.ask_or_raise` — the answer threaded host-side through `EffectContext`, deliberately never through the model-facing `ExecuteCodeArgs` schema (I3). Proven in `tests/test_agent_session_suspend_codeact_ask.cpp` (B1-B7, real embedded CPython): suspend-on-ask, a widened `start_run()` admission check rejecting a second `StartRun` while an ask is open (the gap Design A's own red-team finding named), correct replay of a deterministic prefix, chained multi-ask suspension under the same `interaction_id`, no regression to the no-ask path, a named fail-closed boundary for multi-call rounds, and a demonstrated (not just asserted) residual — replay repeats a script's own prior side effects, e.g. a file write before the ask, since none of that is idempotent by construction. Full regression: 964/964 build targets, 196/196 `ctest`, including ADR-029's own suite unaffected. Residuals named, not solved: side-effect repetition on replay (above); typed/schema'd answers deferred (plain string only); `Interaction::expires_at_ns` stays unwired; multi-call-round asks fail closed rather than being handled; genuine mid-script pause (Design C's actual goal) stays blocked on two separate, unbuilt runtime-substrate prerequisites (cross-thread coroutine resumption, GIL-safe multi-thread CPython embedding) — a Windows-fibers direction was named as a possible way to sidestep the GIL problem specifically, unexplored. |
| [058](ADR-058-agent-output-live-wiring.md) | Can `OutputSchema<T>`'s already-proven compile-time/request-assembly machinery (Milestone 5 Phase B5/B6) be connected to a live `AgentSession` turn, using this project's own existing type-driven-parse idiom as the validator rather than inventing a generic JSON-Schema 2020-12 engine, without silently misrepresenting 003 §4's own "one validator serves both" claim? | **Proposed** (design → red-team → prove complete for Design B; awaiting explicit user "Judged"). Corrected an earlier overstatement (this project's own Milestone 3 doc, and an earlier pass of mine, called `agent.output` a blank slate): `OutputSchema<T>` compiles to real JSON Schema text and `ChatRequest::output_schema_json` reaches real OpenAI/Anthropic request bodies — proven, not scaffolding — but none of it reaches a live `AgentSession` turn, `AgentResponse` has no field for a structured result, and no JSON Schema validator exists anywhere despite 003 §4 claiming one. Independent red-team (real source, file:line cited throughout) **defeated two of three sketched designs**: Design A (thread the whole `AgentMetadata` into `AgentSession`) is worse than drafted — the duplication hazard isn't just `tools`, it's also `max_turns` (`AgentMetadata` defaults 16, the actually-enforced `AgentSession::max_turns_` defaults unbounded) and `capability_ceiling` vs. `capabilities_`, three independently-diverged fields one drive-by edit from silently overriding the session's real, enforced state — and it doesn't even solve the type-erased-validator problem, since `output_schema_json` is already fully type-erased inside `AgentMetadata` regardless. Design C (a real, generic JSON Schema 2020-12 validator) is defeated for this pass on a definitive reading-derived finding: `json_schema_of<T>()`'s complete constraint vocabulary is `{"type": ...}` plus an optional description — zero `pattern`/length/bound/`enum` keywords ever emitted (grepped, zero matches) — so a real validator run against this codebase's own schemas would reject nothing `schema::from_json_value<T>`'s type-driven parse doesn't already reject; even the one theoretical gap (enum range) turns out doubly unenforced, since the parse side does an unchecked cast accepting any in-range integer regardless of the enum's real members. Per CLAUDE.md's "fix the spec first" rule, `003-Message-and-Content-Model.md` §4's "one validator serves both" sentence was corrected in this same pass to describe what's actually enforced. **Design B (narrow opt-in setter, matching `suspend_for_approval_`/`stream_model_calls_`'s established shape) survives**, with two named fixes: the draft's claimed precedent call site doesn't exist (only one production `register_agent<A>()` call anywhere, an offline reachability fixture that never touches a live session — this needs new wiring, not a migration), and the validator should return `result<void>` over bare `bool` for idiom-consistency. Concrete prove-phase plan (§8): request-side population scoped to `native` strategy only (the one with a real translation path); validation runs at the existing `calls.empty()` convergence branch, regardless of strategy, failing the run closed on mismatch rather than silently passing through unvalidated text; `AgentResponse` gains an additive `structured_output_json` field. Explicitly out of scope: real `tool_shaped`/`parse_and_repair` behavior; `agent.output` itself (026 §5's CodeAct module) — has nowhere to write to until this pass lands, a follow-on not part of it; streaming validation. **Implemented and proven**: real code lands exactly per the plan (one correction found while building — only ONE `ChatRequest{...}` construction site exists in the codebase, not three); full rebuild clean, `ctest` 154/154, new `test_agent_session_output_schema` 17/17 (O1-O5) — against the fully-integrated tree including ADR-057's `codeact_ask` machinery. Resolved the request-side scoping question directly rather than assuming: neither OpenAI's nor Anthropic's translation code gates `output_schema_json` on `ChatClientCapabilities.structured_output_native` (unlike `reasoning_effort` in the same function), confirming the native-only scoping is load-bearing, not redundant. One named, unconfirmed residual: the `AGENTENGINE_BUILD_PYTHON_RUNNER`-gated tree (ADR-057's own codeact_ask test) wasn't rebuilt against this change — reasoned low-risk (additive-only, gated behind an unused setter) but not directly verified. |
| [059](ADR-059-invoke-agent-tool-capability-attenuation.md) | Can `invoke_agent_tool()` — which minted a target agent's own full, unattenuated `capability_ceiling` via `grant_root()` unconditionally, ignoring what the calling `EffectContext` actually holds, a textbook I2 ambient-authority hole — be fixed to grant only the intersection of what the caller holds and what the target declares, by reusing `CapabilitySet::attenuate()` (ADR-009, already proven), without breaking the one existing test or being defeated by `attenuate()`'s own capped-vs-uncapped WIDENING rule? | **Proposed** (design → red-team → prove complete; awaiting explicit user "Judged"). Confirmed the bug matches its own draft exactly (`ctx.capabilities` was read nowhere in the old body) and re-confirmed directly that `invoke_agent_tool()` has zero production callers today (`native_jail/tool_bridge.hpp` and `core/run_event.hpp` both say so in their own comments, written before this fix) — so this is real, purely forward-looking hardening for the future `agent.spawn`/MCP-tool-exposure path this glue would back, not a live vulnerability closed today. The main attack — does the capped-vs-uncapped WIDENING rule reject the fix's own realistic case — was run to ground: every `cap::decl::*` declaration tag (`FsRead<Mount>`, `FsWrite<Mount>`, `NetOut<Host>`, `Exec<Profile>`) has no cap/quota NTTP parameter at all, and every `to_capability()` overload compiles a nullopt/empty cap on every scalar field, so `meta.capability_ceiling` is structurally uncapped for every agent declarable with today's DSL — meaning the WIDENING rule only rejects when a caller's own held capability is itself capped, exactly the already-judged, already-proven ADR-009 behavior (R-C3), not a new defect. **The fix survived red-team as originally proposed, unmodified** — implemented as `CapabilitySet::attenuate()` against `ctx.capabilities`, failing closed with a dedicated `agent_call.no_caller_capabilities` code when `ctx.capabilities == nullptr`, reusing `tool_pipeline_detail::make_error_result()` (the same helper ADR-029's `make_denial_result()` already uses). `tests/test_agent_tool_invocation.cpp`'s original two cases were fixed to attach a real, covering `CapabilitySet` (their default-nullptr `ctx.capabilities` would otherwise now be rejected before ever reaching what they were written to prove), and three new cases were added: a caller holding less than the ceiling rejected (`capability.attenuation_not_subsumed`), a caller holding more than needed still succeeds with the derived grant proven bounded to the target's own ceiling size (no leak-through), and the null-capabilities fail-closed path. Full evidence: clean Windows/MSVC build, targeted test `ALL PASS`, full `ctest` sweep **154/154 passed, 0 failed**. Named residual, found during red-team, not anticipated in the design: the declarative `cap::decl::*` surface has no syntax for a capped ceiling entry at all, so in practice a caller today can only satisfy the new gate on scalar axes by holding an equally-uncapped grant — correct per ADR-009's own rule, but a real limitation of the surrounding capability-declaration system worth its own future ADR if capped declarative ceilings are ever added. |
| [060](ADR-060-tool-call-progress-reporting.md) | Can `EffectContext` carry a real, call-scoped `report_progress()` channel that a `Tool::invoke()` calls synchronously, routing through the session's own `emit_run_event()` with the correct `call_id`, without leaking a stale binding between calls and without reopening the ADR-028 dangling-reference/data-race hazard class through a different door? | **Proposed** (design → red-team → prove complete; awaiting explicit user "Judged"). The design as originally drafted did NOT survive red-team as-is — a real, must-fix hazard was found and corrected before implementation. Confirmed clean: the core mechanism (bind `effect_context_.report_progress` immediately before, reset immediately after, at all three real `invoke_tool()` call sites in `rt/agent_session.hpp`) is sound on the synchronous, foreground path — `invoke_tool()` passes `EffectContext` by reference, never a copy, and no `co_await` sits inside any bracket, so two sequential calls in one round never leak a binding. The sharpest question (a tool stashing `ctx.report_progress` into a member and calling it later) is confirmed-clean: only a session-scoped stateful tool (ADR-028's `make_tool_descriptor_with_invoke`) could ever capture something reaching the session, and that construction is already structurally forbidden from ever being `Backgroundable` — so a stashed closure can only replay synchronously, on the session's own `session_mutex_`-serialized thread, producing at worst a stale-but-real `call_id`, never a memory-safety hazard. **The real finding**: `tool_pipeline.hpp::background_task()` — the one function that ever detaches a `std::thread` to run a `Backgroundable` tool's `invoke()` — takes `EffectContext` BY VALUE, and `AgentSession::start_background_task()` is documented "PLAIN, UNLOCKED," explicitly able to race a live `report_progress` bracket on a different thread of control. An ordinary, non-malicious call to `ctx.report_progress()` from a backgrounded tool could therefore reach back into the originating session's `emit_run_event()` from off-thread, racing `run_event_seq_by_run_` (an unlocked `std::unordered_map`) — a confirmed, concrete hazard, not the same class as the OTHER, already-accepted `EffectContext` races on passive data. **Fixed structurally**: `background_task()` resets its own local copy's `report_progress` to the no-op unconditionally before step 8 ever runs, matching ADR-028's own "closed here structurally, not by convention" precedent. Proven, not just argued: a new `tests/test_agent_session_tool_call_progress.cpp` (P1-P3, D) shows a real `tool_call_delta` event with the correct `call_id`/text (P1), a silent tool producing no event (P2, positive control), two sequential calls in one round each getting their own correctly-tagged `call_id` (P3), and — the regression check for the must-fix finding — a `Backgroundable` tool's `report_progress()` call from its own detached thread never reaching the caller's closure (D), verified non-vacuous by temporarily reverting the fix and confirming check D fails exactly as predicted, then restoring it and confirming all 13 checks pass. Full evidence: clean Windows/MSVC build (zero errors, no new warning class), full `ctest` sweep **155/155 passed, 0 failed**, run three times across two rebases (`main` advanced mid-pass; re-verified against the fully-integrated tree both times). Residuals named, not solved: `agent.progress`'s own CodeAct/`_ae_internal` wiring; structured/typed progress payloads; real `StandingEffect`/background-task progress delivery (now backed by a structural, not merely documented, absorption guarantee); end-to-end MCP-progress/AG-UI-SSE projection of a real (not hand-constructed) `tool_call_delta` event. |
| [040](ADR-040-wasm-tool-pipeline-bridge.md) | How does a loaded `WasmBackend` component's tools become reachable through the real 006 §3 tool pipeline, without opening a new authority-crossing hole between an agent's capability ceiling and a plugin's own capability grant? | **Proposed**, awaiting explicit user "Judged." `backends/wasm/wasm_tool_bridge.hpp`'s `wasm_tools_as_descriptors_from()` mirrors `mcp_tool_bridge.hpp`'s already-proven shape: each adapted `ToolDescriptor` is gated behind exactly one `cap::ToolCall{plugin_id + "::" + tool_name}` — an agent-level gate deliberately independent of `WasmBackend::invoke_tool()`'s own, already-proven `operator_grant`/`manifest.requested_capabilities` check (ADR-010 §3.2/§3.3); two authority layers at two different points, not a double-binding conflict. A pre-implementation design-review pass caught two real gaps before any code was written: a delimiter-collision spoofing path (`PluginManifest.id` is plugin-author-supplied, not host-assigned, so naive concatenation could let two different `(plugin_id, tool_name)` pairs collide onto the same capability name — closed by rejecting `"::"` in either input, fail closed) and a missing `captures_session_state = true` (the adapted closure holds a `shared_ptr<WasmBackend>` into live, mutable instance state — exactly ADR-028's hazard). A `Media`/blob wasm tool result fails the call closed (`wasm.tool_result_unsupported_content`) rather than silently dropping it — `tool_pipeline.hpp`'s own step 9 has no path today for a success reply to become anything but one `Data` item, so this is a fresh output-direction scope cut, not a reuse of ADR-010 §3.1's input-direction blob residual. `union_codeact_tools()` (`core/codeact_tool_union.hpp`) extended with wasm as a fourth source, same generic collision-detection logic. Proven end-to-end in `tests/test_wasm_tool_bridge.cpp` against the real compiled `ae:tool` fixture already used by `test_wasm_backend.cpp`: a wasm-hosted tool reachable through the real `agentengine::invoke_tool()`, the I2 gate denying independent of the wasm instance's own fully-granted `operator_grant`, and cross-source collision detection firing against a wasm-sourced name clash — plus pure-logic unit tests for the `is_error`/`Media`-content mapping paths the real fixture has no tool to exercise end-to-end. Residuals named, not closed: no production loader exists anywhere in this repo yet (this bridge takes an already-loaded handle, same assumption `mcp_tools_as_descriptors()` makes toward an already-connected `McpClient`); `PluginManifest.id` uniqueness is unenforced; `WasmBackend::destroy()` racing a live `ToolTable` built from this bridge is a named, not solved, caller-responsibility race; manifest approval/signature verification remains ADR-010 §5 F6's unresolved residual. |
| [061](ADR-061-host-provided-inbound-transport.md) | When a consumer-supplied host owns the HTTP transport and hands the engine an already-parsed request, does the engine derive the request's `Principal` by validating credentials itself, or accept one the host asserts? | **Split by tier (2026-08-20). Tier 1 — Judged** (§11, project owner sign-off): the client role needs no host at all, because `conformance client --command` spawns our binary (`tools/mcp_conformance_client.cpp`). §10b's prove phase ran for real against the official `@modelcontextprotocol/conformance@0.2.0-alpha.11` CLI: all 6 falsifiable claims CORRECT, two with real executed teeth (breaking `derive_param_headers` drops the passing count by 30, reverted; a planted canary proves the stdout/stderr leak-scan is non-vacuous). **Honest gate result, not smoothed over: non-auth conformance is 75/75 (100%), but 011 §10 G2 also names the `auth` suite, and this driver has zero OAuth machinery — auth is 3/49 (~6%), so G2 is NOT met.** A real defect was found and fixed as a byproduct: `perform_http_exchange`'s chunked-decode gap (ADR-011's own 2026-08-19 addendum). **Tier 3 (host-fronted server role) — Judged** (§45, project owner sign-off): after two red-team rounds returned 33 findings against the original identity-exchange design (it laundered a host-chosen identity into `verified_by_engine`) and iteration 3 re-scoped the work, the session-side admission mechanism and both bearer-credential-to-`RequestAuthority` bridges (`rt::`/`mcp::`) are implemented and proven for real (§20-§44, 207/208 tests green) — including `A2aServer::send_message()`'s and `McpServer::dispatch()`'s per-request `CapabilityGrant` wiring. Tier 3's first red-team round also found **four live security defects in shipped M7 code**, all fixed and proven (`tests/test_task_principal_binding.cpp`, 19 checks): unauthenticated cross-principal `GetTask`, an admission fail-open on every inbound A2A message, `mt19937_64` task ids, and audit records with no identity. R27's `approve_` finding (a principal-aware `ApprovalDecider`) was separately designed and proven (§46-47, 208/208 green) after the Tier 3 sign-off and awaits its own judge pass. **What Tier 3 does NOT close**: the actual test-fixture listener (§6, over `quark::pal::tcp_listen`) needed to run `conformance server`/`a2a-tck` against a real URL was never built in this ADR, so 011 §10 G1 and 012 §8 G1 remain unmet; other named residuals (007 §5's policy engine, `A2aServer::context_id_`, `RunEventProjector::thread_id_`, ADR-039 §3e's reference host-side example) are untouched. One measured result stands: a 16-byte authority handle puts `Ask<StartRun, AgentResponse>` at 104 bytes against `kMaxPayload`'s 192. |
| [063](ADR-063-retrieval-augmented-context-provider-shape.md) | What should a retrieval-augmented-generation `ContextProvider` and its storage seam look like — `Embedder`/`VectorIndex`/corpus mounting/citation rendering — without repeating Memory's own historical cross-tenant and citation-forgery bugs? | **Judged** (2026-08-19, project owner sign-off; design → red-team → two implementation passes → second red-team pass → fixes, all same day). Real, compiled, tested code for every piece §2 designs: `Embedder`/`BruteForceCosineIndex`/`corpus_scope`/`CorpusChunkRecord` foundation, `DiskCorpusSource` folder-mount ingestion with re-mount dedup, `OpenAIEmbedder` (OpenAI/OpenRouter, live-tested against real `openrouter.ai`), `VectorRagContextProvider` tying it together with multi-tenant isolation and citation-forgery-resistant rendering. §3 claims 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and finding 7 all CORRECT with real executed evidence; claim 1 (brute-force latency) measured for real (Release/clang: ~248µs/~1.45ms/~7.6ms at n=200/1000/5000, dim 1536) now has a real RFC 023 §3 budget row too (added 2026-08-19, project owner's own choice: ≤500µs, Goal, matching "Context assembly"'s existing row) — n=200 PASSES, n=1000/5000 are genuine, accepted Goal-tier MISSES (023 §6: warns, doesn't hard-fail); claim 7 (Vulkan) deliberately deferred. A second, independent red-team pass against the real implementation found and fixed 5 real issues: a `rag_corpus_key()` collision (two differently-scoped mounts could alias under an unescaped `:`-joined key — fixed via length-prefixed encoding), a `last_user_text()` path that could let model output reach the embedder as a live network payload (I3 — fixed by walking to the last real `role::user` message, also fixed in the inherited `memory_provider.hpp` copy), an unbounded chunk size for no-whitespace content, missing `VectorIndex` dimensionality validation, and a `CorpusChunkRecord` JSON field that silently defaulted instead of rejecting. Full suite 194/194 (`ctest -LE live-network`) throughout. `recall(query)`'s `invoke` gap (synchronous-tool-vs-async-embedder) is now CLOSED — see ADR-064's own row: `rt::drive_leaf_task()` + `Embedder::synchronous_leaf` implemented and tested, `recall` now genuinely invocable end-to-end for any `synchronous_leaf = true` conformer. |
| [064](ADR-064-recall-tool-sync-invoke-vs-async-embedder.md) | Can a synchronous `ToolDescriptor::invoke` safely drive `Embedder::embed_batch()`'s `task<T>` to completion, closing ADR-063's `recall(query)` gap, without changing `ToolDescriptor::invoke`'s signature (and therefore 25+ call sites across the tool pipeline, MCP/A2A servers, and the native-jail CodeAct bridge)? | **Judged (2026-08-19, project owner sign-off) — Design B implemented and tested.** `rt::drive_leaf_task<T>()` (`rt/drive_leaf_task.hpp`) drives a leaf `task<T>` in exactly one `resume()`, per the design's own corrected (post-red-team) contract; `Embedder`'s new REQUIRED `synchronous_leaf` trait (`core/embedder.hpp`) gates its use — all 4 real conformers migrated (`OpenAIEmbedder` declares `true`, justified by its body never awaiting anything; the 3 test mocks declare `true`, each genuinely leaf by inspection). `VectorRagContextProvider::recall`'s `invoke` (`vector_rag_context_provider.hpp`) now genuinely drives `embedder_.embed_batch()` + `index_->search()` + the SAME `render_scored_chunk()` helper `on_context()` already used, end-to-end, for any `synchronous_leaf = true` conformer; a conformer declaring `false` still gets the original fail-closed `failure_class::contract` error, unchanged (no regression). Proven by a new, dedicated `tests/test_rt_drive_leaf_task.cpp` (D1-D5: a conforming leaf task drives in one resume(); the double-wrapped `result<T>` is preserved, not flattened, both for the ordinary inner error channel and a genuine outer task-level fault; nested `task<T>` composition still resolves in one resume(); a task genuinely parked on a contended `AsyncMutex::lock()` is reported as a violated `synchronous_leaf` contract — reproducing ADR-037's own addendum finding directly against `drive_leaf_task()` itself, not only through `ThreadPool` — and the abandoned contender does not corrupt the mutex for later legitimate use) plus real end-to-end coverage added to `tests/test_vector_rag_context_provider.cpp` (R8/R8b/R8c: `recall()` invoked with well-formed args against a `synchronous_leaf` `MockEmbedder` returns the real ranked, cited chunk text, not the old stub error; R9: malformed args still rejected before any embedder/index work). Full suite 195/195 (`ctest -LE live-network`), zero regressions. Rejects Design A (async the whole tool pipeline, too large a blast radius, 25+ call sites) and defers Design D (a real async executor) as future work; Design C (`Backgroundable`) remains a documented, not-yet-implemented complementary follow-on for slow-backend deployments. Separately, the `rt::ThreadPool`+`rt::AsyncMutex`+`rt::AgentSession` reentrancy/UB hazard this ADR's own red-team found in already-shipped code is FIXED (`rt/thread_pool.hpp`, `tests/test_rt_thread_pool.cpp` T6, documented as an addendum to ADR-037's own index row above), independent of Design B. **A second red-team pass against this real implementation (2026-08-19, no blocking defect found)** fixed one real gap (`drive_leaf_task()` now wraps `resume()` in the same try/catch as `take_value()`, matching `ThreadPool::run_job()`'s own defense-in-depth exactly), corrected an incomplete `this`-capture-safety comment (now backed by a new test, R12, driving `recall` through the real `make_shared<ProviderT>`-backed wiring), narrowed an overclaimed thread-safety line (`IndexT` sharing across a future concurrent-writer ingestion path named as a real-but-currently-unreachable residual), and added R13 (`recall` against a genuinely empty index). Both of those test gaps were closed the same day: R14 proves a stale, unbacked index entry reached through `recall` is silently skipped, not a whole-call failure; R15 drives 8 real threads calling `recall`'s invoke concurrently against the SAME provider instance, all correct, stable across 5 repeated runs (scope-limited to no concurrent writer, since no writer/ingestion path exists in the tree yet). **The two remaining named residuals were then closed for real (2026-08-19)**: `BruteForceCosineIndex` (`core/vector_index.hpp`) gained a real `std::shared_mutex` (unique lock on `add_batch()`, shared lock on `search()`/`contains()`/`size()`) — no longer "currently unreachable," genuinely thread-safe under concurrent read+write, proven by a new `test_vector_index.cpp` block with a real writer thread interleaved with 4 reader threads (200 writes, zero corrupted reads, stable across 5 runs); and a new `NonLeafEmbedder` test conformer (`synchronous_leaf = false`) drives `recall`'s invoke through the `if constexpr` FALSE branch for the first time via a real instantiation (R16), confirming the fail-closed fallback works exactly as designed, not merely compiles. |
| [065](ADR-065-tool-optimizer-provider.md) | Can a large MCP/plugin/agent tool surface stay under the ~200k-token schema budget that degrades tool-selection accuracy, via an on-demand `ToolOptimizerProvider` gate rather than hand-pruning? | **Judged** (2026-08-20). As-built: decision, real implementation, and tests landed together (matching ADR-024's role for `mount_skill`) — not a pre-implementation contested-design cycle, since issue #15's own design draft (§6) already established this changes nothing about `assemble_context`/`ContextProviderDescriptor`/`ContextProvider` themselves. Reuses `core/codeact_tool_union.hpp`'s cross-source collision machinery rather than reimplementing it; inherits ADR-024 §8's mount-visibility cadence invariant and its unmount gap. Not a reopening of OQ-18 (already resolved) — see the ADR's own §1. |
| [066](ADR-066-context-provider-attribution-provenance.md) | Should `ContextContribution` provenance be stamped by each contributor's own discipline (MAF's shape), or enforced structurally at `assemble_context()`'s seam? | **Judged** (2026-08-21, project owner sign-off). Implemented (`ContributorProvenance` in `content.hpp`, `Message`/`ToolDescriptor::attribution`, `HasContextProviderName`/`ContextProviderDescriptor::name` in `context_assembly.hpp`, stamping logic in `assemble_context()`), proven (`tests/test_context_provenance.cpp`, 16/16, Windows/MSVC; full-tree rebuild 0 compile errors; `ctest` 181/191, the 10 not-run are pre-existing/unrelated CPython-embedding targets). Decision: stamp centrally at `assemble_context()`, requiring a declared `name` on every `ContextProviderDescriptor` (mirrors ADR-033's `HasMiddlewareName`) — cannot be skipped by a non-cooperating contributor the way MAF's own per-provider self-stamping can. **A real finding surfaced only DURING implementation**: the draft's own `content_origin` fix ("any contributor-sourced message maps to a provider-derived origin, regardless of what the contributor set") is WRONG as literally stated — it would have regressed `SkillsProvider`'s already-shipped `content_origin::system` advertisement. Fixed by narrowing the check to `content_origin::user` specifically (the one origin only genuine, verbatim-replayed history may claim), leaving every other origin exactly as the contributor set it. Also surfaced, but NOT closed by this ADR (named residual): `HistoryProvider<Summarize<...>>`'s synthesized summary message inherits an unmarked `content_origin::assistant` with nothing distinguishing it from a real assistant turn. Creates and closes `OpenQuestions.md` OQ-22; does not reopen OQ-18. Foundational to ADR-067. **Wired into `rt::AgentSession` (2026-08-20)**: `tests/test_rt_agent_session_context_provenance.cpp` (13/13) proves a real, composed multi-provider `HistoryProviderT` slot carries attribution all the way to the real outbound `ChatRequest`, zero `agent_session.hpp` changes needed. |
| [067](ADR-067-middleware-turn-point-pre-model-enforcement.md) | Does closing 017 §4's `pre_model` filter gap need a new "terminal `ContextProvider`" concept, or does it reuse 002 §5's already-declared, unwired `Middleware` `turn` point? | **Proposed** (design → red-team → prove complete; awaiting explicit user "Judged"). Implemented (`core/turn_middleware.hpp`: `TurnContext`/`ToolSurfaceView`/`redact_subspan()`/`Compactor<N>`/`run_turn_middleware_chain()`), proven (`tests/test_turn_middleware.cpp`, 22/22, Windows/MSVC; full-tree rebuild 0 errors; `ctest` 182/192, same 10 pre-existing not-run targets as ADR-066). Decision: wire the existing `turn` point (002 §5), reusing 017 §4's verdict vocabulary — closes `pre_model` only, explicitly NOT `post_model`. FATAL finding fixed structurally: `ToolSurfaceView` never exposes a mutable `ToolDescriptor`, only narrow non-`invoke`-touching mutations on stable handles. **Two real findings surfaced only DURING implementation**: (1) the draft's claimed ADR-033 before/after onion reuse doesn't fit — the turn point has no real inner action to sandwich (unlike the model call), so what's actually built is a single forward pass, first-deny short-circuits; (2) `ToolSurfaceView` as drafted (each middleware constructs its own) silently disconnects `redact()`/`reorder()` from `finalize()` across separate instances — found by the test file's own first, failing version — fixed by giving `TurnContext` one shared `tool_surface` instance. Taint boundary enforced via subtractive-only `redact_subspan()`, property-tested against 8 offset/length cases including adversarial ones. `Compactor<N>`'s "never touches `history[]`" claim proven both structurally (the type carries no reference to it) and behaviorally. `ToolSurfaceView`'s guarantee is scoped to middleware using ONLY its sanctioned API, not one bypassing it into the raw `TurnContext::assembled` reference (named residual, not closed). Depends on ADR-066 (Judged); explicitly does not reopen OQ-18. **Wired into `rt::AgentSession` (2026-08-20)**: `AgentSession::set_turn_middleware_hook()` runs the chain once per round, confirmed as the ONE genuine `pre_model` seam in the file (the other two `on_context()` call sites never reach the model). `tests/test_rt_agent_session_turn_middleware.cpp` (8/8): a redacting middleware's decision reaches the real outbound request; a denying middleware fails the round with the model NEVER called. |
| [068](ADR-068-runtime-secret-quarantine-host-delegated-detection.md) | When a secret leaks into ordinary content at runtime, does AgentEngine own detection and audit durability itself, or delegate both to the host through a declared seam? | **Proposed** (design → red-team → prove complete; awaiting explicit user "Judged"). Implemented (`trust/secret_quarantine.hpp`), proven (`tests/test_secret_quarantine.cpp`, 24/24, Windows/MSVC, `ctest` 2/2 clean). Decision: neither detection (`SecretDetector`) nor audit durability (`QuarantineAuditHook`) is engine-owned — both are host-injected callbacks, `nullptr`-by-default, same idiom as `ApprovalDecider`/`MiddlewareTraceHook`. One FATAL finding fixed pre-implementation: the original design leaned on `invoke_tool()`'s in-memory-only audit record for accountability — fixed via host-delegated durability. **A second, real finding surfaced only DURING implementation, not anticipated by the ADR text**: the design's own "quarantine() grants a capability in the same step" mechanism is not implementable against the real `CapabilitySet` (empty by construction, no incremental-grant method, `grant_root()` explicitly "never reachable from anything derived from model output") — and would have been I3-unsafe regardless, since the agent-initiated tool's argument is model-supplied. Fixed by splitting grant-eligibility bookkeeping (host-only, never touched by model-reachable code) from any actual grant (entirely the host's own `CapabilitySet::grant_root()` call, unchanged). Mint-at-runtime `SecretRef` naming is content-addressed (SHA-256 via the existing audited `trust::hmac_sha256`), giving cross-contributor dedup for free without needing ADR-067's chain mechanism. Independent of ADR-066/067. **Wired into `rt::AgentSession` (2026-08-20)**: `QuarantineToolProvider` (new, `trust/secret_quarantine.hpp`) is a real `ContextProvider` conformer contributing the `quarantine_secret` tool — occupies `AgentSession`'s `HistoryProviderT` slot directly, zero `agent_session.hpp` changes needed. `tests/test_rt_agent_session_quarantine_tool.cpp` (5/5) drives a real round where the model calls the tool through the actual `invoke_tool()` pipeline; a real bug in the test's own first version (only scanning top-level `ContentItem`s, missing the `ToolResult`'s nested content) silently found neither the leak nor the marker until fixed. |
| [069](ADR-069-content-triggered-model-response-replay.md) | When a model response has already committed content that violates policy, does AgentEngine discard-and-retry inside the existing `after_model` point, or leave remediation entirely to the host? | **Judged** (2026-08-21, project owner sign-off). Implemented (`core/content_replay_gateway.hpp`: `ContentReplayGateway<Inner>`/`ContentReplayTrigger`/`ContentReplayTraceHook`/`corrective_message()`), proven (`tests/test_content_replay_gateway.cpp`, 30/30, Windows/MSVC; full-tree rebuild 0 errors; `ctest` 183/193, same 10 pre-existing not-run targets as ADR-066/067). Decision, corrected during implementation: rather than extending `MiddlewareModelCallGateway`/`ModelCallContext` (ADR-033/036, shipped and Judged) with new fields, built a SEPARATE wrapper composing over any `ModelCallGatewayLike` — the same compositional shape `MiddlewareModelCallGateway` already uses over `ModelCallGateway` — leaving ADR-033/036's code untouched. **A real finding surfaced only DURING implementation**: the amended retry request must never re-include the discarded response's own content — doing so would re-send whatever got a response discarded (e.g. a secret) back to the vendor a second time, inside the very call meant to correct it; proven directly against the real captured request in tests, not just documented. Bounded two independent ways (per-trigger-site AND session-lifetime, the latter persisting across separate `call()` invocations on one gateway instance), both proven. Streaming excluded structurally (no `chat_stream()` method exists on the type at all, not a documented-only rule). `TokenBudget` accounting verdict narrowed to INCONCLUSIVE: every attempt's real usage is now observable via `ContentReplayTraceHook`, but `AgentSession`'s own existing budget enforcement is not wired to consume it (named residual). Independent of ADR-066/068 (confirmed: no shared includes). **Wired into `rt::AgentSession` (2026-08-20)**: `ContentReplayGateway<Inner>` plugs directly into the existing `ChatClientT` slot (`ModelCallGatewayLike`), zero core changes. `tests/test_rt_agent_session_content_replay.cpp` (8/8): the gateway is transparent to the turn loop, durable history holds only the final kept response. Relationship to ADR-067, now answered with real positive evidence: `tests/test_rt_agent_session_turn_and_replay_composition.cpp` (4/4) runs both mechanisms in ONE session and proves they compose without interference (turn-middleware shaping applies uniformly across every replay attempt) — whether they SHOULD be unified remains open. |
| [070](ADR-070-host-configurable-responsibility-boundary.md) | Should AgentEngine keep enforcing one strict safety posture everywhere, or name a formal seam for shifting part of that responsibility to the consumer dev, and where does that seam stop? | **Judged** (design → red-team → prove complete; approved by project owner, 2026-08-21). Names the "Delegated Decision Seam" pattern (explicit opt-in, fails closed/safe when unset, narrows-or-decides-among-already-possessed-authority only, host code never model output, always audited) already implicit in `ApprovalDecider`/`SandboxBackend`/ADR-068, and states what must NOT become one (the `text_derived` closed declassifier allowlist, `Tainted<T>::unsafe_view()`, ADR-033's middleware closure, `SpawnBudget`/`SpawnCostBudgetActor` attenuation-only, I2's empty-by-default guarantee, `check_workflow_executable()`'s agent/sub_workflow rejection — OQ-19 stays "document only, do not implement yet"). Implements four bounded instances closing real, previously-named gaps: (a) `PolicyDecider` — a host-injectable graduated resolution for `Agent<...>`'s `policy_driven` approval mode, proven to never bypass capability binding or the `text_derived` gate (`tests/test_tool_pipeline.cpp`, 6 new checks; `tests/test_rt_agent_session_suspend_approval.cpp` SU7/SU8); (b) `AgentSession::expired_interaction_ids()`/`set_interaction_expiry()` — closes ADR-029 §6's named "nothing checks `expires_at_ns`" gap as a host-driven query pair, not an engine-internal timer (no wall clock is wired in this project) — reuses the existing `resolve_interaction()` path, no new resolution mechanism or race (SU9); (c) `WorkflowSupervisor::token_budget_unenforced()` — an unconditional, deterministic diagnostic closing the silent gap that `TerminationBound.token_budget` is host-settable but never engine-checked (`tests/test_rt_workflow_supervisor.cpp`, 3 new checks); (d) formalizes ADR-066 §7's already-named single-provider-path attribution gap as a deliberate, documented two-tier contract (documentation-only). Full Windows/MSVC rebuild: zero compile errors; full `ctest`: see the ADR's own §5 for the exact count. |
| [071](ADR-071-native-unsandboxed-process-execution-providers.md) | Should AgentEngine ship native, unsandboxed host-process-execution providers, and how do they stay compatible with I2/I3 and worktree confinement without a sandbox jail? | **Judged** (design → red-team → prove complete; approved by project owner, 2026-08-22). Implements `NativeShellProvider`/`NativeBashProvider`/`NativePythonProvider`/`NativeNodeProvider` (`src/backends/native_process/`, a new Tier-2 backend gated behind `AGENTENGINE_WITH_NATIVE_PROCESS`, OFF by default, never linked into `agentengine_shell_runner`/`agentengine_python_runner`), the next bounded instance of ADR-070's Delegated Decision Seam. Decision: capability-first discovery (Design B) — a host-granted `cap::NativeExec` pattern is the ONLY thing that determines what `scan_path()` may return or a tool call may invoke; PATH-scanning is real but can only narrow "what's on PATH" down to "what's on PATH AND already authorized," never the reverse (proven against a real, ungranted-but-present executable, `test_native_path_scan.cpp` R-P3). Worktree confinement reuses ADR-014's handle-anchored primitive for argv pre-validation; `real_run()` re-verifies the grant fresh per invocation against the live `EffectContext`, never a cached prior-turn scan. Narrowly amends CLAUDE.md's/`008-Sandbox-and-Isolation.md`'s "embedded CPython, never a second runtime" bullet (ADR-037 precedent for editing a locked decision): scoped to the mediated code interpreter specifically, since `NativePythonProvider` is reached only through its own distinctly-named tool, never `execute_code`. Two real findings caught and fixed before evidence was finalized: an argv-shape false positive live-wiring `cmd.exe` (`"/c"` misread as a path-escape attempt) and an unclosed family-distinctness gap (`assemble_context()` has no tool-name collision check) closed via a compile-time `requires`-clause proof (`compile_fail/native_capability_announcer_rejects_duplicate_family.cpp`). 99 new checks across 6 test executables, 227/227 full suite (`ctest -j1`), zero regressions; default build confirmed zero-footprint with the flag unset. **Honestly disclosed, not-eliminated residual:** a spawned child's own subsequent raw syscall on a string-passed path argument is a genuine TOCTOU window this mechanism cannot close (unlike `open_within_mount_root`'s own callers, which use the verified HANDLE directly) — matches this project's disclosure norm for ADR-041's accepted residual rather than overclaiming a hard boundary. POSIX (`posix_spawn`) implementation is design-only, no Linux env available to verify against. |
| [072](ADR-072-external-host-discovered-skill-sources.md) | Should AgentEngine auto-scan the host machine for skills belonging to OTHER installed AI coding tools (Claude Code, GitHub Copilot, OpenAI Codex), and if so, how? | **Proposed** (design → red-team → prove phases complete; awaiting project-owner "Judged" sign-off, matching ADR-070/071's own flow). Reuses `009-Plugin-and-Extension-System.md` §8's `SkillSource`/`SkillsProvider` machinery unchanged. Checked against ADR-070's Delegated Decision Seam pattern (§4) and named against ADR-066's provenance mechanism, which this ADR's own residual falls short of (§7). `kKnownRoots` backed by dated, sourced research (`docs/research/2026-08-21-external-ai-tool-skill-conventions.md`). |
| [073](ADR-073-unified-streaming-gateway-call-stream-tool-argument-result-streaming-ask-stream.md) | Can four independent streaming gaps (gateway-typed sessions can't stream, tool-call arguments/results are invisible mid-flight, the quickstart `Bundle` facade has no streaming entry point) plus `EffectContext::report_progress`'s text-only limitation be closed as one coordinated design, without reopening I2/I3/004 §4's no-silent-substitution rule? | **Judged** (design → red-team → prove complete; approved by project owner, 2026-08-22). Five pieces: `ModelDelta` becomes a real discriminated union (tool-call-argument fragments streamed from both OpenAI and Anthropic backends); `ToolCallFinished` widened to carry the real `ToolResult`; `EffectContext::report_progress` widened from `std::string_view` to `ContentItem`, taint-forced recursively; `ModelCallGateway::call_stream()` — a new, `chat_stream()`-shaped, commit-gated streaming entry point (`ModelCallGatewayStreamLike`, a separate optional concept, not a widening of `ModelCallGatewayLike`); `Bundle::ask_stream()`, reusing `ask()`'s own proven bounded-single-`resume()` contract via a driver+relay thread pair. **Five independent adversarial red-team rounds** (three against the first three pieces, two against the gateway/Bundle pieces) found and fixed 20+ real findings before any `prove` work started, including one architecturally significant miss surviving two full rounds (Piece B silently scoped to OpenAI only) and a real deadlock risk + a reintroduced double-resume hazard in the gateway/`Bundle` pieces' first sketch. `prove` then caught 4 MORE real bugs no red-team round found: a stale lambda signature, a double-move hazard, `Bundle::ask_stream()` never enabling `stream_model_calls_` (would have silently returned an empty stream), and a stale test assertion in the Anthropic translation suite. New, non-vacuous tests added (`test_model_call_gateway.cpp` G9-G11 proves the commit gate actually gates: a post-commit failure never falls over to a configured fallback). Full rebuild zero errors (818 targets, then a second `AGENTENGINE_WITH_HTTPS` tree closing every remaining compiler-verification gap); `ctest` 196/196. **First live-network test of `Bundle::ask()`/`Bundle::ask_stream()` ever written**, run against real OpenRouter: both succeed end to end, `ask_stream()` delivers 4 real live chunks, a wrong credential is correctly rejected through the full stack.
