See `docs/planning/unified-streaming-design-draft.md` for the complete, uncompressed record of every finding and fix. **NOTE (merge, this session): `Bundle::ask_stream()` was re-ported onto ADR-072-era's `Bundle` redesign (variadic `.store()`, `ComposedQuickstartSessionBuilder`, `.max_turns()`/`.token_budget()`) during reconciliation of two divergent sessions — see `session_builder.hpp`'s own top comment, finding 13, for the merge account.** |
| [074](ADR-074-composed-context-provider-consolidation.md) | Should the three overlapping `ContextProvider` composites (`HistoryAndSkillsProvider`, `ComposedContextProvider`, `LazyComposedContextProvider`) be consolidated into one type? | **Judged** (2026-08-23, project-owner sign-off, same-session decision — not a full multi-agent design→red-team→prove→judge cycle; the two mechanisms merged here were already separately red-teamed and proven on `LazyComposedContextProvider`). Closes `docs/planning/2026-08-22-component-role-audit-tracker.md` Findings A (redundant `HistoryAndSkillsProvider`, deleted) and B (`ComposedContextProvider`'s fork-aliasing bug, I1/I4-adjacent — closed at the source via move-only semantics). Verified non-breaking before implementation: zero existing `.fork_from()` caller touches any of these types. **A real design conflict found DURING implementation, not anticipated in the plan**: an early attempt auto-engaged the default constructor whenever every `Ms` happened to be default-constructible (matching the old eager type's ergonomics) — this silently broke `ComposedQuickstartSessionBuilder::build()`'s own `.engage()` call for any real caller choosing all-default-constructible providers (`already_engaged` failure), caught by `tests/test_session_builder.cpp`'s own pre-existing B22 exception-safety regression test cascading into 5 failures. Fixed by making default construction ALWAYS start empty/unengaged, unconditionally — four call sites that relied on the old auto-engage behavior needed one explicit `.engage()` call added each. Proven: `tests/test_composed_context_provider.cpp` (new Part 3: `engage()`, move-only, no-aliasing-after-move), the full pre-existing `test_session_builder.cpp` suite (B14–B22, all passing against the same type now), plus every other real/test call site rebuilt and re-run clean. See the ADR's own §4 for the full account. |
| [075](ADR-075-context-budget-fail-closed.md) | When a `ContextProvider` contributor exceeds its own declared `ContextBudget`, should `assemble_context()` silently trim it, or fail closed? | **Judged** (2026-08-23, project-owner sign-off, same-session decision). Closes `docs/planning/2026-08-22-component-role-audit-tracker.md` Finding E (per-contributor budget trimming was invisible — `ContextAssemblyResult.drops` was computed but discarded by every real composite, an I4 gap). `assemble_context()`'s return type widened to `task<result<ContextAssemblyResult>>`; a contributor exceeding its own `max_tokens` now fails the whole call closed (`failure_class::resource`, `"context_assembly.contributor_budget_exceeded"`, naming which contributor by index/name) instead of silently trimming its oldest messages and returning success — matches the already-shipped `token_budget_` precedent (`rt/agent_session.hpp`) at a different layer, rather than inventing a second shape. Deliberately no dual "trim vs. error" config knob: grepped the whole tree first and found zero production callers ever set a nonzero `ContextBudget` today, so there was no shipped graceful-degradation behavior to preserve alongside the cleaner one. `ContextAssemblyResult.drops`/`ContextDrop` are kept, not deleted — real vocabulary `TurnMiddleware::TurnContext` uses independent of this mechanism — but `assemble_context()` itself has no producer of a real one left (confirmed, via `ADR-049`'s own independent finding, that nothing else ever populated one either). `ComposedContextProvider::on_context()` propagates the new failure verbatim. Proven: `tests/test_context_assembly.cpp` (rewritten to prove the failure's class/code/attributed-contributor, plus an exactly-at-budget boundary case), `tests/test_composed_context_provider.cpp` (new Part 1b), signature-only migrations elsewhere (no other caller exercises a nonzero budget). See the ADR's own §3 for why no config knob was added. |
| [076](ADR-076-undeclared-tool-call-leak-refusal.md) | Should the engine notice and refuse, rather than silently pass through, a response that leaks a tool call as raw text when `tool_calling` is declared true but the recovery scan (`scan_response_format_leaks`) isn't armed for that endpoint? | **Judged** (design → red-team → prove complete; approved by project owner, 2026-08-23). Resolves `OpenQuestions.md` OQ-23. New `detect_undeclared_tool_call_leak()` (`core/response_format_leak_scan.hpp`) — detect-only, refuse-not-promote, never touches ADR-023's declassifier — wired into `AgentSession::run_model_call()` at both real insertion points as a structural `else if` sibling to the existing scan branch. An independent, fresh-context red-team pass found one required revision before any code was written (the detector must explicitly skip already-tainted diagnostic content, not merely rely on today's insertion points staying mutually exclusive) and three residuals (a cross-session refusal-amplification surface and a tool-name-enumeration oracle, both host-scope by this project's own locked networking architecture; a domain-specific false-positive risk) — no finding was fatal to the central refuse-not-promote safety claim, which was traced against the real retry/turn-counting code (`failure_class::contract` is retried nowhere in the engine, so a refusal is bounded to exactly one wasted run). 22 new tests, all passing (9 function-level, 13 real `AgentSession::run_model_call()` round trips); full `ctest` 227/229, the only 2 failures confirmed pre-existing via a `git stash` baseline comparison, unrelated to this change. Amends `004-Model-Provider-Plane.md` §2 with the inbound half of "capabilities are declared, not probed." Full record: `docs/planning/oq23-undeclared-tool-call-leak-design-draft.md`. **Renumbered from 074 to 076 (2026-08-23)**: 074/075 were independently claimed by a concurrent session's work (`ADR-074-composed-context-provider-consolidation.md`, `ADR-075-context-budget-fail-closed.md`) that reached `origin/main` first; caught at push time, before this ADR's number appeared anywhere public. |
| [077](ADR-077-agent-executor-capabilityset-bridge.md) | Where does an `executor_kind::agent` workflow node's `CapabilitySet` come from, and how does its runtime bridge to a real `AgentSession` actually work? | **Judged** (2026-08-23, project owner explicitly lifted this file's own prior 2026-08-13 "document only, do not implement yet" instruction this same session, then design → red-team → prove completed against the already-twice-red-teamed design draft). Resolves `OpenQuestions.md` OQ-19. `Executor::capability_ceiling` (declared, static) + `WorkflowSupervisor::initialize()`'s previously-unused `contexts[i]` (the actual granted `CapabilitySet`) + a new `check_workflow_executable(graph, contexts)` overload verifying the grant satisfies the ceiling — never the principal-derived option ADR-070 §7 already steered away from. New `rt/agent_workflow_executor.hpp::agent_session_as_executor_body()`, structurally marked via `AgentExecutorBodyTag`/`std::function::target<T>()` (a `TaggedExecutorBody` wrapper was FATAL as first scoped — breaks every real call site). The concurrent-same-node race the first draft missed (two non-`fan_in` edges converging on one agent-kind node in a round would double-call the same `AgentSession::start_run()` from different threads) is closed by a gather-time quarantine in `WorkflowSupervisor::execute()` routed through the existing failure-policy machinery, not a whole-round abort. Checkpoint/resume amnesia (the first draft's other FATAL finding) is an accepted, TESTED limitation — `restore_from_record()` never reconnects `bodies_`. YAML authoring of the new field is refused loudly, not silently dropped (I6). 26 new tests across two suites (`test_workflow_agent_executor_gate.cpp`, `test_rt_agent_workflow_executor.cpp`), all passing, standalone-built and run (this session's build cache has `AGENTENGINE_WITH_HTTPS=OFF`, which also gates several pre-existing tests out of CTest registration in that tree — not specific to this change; full `ctest` was not re-run). Full record: `docs/planning/agent-as-workflow-executor-design-draft.md`, `OpenQuestions.md`'s own resolved OQ-19 entry. |
| [078](ADR-078-tool-call-hook-stage.md) | Does closing the tool-call gating half of `OpenQuestions.md` OQ-21 (external process hooks) need a new, parallel decision mechanism alongside the already-proven `ApprovalDecider`, or does it extend that seam — and can a hook that reaches an external process be supported without reopening the session-wide blocking hazard 009 §6 already rules out? | **Judged** (2026-08-23, design → red-team → prove complete, run as an 8-agent workflow — 1 design, 3 parallel red-team lenses, 1 judge, 1 core implementation, 1 test implementation, 1 verify — then independently re-verified by the orchestrating session directly). Resolves the minimum, tool-call-point slice of `OpenQuestions.md` OQ-21. New `core/tool_call_hook.hpp` (`ToolCallHookContext` — no `EffectContext&`, no capability type, matching `ModelCallContext`'s I2 discipline — `ToolCallHook`, `hook_call_outcome`, `HookProcessedCall`/`PendingHookDecisionRound`, `HookDispatchAnswer`); `tool_pipeline.hpp` gained `enforce_hook_rewritten_tool_call_provenance()`, mirroring `middleware.hpp`'s `enforce_backend_tool_call_provenance()` exactly; `interaction_reason::hook_decision` added (both directions of the JSON codec, the AG-UI event switch, and `001-Execution-Model.md`'s normative cross-reference all updated). The design pass itself found a real coverage gap before any code was written: inserting the hook stage only right before `invoke_tool()` would leave a hook's rewrite invisible to the suspend-for-approval PRE-check one call site up, silently turning "should suspend for human approval" into "silently denied" — fixed by computing `any_needs_external_dispatch`/`any_needs_approval` from the same post-hook per-call state in one pass. A second fatal finding, closed during implementation (not the original red-team pass): `resolve_hook_decision()` deliberately does NOT reuse `resolve_codeact_ask()`'s `one_shot_approve` shape — an external process's own dispatch answer is a different question from a human's approval, so a `hook_decision` resume re-checks approval need against the real `approval_decider_`/`policy_decider_` and cascades into a genuine approval suspend if one is still needed, never treating the hook's answer as if a human had approved. 46 new tests (`test_rt_agent_session_tool_call_hook.cpp`, H1-H4), all passing; full `ctest` 229/231, the same 2 pre-existing unrelated live-TLS failures already confirmed independent of this session's work, zero new regressions. Run/turn-level gating hooks, a reference external-process dispatcher, and a declarative hook-registration surface remain named, unimplemented follow-on work — `decisions/ADR-067-middleware-turn-point-pre-model-enforcement.md`'s `TurnMiddlewareHook` already covers a real, wired, async, pre-model turn-level GATE for a host that wants one today, documented rather than re-solved here. Full record: `docs/planning/external-process-hooks-design-draft.md` (including its 2026-08-23 Addendum re-grounding every citation against nine days of code drift before this pass started). |
| [079](ADR-079-agent-spawn-runtime-and-capability-minting.md) | Can a model-invocable `agent.spawn` tool construct and drive a brand-new child `rt::AgentSession` to completion from inside the parent's own tool-call loop such that the child's granted `CapabilitySet` can never exceed what the caller holds (I2), both already-proven budget primitives (`trust::SpawnBudget`/ADR-006, `rt::SpawnCostBudget`/ADR-031) fail closed before any child is constructed (I8), and nothing the model's own tool-call arguments supply is ever trusted as authority (I3) — using only already-proven primitives, wiring nothing new into the security-critical path except one reviewable capability-minting function? | **Judged** (2026-08-23, design → four-lens red-team (I2/I3/recursion-concurrency/worktree-isolation) → prove (six pieces, one implementer per piece) → judge, this ADR). Resolves `OpenQuestions.md` OQ-14's "sharpest case" and OQ-16 for a real session. Design B accepted (child = caller's held set attenuated down to the target's declared ceiling via `attenuate()`, `cap::AgentCall` re-rooted per-agent_id to the tighter of live chain depth and the target's own declared depth) — Design A (target's declared ceiling granted unconditionally) and Design C (host-declared "service account," caller needs only a boolean gate) both rejected as I2-equivalent ambient authority, the same class of bug ADR-059 already found and fixed for `invoke_agent_tool()`. New: `rt/agent_spawn.hpp` (`AgentSpawnTool`, `SpawnTargetRegistry`, `SpawnPump` — a single-worker-thread serialization point closing a real, not-hypothetical `AsyncMutex` double-resume hazard and a racy worktree-mint check-then-act, `perform_agent_spawn()`'s nine-step pipeline), `rt/agent_spawn_child_run.hpp` (`run_child_agent_session()`, `derive_on_behalf_of`-derived child identity, background execution unconditionally disabled closing a genuine spawned-child use-after-free), `core/agent_spawn_worktree.hpp` (`mint_spawn_worktree()` — the critical I2-1 fix: a `branch`-mode grant is intersected with what the caller already holds on its own mount, never uncapped over the whole branched tree), `trust/agent_spawn_capability.hpp` (`mint_child_spawn_capabilities()`, Design B's central function). OQ-16: `AgentSession::set_static_instructions()` plus `core/session_builder.hpp` wiring `trust::push_side_summary()` — its first real caller — into both the parent and spawned-child instructions. Independently re-verified by the judging pass, not taken on self-report: full `ctest` 205/205 passing (2 pre-existing unrelated skips), the four new test binaries re-run directly (136 checks total, all passing), all four re-run clean under MSVC ASan. **Two of the design's own falsifiable claims (C3, C9 — the `SpawnPump`'s concurrency safety) are judged INCONCLUSIVE, not CORRECT**: the mechanism is sound by code-level construction (only its own worker thread ever resumes the `consume()` coroutine or touches the worktree ref-store), but no test exercises real multi-OS-thread concurrent `submit()` calls, and TSan was not run at all (no supported `-fsanitize=thread` in this MSVC/Windows toolchain). C8's own adversarial negative control (a real `Backgroundable` tool reproducing a use-after-free with the fix removed) was likewise never built — the shipped guard is structurally sound by inspection, but this judging pass corrects the prove-phase report's claim of an ASan/TSan-verified repro to INCONCLUSIVE. Other named residuals: no ref-store GC/reclamation, no refund on ordinary child-run failure (mitigated by a new per-principal `SpawnQuota`, not eliminated, both inherited from ADR-031's own Judged no-refund design), no merge-on-completion for a branch-mode child, `SpawnWorktreeGrant`'s one-grant-per-axis shape fails closed rather than guessing when a caller holds more than one distinct `FsRead`/`FsWrite` on its own mount, and the `AGENTENGINE_WITH_HTTPS`-gated half of the `session_builder.hpp` change was reviewed but not build-verified in this pass. Full record: `docs/planning/agent-spawn-runtime-design-draft.md`. |
| [080](ADR-080-sandbox-backend-registry.md) | Does `Strict` sandbox-profile resolution get a real, host-curated backend registry, or stay a permanent no-op? | **Proposed** (design → red-team → prove phases complete; awaiting project-owner "Judged" sign-off, matching ADR-070/071/072's own flow). Closes the gap `decisions/ADR-012-sandbox-profile-template-parameter-kind.md` §9 named as deferred, not decided against: `SandboxBackendRegistry` (`sandbox/sandbox_backend_registry.hpp`, new) mirrors `ChatClientRegistry`/`ToolRegistry` (host-curated, never auto-discovered) — `register_backend<B>()`, `resolve_strict(platform_id)` (008 §3's rule against the real registered set, `strict_eligibility{eligible, named_only}` bounding the blast radius of registering a backend for one agent's named selection only), and `resolve_named(HostSandboxSelection)` (the actual "always use backend X" config surface `Strict` alone cannot express, `HostSandboxSelection`'s `explicit` constructor raising the bar against an accidental I3 violation without claiming to prove provenance). `register_agent<A>()` grows an additive, defaulted `SandboxBackendRegistry const*` parameter — every existing call site unaffected. A real, independent 3-agent red-team pass (security/I2-I3, C++ correctness, architecture-fit) found and fixed a blocking bug before any code was written: the original sketch closed over a fresh, default-constructed backend per call, silently discarding per-instance state between `create()` and the next `exec()`/`destroy()` (traced by one reviewer to a spawned process being killed on the same call's return via `JobObjectLimits`'s destructor) — fixed by one long-lived `shared_ptr<B>` per registration. 15 new tests (`test_sandbox_backend_registry.cpp`, `test_agent_registry_sandbox_backend_registry.cpp`), the load-bearing one a literal regression test for that confirmed bug; full `ALL_BUILD` clean, full `ctest` 213/223 passing (the only 10 non-passing pre-existing and confirmed unrelated — gated behind an embedded-CPython toolchain check this environment lacks). Amends `008-Sandbox-and-Isolation.md` §3. Explicitly out of scope: the microVM backend itself (a separate ADR, per project-owner direction) and real production consumption of a resolved backend — nothing in this engine constructs a `SandboxHandle` from one outside tests, named as a tracked residual, not silently dropped. **Renumbered from 079 to 080 (2026-08-23)**: 079 was independently claimed by a concurrent session's work (`ADR-079-agent-spawn-runtime-and-capability-minting.md`) that reached `origin/main` first. Full record: `docs/planning/sandbox-backend-registry-design-draft.md`. |
| [081](ADR-081-jailed-python-worker-process-slice-1.md) | Does routing `execute_code` through a real, separately-jailed worker process close the gap between what 008 §1b/§3 and 010 §6 require for T3 code (kernel jail as a mandatory third layer, on top of interpreter mediation) and what the embedded CPython interpreter actually ran with (in-process with the host, zero OS-level containment) — without reopening I2/I3 or regressing the existing native-jail Windows suite? | **Judged** (2026-08-23; formalizes work already implemented in commit `a60fc3d` under project-owner direction — design → red-team → prove already complete there, this ADR independently re-built and re-ran the evidence rather than taking the commit message on self-report). Closes `docs/planning/2026-08-22-component-role-audit-tracker.md` Findings Q ("zero OS-level resource containment on the interpreter process") and R ("the RFC's mandatory kernel-jail backstop is entirely absent, and a same-day code comment had asserted this was permanently resolved without any ADR actually deciding that") — **008 §1b/§3's "kernel jail, permanently, for the interpreter" requirement is reaffirmed, not amended**; this ADR is the real work that requirement was calling for. `NativeJailBackend::create_python_worker()`/`exec_session()` (`src/backends/native_jail/native_jail_backend.{hpp,cpp}`) spawn an AppContainer + Job-Object-jailed `agentengine_python_worker.exe`, additive to and never touching the existing per-exec `create()`/`exec()`/`destroy()` path; a session-scoped watchdog thread started before `ResumeThread()` gives a real, externally-preemptible wall-clock kill and a real Job Object memory cap, re-measured directly for this ADR (an 800ms-budget busy-loop killed in 804ms; a 512MiB allocation under a 64MiB cap never completes as `ALLOCATED_OK`). `call_tool`/`agent.tools` relay through the real host-side `bridge_tool_call()` with host-minted `exec_seq` anti-replay (`RT1 Finding 1`: a mismatched-`exec_seq` `worker_query` is never dispatched, the worker is terminated) and single-flight `call_mutex` (`RT1 Finding 2`); a background idle-phase CPU budget (`RT2 Finding 1`) and watchdog-before-resume (`RT2 Finding 4`) close the resource/DoS/lifecycle lens's own findings, both re-verified at their fix sites for this ADR, not merely cited. `mediated_python_runner.cpp` is now a confirmed thin IPC client (1561→97 lines, no `Python.h`). Re-run for this ADR: the design's own 3 positive controls all pass; the pre-existing 10-test native-jail/tool-relay/B7-fix/containment-invariance suite is 100% green (zero regression); the disclosed Slice 2 regression (6 test files depending on unbuilt file/socket relay) was independently reproduced and confirmed to be clean, named denials, never a crash or hang. **Honestly disclosed, not-eliminated residuals**: Slice 2 (real file/listdir/socket relay) is unbuilt, so `execute_code` sessions needing file or network I/O do not yet work under this model at all; Linux (`LinuxNativeJailBackend`) parity for the jailed-worker model does not exist; `execute_shell`'s own process-spawn model (tracker Finding O) remains unreconciled with this design; `cpu_ms_cap` inherits ADR-004's own best-effort-only finding (`wall_ms` is the dependable bound); ADR-041's accepted Windows ACE-leak residual is unchanged. **Provenance gap named on the record**: the original design phase's two competing candidates were compared inside an agent workflow whose transcript was not preserved as a repository artifact, so this ADR reconstructs its red-team record (§5) from labeled findings (`RT1`/`RT2`) actually embedded as comments at their fix sites plus the tracker's own prose, rather than fabricating a steelman for a rejected design nobody can now check — flagged as process guidance for future security-critical passes run the same way. **Renumbered from 080 to 081 (2026-08-23)**: 080 was independently claimed by a concurrent session's work (`ADR-080-sandbox-backend-registry.md`) that reached `origin/main` first. |
| [082](ADR-082-native-jail-promotion-gate-008-9.md) | Does the `native-jail` promotion gate (008 §9, G1-G8) actually hold for the real, shipped M2 Phase C implementation, gate by gate, on the evidence that exists today — and where it doesn't, what specifically is missing? | **Judged** (2026-08-23) — a synthesis-and-verification pass over already-shipped M2 Phase C work (tasks C1-C6, all marked "(done)" in `docs/planning/milestone-2-tools-capabilities-sandbox-breakdown.md`), not a fresh design decision: no ADR had ever formalized this isolation boundary as a whole, only ADR-004 (Spiked at the time, superseded implementation-wise, since promoted to Judged 2026-08-23 by this same-day pass) and ADR-041 (Judged, one narrow residual). Re-ran the Windows native-jail suite directly for this ADR (`runner_stubs`/`backend_windows`/`abuse_corpus_windows`/`parity_windows`/`ambient_authority_windows`/`teardown_cycles_windows`, 6/6 passing) and read every Linux counterpart in full rather than trusting the roadmap's own "(done)" language. **G1 (parity): Judged**, correctly scoped — `tests/helpers/abuse_case_corpus.hpp` is one shared table compiled into both platforms' binaries, structurally proving parity for 5 outcome-classifiable cases; fork-bomb and fs-escape are deliberately excluded from that claim (asymmetric semantics), not silently folded in. **G2 (containment): Judged on Windows in full** (including fs-escape, ADR-041's own Case 4); **Judged on Linux minus fs-escape** — the Linux abuse-corpus test's own header states plainly `CLONE_NEWNS` gives a private mount namespace with nothing populating a restricted view, a real, already-tracked gap (GitHub issue #5), not discovered here. **G3 (no ambient authority): Judged on Windows** (all 4 axes, real canary positive controls); **Judged on Linux minus filesystem/process visibility** — traced to the identical root cause as G2's Linux gap (no `/proc` remount either), one defect not two, already the explicit subject of `docs/planning/linux-native-jail-pivot-root-containment-design-draft.md`. **G4 (teardown): Judged on Windows** at the bounded, disclosed 300-cycle/no-ASan scope (a real sensitivity margin, not just speed — checked directly); Linux file exists but unverified this pass (no Linux environment). **G5 (cold start)**: correctly out of scope pending 023's M8 baseline, not a gap. **G6 (downgrade visibility): open** — deliberately, honestly deferred by M2 Phase C's own tracker, still unclosed, no follow-on tracked since. **G7 (session boundary): open, INCONCLUSIVE** — unlike G5/G6, absent from M2 Phase C's own deferred list entirely (a tracking gap, not just an implementation one); plausible by code-level construction (`create()`/`create_python_worker()` mint a fresh `Instance` every call, no pooling code path exists to reuse one across sessions) but never proven by an actual canary/pooling test — the same "sound by construction, not CORRECT" call ADR-079 made for its own unproven concurrency claim. **G8**: not applicable to `native-jail` by 008's own text (wasm-only). No code changed in this pass. **Residuals named**: the pivot-root design draft closes G2+G3's shared Linux gap in one fix if built; G6 needs a real negative test plus diagnostics/trace/metrics wiring; G7 needs a real canary test turning today's structural argument into a proven fact before anyone adds instance pooling and quietly invalidates that argument's own premise; G4's Linux claim needs re-verification the next time a Linux build environment exists. **Update (2026-08-23, same day):** G6 and G7 closed with real code + tests, not left open. G6: `SandboxBackendResolutionEvent::downgraded` (new field, `sandbox_backend_registry.hpp`) distinguishes a real fallback from an ordinary win — `test_sandbox_backend_registry.cpp` items 8a-8c, paired with the registry's pre-existing "no fallback → startup fails" negative test (item 5). G7: `tests/test_native_jail_session_boundary_windows.cpp` (new) proves a canary planted in one jailed-worker session is unreachable from a second, concurrently-live session — upgraded from the original pass's INCONCLUSIVE (code-level argument only) to CORRECT (real evidence against the kernel-enforced OS-process boundary ADR-081 gave this claim). Only G2/G3's shared Linux root cause remains open from the original pass. **Update (2026-08-23, same day): G2/G3's shared Linux root cause is also closed** — see `decisions/ADR-083-linux-native-jail-pivot-root-containment.md`, run against a real Linux environment (WSL2) obtained specifically for this closure. Nothing from this pass's original findings remains open except G4's Linux re-verification (still needs a Linux environment run, now available but not yet spent on that specific re-check) and G5 (correctly out of scope pending 023's M8 baseline). |
| [083](ADR-083-linux-native-jail-pivot-root-containment.md) | Does a real `pivot_root` + bind-mount jail close `LinuxNativeJailBackend`'s filesystem and process-visibility gap (008 §9 G2/G3's Linux half) without reopening I2/I3 or regressing the existing Linux native-jail suite? | **Judged** (2026-08-23, same day as ADR-082's G6/G7 closures). Design + self-red-team phase already complete before this ADR: `docs/planning/linux-native-jail-pivot-root-containment-design-draft.md` (MS_PRIVATE-first ordering to avoid a host mount-table leak; a dedicated tmpfs jail root, not the cgroup directory; the required second `MS_REMOUNT|MS_RDONLY` call for a read-only grant, since the initial `MS_BIND` call silently ignores that flag alone; `pivot_root` not `chroot`; a fresh, namespace-local `/proc`). Prove phase: a real Linux 6.6 kernel (WSL2/Ubuntu) obtained specifically for this ADR — cgroups v2 fully mounted, real seccomp-BPF, working namespace creation under root. `LinuxNativeJailBackend::setup_jail()` (new) implements the design verbatim inside the child, before `install_seccomp_filter()` (`mount`/`umount2`/`pivot_root` are all three in that filter's own denylist). Measured, not merely argued: an ungranted host path is unreadable (`READ_DENIED err=2`) while a granted one is readable (`READ_OK`); a read-only-granted mount rejects a write with `EROFS` while a read-write one accepts it, host-side-content-verified both ways; host `/proc/mounts` line count is identical (41→41) before and after hundreds of jail creations, directly confirming the design's MUST-FIX 1 concern never fires; a new 4th teardown-census axis (`jail_dirs`) shows zero growth across 300 cycles. A real pre-existing bug was surfaced running the full suite under `ctest -j 4`: every Linux native-jail test shares the same default host paths and a process-local (not host-unique) id counter, so concurrent test binaries could mint the identical id and collide — fixed with `RUN_SERIAL TRUE` on all six gated tests, the same treatment `test_native_jail_teardown_cycles_windows` already has. Full suite 100% green both before (155/155) and after (161/161, the 6 new/gated Linux tests included) this ADR's changes. Every existing Linux native-jail test's `SandboxSpec` needed an explicit toolchain grant (`tests/helpers/native_jail_linux_toolchain_mounts.hpp`, new) for `/bin/sh` itself to be reachable inside the new jail — the exact "no implicit mount" consequence the design predicted. **Update (2026-08-23, same day): the id-collision bug is fixed at its root**, not just worked around — `create()`'s id is now PID-prefixed (`getpid()` + counter, was counter-only/process-local), verified by launching two copies of a gated test concurrently outside `ctest`/`RUN_SERIAL` entirely, both passing. `RUN_SERIAL` stays as defense in depth. Same update also closed ADR-082's G4 Linux residual (re-ran `test_native_jail_teardown_cycles_linux.cpp` directly: `jail_dirs 0 -> 0` across 300 cycles) — see ADR-082's own row. **Remaining residuals**: the test-only toolchain-mount helper grants host paths at identical guest paths, acceptable for this M2 raw-shell-exec test scope but explicitly not a production filesystem-visibility policy (010's interpreter-level mediation is that boundary); Linux parity for the *jailed Python worker process* (ADR-081, Windows-only) remains a separate, still-open gap this ADR does not touch. |
| [084](ADR-084-kata-backend-slice-1.md) | Now that `native-jail`'s own 008 §9 gate has reached Judged on both platforms (ADR-004, ADR-083), does a first-party `KataBackend` (Design A, narrowed per the microVM reversal design draft's §7 C2 finding) work as a real `SandboxBackend`, and can it clear an independent red-team pass before being treated as more than a spike? | **Judged** (2026-08-23) — real, new code, not a formalization. Operates a project-owner-directed, narrowed exception to 008 §1's locked "no `microvm` profile" decision (the decision itself is not amended — see `docs/planning/microvm-first-party-backend-design-draft.md` §9, which recorded this same day that reopen condition (i), native-jail's own gate reaching Judged on both platforms, was met). Scope: ONE pinned Kata Containers release (4.1.0, no upstream LTS track exists — checked directly), ONE runtime class (`kata-clh`/cloud-hypervisor, chosen over `kata-qemu` specifically to bound the CVE-tracking surface per the design draft's own C2 finding), registered only via `register_hardware_isolation_backend()` (ADR-080, structurally `named_only`, no call site registers it yet). `src/backends/kata/kata_backend.{hpp,cpp}` shells out to `ctr` (no new C++ dependency vendored) via `posix_spawn`; real end-to-end evidence against a live deployment (a dedicated WSL2 Ubuntu-24.04 distro, deliberately separate from the native-jail Linux work's own distro after a real conflict risk — a root-cgroup-migration script — was raised and avoided): a real guest kernel distinct from the host, state written by one `exec()` call read back correctly by a later one on the same instance (the real-VM analogue of `test_sandbox_backend_registry.cpp` item 1's own regression test), correct exit-code classification, closed-handle fail-closed, idempotent `destroy()`. **A real, load-bearing infrastructure bug found and fixed during bring-up** (not in this backend's own code): the shipped Kata `runtime-rs/configuration.toml` default symlink pointed at QEMU, not cloud-hypervisor — every `ctr run` silently launched the wrong VMM until this was caught by directly inspecting the running process tree, not by trusting configuration intent. **A real independent red-team pass** (fresh reviewer, no prior context, the same bar ADR-004 §12 met earlier the same day) against the real shipped code found and this same pass fixed two BLOCKING findings, both verified by execution: an unbounded output-drain (a 200MB guest `exec()` landed in full in host RSS with no cap — fixed with a 16 MiB cap matching `LinuxNativeJailBackend::drain_pipe_bounded()`'s own constant) and an unbounded subprocess wait (a hung/slow `ctr` call, including inside `destroy()`'s own cleanup sequence, had no timeout at all — fixed with a 30s deadline, `SIGKILL`-on-expiry, and a new correct use of the existing `exec_outcome_class::timeout` enum value); plus two MINOR fixes (a `pipe2()` partial-failure fd leak; `destroy()`'s previously fully-silent cleanup failures now logged) and one MINOR finding deliberately left unfixed (PATH-resolved `ctr` binary, no demonstrated exploit). **Honestly disclosed, not-eliminated residuals**: `SandboxSpec::capabilities`/`mounts`/`limits`/`net` are NOT enforced by this backend at all (Kata's own defaults — no CNI, ephemeral per-image rootfs — are the only containment that exists today); no GPU passthrough (would need a new `capability_kind`, deliberately not added); no upstream-published checksum manifest exists for the Kata release tarball to cross-verify this project's own recorded SHA-256 against (a narrower verification bar than ADR-013's mbedTLS precedent, named rather than glossed over); a real accuracy nuance found during the fix-verification pass itself, where a correctly-capped-but-slow call can be labeled `timeout` rather than `ok` under one specific interaction; no 008 §9 G1-G8 promotion-gate evidence exists for this backend yet. |
| [085](ADR-085-jailed-python-worker-slice-2-handle-relay.md) | Does `HandleRelay` give the jailed Python worker (ADR-081 Slice 1) real, capability-mediated file and socket I/O, closing Slice 1's own named residual, without reopening I2/I3 or regressing the existing suite? | **Judged** (2026-08-23). Real, new code: `dispatch_open`/`dispatch_listdir`/`dispatch_file_read`/`dispatch_file_write`/`dispatch_file_close`/`dispatch_connect_authorize`/`dispatch_connect_send`/`dispatch_connect_recv`/`dispatch_connect_close` (`native_jail_backend.cpp`), the worker-side `_AeRelayFile` class and socket relay wrappers (`python_worker_mediation.cpp`'s bootstrap source). **Two real implementation-time falsifications, each found by running the actual tests, not by review, and disclosed rather than hidden**: (1) the ORIGINAL design planned `DuplicateHandle`-based file relay; real I/O against the duplicate fails with `ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE` inside the AppContainer'd worker even though the duplicate is confirmed open (`GetHandleInformation` succeeds) — a real Windows AppContainer limitation, not a wiring bug; shipped design keeps the real `HANDLE` host-side and relays read/write/close by `file_id` instead, the same shape sockets already needed. (2) the socket_id<->socket correlation was planned as an ordinary instance attribute; this vendored CPython's `socket.socket` carries no `__dict__` at all — fixed with a `weakref.WeakKeyDictionary`. **A real independent red-team pass** (fresh reviewer, no prior context) found and this same pass fixed one BLOCKING finding, verified by a new positive control: only `connect`/`send`/`sendall`/`recv`/`close` were mediated on `socket.socket` — `bind`/`listen`/`accept`/`connect_ex`/`sendto`/`recvfrom`/`makefile`/`dup`/`detach`/`share`/`shutdown` were still the real, unmediated CPython implementation with zero capability check (ADR-004 AC-S1's own measured evidence covers `connect()` specifically, not this wider surface) — fixed with explicit denial, the same defense-in-depth posture the existing `os.*` denial list already uses; plus one real bug (a `uint16_t` cast on an unvalidated guest-supplied port was undefined behavior, reachable via an ordinary typo, not an attack) and three MINOR fixes (a base64-decode-before-size-clamp gap that undercut the stated per-call memory ceiling by ~64x; a comment overstating the outer watchdog as a backstop for a host-thread-blocking call it cannot actually interrupt; an exception-unsafe test-only resolver-override seam, now RAII-guarded). **Executed evidence**: 5 of ADR-081's own 6 disclosed-failing tests now fully pass; the 6th (`test_reference_agent_task_corpus`'s pandas_groupby) still fails on a confirmed pre-existing, unrelated `extra_sys_path` ACL gap (verified via direct `git stash` bisection against the unmodified baseline); two new positive/negative controls (a real socket round trip under a real `cap::NetOut` grant, requiring a new SSRF-bypass-free test-only resolver-override seam since `resolve_and_validate` categorically blocks every hermetic same-machine target by design; kernel-level, not merely Python-wrapper-level, read-only enforcement) both pass; full project suite 264/265 passing both before and after the red-team fixes, zero collateral regression. **Honestly disclosed, not-eliminated residuals**: the pandas-import ACL gap; no `ResourceLimits::net_bytes` reconciliation for the raw-socket relay's flat per-call byte cap; no UDP relay (denied outright) or IPv6; a non-ASCII UTF-8 chunk-boundary decode gap in size-bounded text reads (unexercised by any current ASCII fixture); `execute_shell`'s still-separate process-spawn model (unchanged from ADR-081); Linux parity (still unbuilt); the test-only resolver override's "never used from production" is convention-enforced only, not type-system- or build-flag-enforced. |
| [086](ADR-086-kata-backend-slice-2-enforcement.md) | Does `KataBackend` Slice 2 (ADR-084's own named residual: enforcing `SandboxSpec::mounts`/`limits`/`net` instead of silently ignoring them) close that gap for real, and can it clear an independent red-team pass before being treated as more than a spike? | **Judged** (2026-08-24). `MountSpec` grants become real `ctr run --mount type=bind,...` bind mounts (`BlobRef` still fails closed, same check `LinuxNativeJailBackend::create()` has); `ResourceLimits.memory_bytes` maps to `--memory-limit`, verified REAL — a 256 MiB cap produced a guest showing ~257 MiB via `free -m`, not the ~2GiB unconfigured default; `wall_ms`/`output_bytes`, if set, override `run_ctr()`'s fixed defaults per already-created instance (verified: a 3000ms cap cut a guest `sleep 10` off at ~3.02s); `NetPolicy` now fails closed on anything beyond the default `deny_all` (no CNI/egress-proxy exists to honor a real allowlist). `cpu_ms`/`pids`/`fds`/`disk_bytes`/`net_bytes`/`quota_bytes`/`capabilities` remain unenforced, each named with its own reason. **A real independent red-team pass** (fresh reviewer, no prior context, the same bar ADR-004 §12/ADR-084 §4 met) found and this same pass fixed one BLOCKING finding, verified by execution: `create()` built the `--mount` value by direct string concatenation into a SINGLE comma-delimited `ctr` value — a `guest_path` (or `source`) containing an embedded `,src=...,dst=...` segment silently hijacked the mount to an ARBITRARY host path (reproduced: bind-mounted the host's real `/etc` into the guest, readable via `cat /mnt/hijacked/passwd`), a full I2 violation through a spec field this backend claims to enforce — fixed by rejecting a comma in either path outright (`ctr`'s mount grammar has no escaping mechanism). Also fixed a REAL GAP (a failed `create()` — reproduced via a nonsensically tiny `memory_bytes` — left a containerd container object leaked with no handle ever reaching the caller, unreachable by any later `destroy()` call; fixed with best-effort cleanup on the failure path, mirroring `destroy()`'s own posture) and documented a scope boundary as a MINOR/judgment-call finding (`wall_ms` does not and should not bound `create()`'s own VM-boot call — stated explicitly in the header comment now rather than left for a reader to infer). Re-verified: both `test_kata_backend_linux` (Slice 1) and the new `test_kata_backend_slice2_linux` 100% passing after the fixes, zero regression; the exact injection payload now returns `kata_backend.mount_path_invalid`; the leak repro's `ctr containers ls` count is unchanged before/after the fix. |
| [087](ADR-087-sandbox-spec-capability-enforcement.md) | Does `SandboxSpec::capabilities` (ADR-086 §5.1's own named residual) become the real authority behind `mounts`/`net` for every mount/net-shaped `SandboxBackend` — not `KataBackend` alone, since that would itself create a per-backend divergence in what `SandboxSpec` fields mean — and can the design clear an independent red-team pass before any implementation lands? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; design → red-team → fix → implement → local Windows verification complete, awaiting project-owner Judged sign-off). Two new capability kinds, `cap::SandboxMount`/`cap::SandboxNetOut` (`trust/capability.hpp`) — deliberately not a reuse of `cap::FsRead`/`FsWrite`/`NetOut` (those are `mount_id`-keyed, Worktree/`FileSystemAdapter`-mediated; `MountSpec` names a raw host path directly — conflating the two would be a real capability-confusion hazard, the same judgment ADR-071 already made for `NativeExec` vs. `Exec`). A single free function, `authorize_spec()` (`sandbox/sandbox.hpp`), called first-thing by all four real mount-granting entry points: `KataBackend::create()`, `LinuxNativeJailBackend::create()`, `NativeJailBackend::create()` (Windows), and `NativeJailBackend::create_python_worker()` (Windows — the mediated Python worker's own separate entry point). Opt-in, scoped to presence of the relevant grant kind specifically (not `CapabilitySet::size()`) — every existing call site holds zero grants of either kind today, so behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged for all of them. **An independent adversarial red-team pass run against the pre-implementation DESIGN DRAFT, before any code existed** (per CLAUDE.md's `design → red-team → prove → judge` for an I2-touching, three-backend change) found this design unsafe to implement as originally written: FATAL — `path_prefix_covers()` reuse has no defense against a literal `..` lexically defeating prefix coverage with zero filesystem access, fixed by rejecting any `.`/`..` path component outright, the same "reject the injection surface entirely" move `KataBackend`'s own comma-injection fix (ADR-086) already established; BLOCKING — the mechanism as drafted never engaged on `NativeJailBackend::create_python_worker()`, the actual production mount-granting path for the mediated Python interpreter (a structurally separate entry point from `create()`), fixed by enumerating and wiring every real entry point, not just the three-method concept surface; BLOCKING — the drafted opt-out (`spec.capabilities.size() == 0`) would fail OPEN the moment any unrelated capability (e.g. `cap::Background`) appeared on a spec with real mounts, fixed by scoping the opt-in to presence of the SPECIFIC relevant grant kind instead; BLOCKING — `NetPolicy` enforcement would have stayed divergent across backends even after this design shipped (Kata already fails closed on non-`deny_all`, native-jail silently ignored it), fixed by giving both native-jail backends the identical unconditional check Kata already had; plus three MUST-FIX findings (an ambiguous, type-mismatched `quota_bytes` "0 = unspecified" rule borrowed from a field shape it didn't match — fixed by not carrying `quota_bytes` on the new capability at all until quota enforcement itself exists; an unpinned `host_allowlist` matching grammar — fixed, pinned to exact match after lowercasing, no wildcard support; a missing I3 struct comment — fixed, matching the `NativeExec` precedent). All fixed before implementation began, not after. **Verified this session** (Windows-only, no local Linux/Kata environment available): full local rebuild, zero new errors/warnings; new pure-logic suite `test_sandbox_capability_authorization.cpp` (16/16 checks, platform-independent, runs in CI everywhere) proves the fixed coverage/opt-in/`..`-rejection/case-insensitive-net-matching/multi-grant/`BlobRef`-passthrough behavior directly; full local `ctest` run confirmed zero regression on every existing Windows-buildable test. **Honestly disclosed residuals**: symlink-based escape inside an otherwise-correctly-granted directory is not detected (same unsolved class of gap ADR-071 §6 item 3 already names for a different surface — a real fix needs a handle-anchored check at the point of use, separate work); `cap::SandboxMount::host_path_prefix` is an unbounded raw filesystem prefix with no ceiling (a syntactically valid `"/"` grant authorizes everything, an operator-discipline risk, disclosed not fixed); Linux (`LinuxNativeJailBackend`, `KataBackend`) and the Windows-only `create_python_worker()` integration path were wired and compile-reviewed but not independently re-verified end-to-end against a live Linux/Kata run this session (no such environment available) — a future session should confirm the existing Linux native-jail/Kata suites still pass unmodified (expected, since they hold zero `SandboxMount`/`SandboxNetOut` grants today, but not yet independently proven). |
| [088](ADR-088-kata-backend-abuse-corpus.md) | Does `KataBackend` get its first 008 §9 G1/G2 abuse-corpus promotion-gate evidence (the last named residual in `kata_backend.hpp`'s own header comment), and can `exec_outcome_class::oom` be closed via an exit-code-137 heuristic mirroring `LinuxNativeJailBackend`'s own fallback? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; design → red-team → fix → implement → local verification complete, awaiting project-owner Judged sign-off). The OOM-classification design was **investigated and REJECTED**, not shipped: an independent red-team pass found the exit-code-137 analogy to native-jail's own heuristic a non sequitur — native-jail's version rests on a documented Linux kernel `wait()` fact about a process it directly reaps as PID 1 of its own namespace, while `KataBackend`'s `outcome.exit_code` reflects the HOST-side `ctr` CLI's own exit, three RPC hops removed from the guest's actual death, with no source anywhere in this repo confirming any hop preserves the 128+signal convention, no fallback for the direct-SIGKILL-to-`ctr` case (`exit_code` stays -1, never 137), and no documented default guest-VM memory size to size a positive control against (the "~2GiB default" the original design assumed was itself unsourced). `exec_outcome_class::oom` stays unreachable for this backend — a named, investigated gap now, not a silently-unattempted one. The SAME red-team pass, reading `exec()`'s timeout path closely enough to evaluate that design, surfaced a real, separate, previously-undisclosed BLOCKING gap: `run_ctr()`'s `wall_ms`-timeout `kill(pid, SIGKILL)` only terminates the HOST-side `ctr` CLI wrapper, not the guest-side process it was attached to, which could keep running orphaned inside the persistent container until a later call happened to reap it — `exec_outcome_class::timeout` was being returned without the workload having actually stopped. **Fixed this ADR**: `exec()`'s timeout branch now also issues a best-effort `ctr tasks kill --exec-id <id> --signal SIGKILL <container>` against the same `--exec-id` the timed-out call minted, targeting the guest process directly; failure logs to stderr rather than silently vanishing, matching `destroy()`'s own established cleanup posture. New test `tests/test_kata_backend_abuse_corpus_linux.cpp`: infinite-loop/`wall_ms` (contained, with a heartbeat-file positive control that specifically proves the guest process actually stops — not merely that the host CLI call returned — the exact defect this ADR fixes) and unbounded-output/`output_bytes` (captured-bytes-only claim, same shape as native-jail's own corpus) each with a positive control per 008 §9 G2; fork-bomb/`pids` is a deliberately bounded, non-asserting documentation case (no `pids` mechanism exists for this backend at all — silently omitting the case would make the corpus non-comparable to native-jail's four-case shape without saying so, but a literal unbounded bomb against an uncapped guest is a real machine-safety risk this project's own "hostile tests are resource-capped" rule forbids). **Verified this session**: the pre-implementation design went through an independent adversarial red-team pass (resumed once after an interruption) before any code was written; this repo's already-configured `build-linux` WSL/Linux CMake cache was reconfigured with `-DAGENTENGINE_BUILD_KATA_BACKEND=ON -DAGENTENGINE_KATA_SANDBOX_TESTS=ON` (confirmed landed via `CMakeCache.txt`, not assumed) and all three Kata test targets plus the `kata_backend` library compiled clean against a real Linux g++ toolchain — more verification than any prior Kata slice received in-session, though still short of execution against a live deployment; full local Windows `ctest` run, 247/247 passing, zero regressions. **Honestly disclosed residuals**: no real Kata/containerd deployment was reachable anywhere in this session, so the new test remains un-executed (same disclosed limitation every prior Kata test in this tree already carries); the unbounded-output case's own guest-process-orphan risk is named but not fixed (scope-bounded to the timeout path the red-team actually found concretely broken); the `ctr tasks kill --exec-id` flag syntax this ADR's fix depends on is not independently re-verified against a live deployment, carried forward from every other `ctr` CLI assumption already in this file; `ResourceLimits::pids`/`fds`/`disk_bytes`/`net_bytes` remain unenforced (Slice 2's own unchanged gap). |
| [089](ADR-089-kata-backend-output-cap-orphan-fix.md) | Does `KataBackend` close the unbounded-output guest-process-orphan gap ADR-088 explicitly disclosed but did not fix — an `output_bytes` cap breach falling through to an unconditional blocking `waitpid()` on the host-side `ctr` process, with no guest-side kill? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; design → red-team → fix → implement → local verification complete, awaiting project-owner Judged sign-off). An independent adversarial red-team pass against the pre-implementation design found the first draft unsound in two BLOCKING ways: the proposed trigger signal (`output_truncated`, set only when a read was *partially* dropped) silently misses a read landing exactly on the cap boundary — a real, not edge-case-only, gap for round cap sizes like 4096/65536 against the function's own 4096-byte read buffer, under which the fix could no-op on realistic inputs and reopen the exact hang it exists to close; and leaving `exec()`'s classification "unchanged" (exit-code-only `ok`/`crash`) let a capped-and-killed run still report `ok` whenever the added courtesy non-blocking reap happened to observe a clean exit race. **Fixed before implementation**: a dedicated `output_capped` flag, set unconditionally at the force-close site rather than inferred from `output_truncated`; `run_ctr()`'s tail now treats `output_capped` the same as `timed_out` (courtesy non-blocking reap first, preferring `ctr`'s real exit code if it already died on its own, then unconditional SIGKILL + bounded reap — never an unbounded blocking wait); `exec()`'s classification becomes a real three-way branch, with a capped exec reporting `exec_outcome_class::policy_violation` (not `crash`) — matching this codebase's own established idiom for "the host stopped this on policy grounds, not the workload's fault" (`LinuxNativeJailBackend`'s idle-phase CPU-budget kill, `MediatedPythonRunner`'s capability denials) rather than risking a caller reading `crash` as "the workload itself failed" and retrying unmodified. The same pass's fourth finding attacked the test-plan draft directly: extending the abuse-corpus test's Case 2 with a heartbeat-file proof (mirroring Case 1's own proof of the ADR-088 timeout fix) reused `contained_spec` unmodified, which had no bind mount for the heartbeat file and a `wall_ms` short enough that — because `run_ctr()`'s poll loop only exits when BOTH streams close, and capping stdout alone doesn't end stderr's still-open read — the case would very likely just run until the wall_ms deadline anyway, exercising the already-proven `timeout` path instead of the new `output_capped`-alone path. Fixed: added the missing mount, a materially larger `wall_ms` (2500ms) so a fast `policy_violation` return isn't ambiguous with hitting the same deadline, and the test now asserts `klass` is one of `policy_violation`/`timeout` (never `ok`/`crash`) rather than assuming which one an unreachable real deployment would actually produce — both trigger the identical guest-side kill, so the heartbeat-stopped-changing proof holds either way. **Verified this session**: same WSL Ubuntu `build-linux` real g++/cmake/ninja cache used for ADR-088 rebuilt `agentengine_kata_backend` and all three Kata test binaries clean; full local Windows `ctest` run confirms zero regressions elsewhere (this backend is gated `NOT WIN32`, entirely absent from the Windows build graph, so this run's purpose is confirming the rest of the tree is untouched). **Honestly disclosed residuals**: no real Kata/containerd deployment was reachable this session, so the extended test remains un-executed; the `ctr tasks kill --exec-id` flag syntax remains unverified against a live deployment (unchanged from ADR-088); `exec_outcome_class::oom`, `ResourceLimits::pids`/`fds`/`disk_bytes`/`net_bytes`, and `ExecRequest::source` Runner-mediation remain the same unchanged gaps ADR-088 already names. |
| [090](ADR-090-kata-backend-pids-limit-investigated-and-deferred.md) | Can `KataBackend` close `ResourceLimits::pids` enforcement, named unenforced since ADR-086 ("would need a full OCI `config.json` via `ctr run --config`")? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; investigation complete, decided in the negative, mirroring ADR-088 §2's own "investigated and rejected" precedent for `exec_outcome_class::oom` — awaiting project-owner sign-off). Every claim checked against containerd's real current source (`docs/research/2026-08-24-containerd-ctr-run-config-vs-convenience-flags.md`), not assumed: no `--pids-limit` (or equivalent) flag exists anywhere in the current `ctr run` CLI; `--config <path>` is the ONLY way to set `linux.resources.pids.limit`, but is fully exclusive of every convenience flag this backend's `create()` already relies on (`--mount`, `--memory-limit`, implicit image pull/unpack/snapshot creation — confirmed directly from `run_unix.go`'s `NewContainer()`, a hard `if`/`else` branch split, not layering); `--config` mode doesn't prepare a rootfs at all, leaving the caller to have one already on disk; no `ctr tasks update` command exists (`attach/checkpoint/delete/exec/list/kill/metrics/pause/ps/resume/start`, no `update`), closing off a lower-risk "create normally, patch pids after" alternative before it could be attempted. The escalating chain of findings hit a genuine information gap, not just added complexity: the only remaining CLI-only path (`ctr images pull` + `ctr snapshot unpack <digest>` + `ctr snapshot prepare`) requires knowing the internal, content-addressed chain-ID snapshot name `unpack` creates — containerd computes this internally but exposes no stable `ctr` CLI-level query for it; discovering it would mean scraping `ctr snapshot list` against undocumented internal naming conventions, not calling a documented API. **Decision: `ResourceLimits::pids` stays unenforced.** This is the direct, foreseeable cost of this backend's own deliberate architecture choice (shell out to `ctr`, never embed containerd's gRPC/ttrpc client, per `kata_backend.hpp`'s own header comment) — real enforcement needs either reopening that choice or a future containerd CLI release closing the information gap, neither undertaken here. No source code changed this pass; `kata_backend.hpp`'s residual list updated to mark `pids` investigated-and-deferred rather than merely unattempted. **A real, smaller opportunity surfaced as a byproduct, not implemented this pass**: `ctr run --rlimit-nofile` IS a genuine existing convenience flag (unlike `pids`) that could close `ResourceLimits::fds` within the existing convenience-flag `create()` path, no `--config` rewrite needed — named in the header comment so a future session does not have to rediscover it. |
| [091](ADR-091-kata-backend-fds-rlimit-nofile.md) | Can `ResourceLimits::fds` be closed via the `--rlimit-nofile` convenience flag ADR-090's own investigation surfaced as a real, much smaller opportunity? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; implemented, local verification complete, awaiting project-owner Judged sign-off). `KataBackend::create()`'s `limit_args` gains `if (spec.limits.fds > 0) { --rlimit-nofile <n> }`, alongside the existing `memory_bytes` mapping — a bare `<n>` (no `soft:hard` colon) sets both soft and hard `RLIMIT_NOFILE` to the same value, confirmed against containerd's real `run_unix.go` parsing logic, not guessed from CLI usage text. **Honestly scoped, not oversold**: `oci.WithRlimit` governs the container's own initial process for certain (this backend's `sleep infinity` placeholder), but whether a LATER `ctr tasks exec`-spawned process — this backend's actual per-call workload path — inherits the same limit is a genuinely open question this ADR does not resolve by inspection alone. A new test case (`tests/test_kata_backend_slice2_linux.cpp` case 6) targets exactly that question directly: execs `ulimit -n` under a specific, unusual cap (123) and asserts the exec'd process's own reported limit matches exactly — real, falsifiable evidence either way the first time this runs against a live deployment, not a claim resting on inspection. Compile-verified via the same WSL real g++/cmake/ninja cache used by every prior Kata slice this session; no live deployment reachable, so the propagation question itself remains genuinely unresolved, named as the ADR's own most important residual rather than assumed either way. |
| [092](ADR-092-kata-backend-disk-net-bytes-investigated-and-deferred.md) | Can `KataBackend` close `ResourceLimits::disk_bytes`/`net_bytes`, carried as "unenforced and uninvestigated" since ADR-086? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; investigation complete, decided in the negative, mirroring ADR-090's own precedent — awaiting project-owner sign-off). Unlike `pids`/`fds`, these two are assigned by `008-Sandbox-and-Isolation.md` §1b to **interpreter-level mediation** — a mediating process AgentEngine itself controls, instrumenting `open()`/`socket()` at the point of use — not a create-time backend resource cap; confirmed against this project's own spec and `src/backends/native_jail/cgroup_limits.hpp`'s own identical precedent for `LinuxNativeJailBackend` ("`disk_bytes`, `net_bytes`... 008 §1b assigns those to interpreter-level mediation"). `KataBackend::exec()` shells `/bin/sh -c <source>` directly inside the guest via `ctr tasks exec`, with no AgentEngine-owned process inside the VM for such mediation to attach to at all — unlike `native-jail`, whose OS jail is explicitly paired with exactly this mediation via `MediatedPythonRunner`/`HandleRelay` (ADR-081/ADR-085), entirely host-side. Building an equivalent for Kata means constructing a wholly new guest-side mediating subsystem, not a resource-limit wiring change. A backend-level fallback for `disk_bytes` (a VM/snapshot quota, independent of mediation) was also checked and is blocked by the IDENTICAL `--config`-rewrite obstacles ADR-090 already found for `pids` — no `ctr run` convenience flag for disk quota exists either (`--blockio-config-file`/`--blockio-class` govern I/O bandwidth/priority, not a size cap). `net_bytes` is additionally moot right now regardless of mediation: this backend already fails closed on any `NetPolicy` beyond `deny_all` (Slice 2/ADR-086, unchanged), so there is no network path to meter in the first place — it collapses into the separately-named CNI gap, now explicitly named as the actual prerequisite for ever revisiting `net_bytes`, not a parallel/independent problem. **Decision: both stay unenforced.** No source code changed this pass; `kata_backend.hpp`'s residual list updated to mark both fields investigated-and-deferred rather than "uninvestigated." |
| [093](ADR-093-kata-backend-netpolicy-allowlist-config-cni.md) | Can `KataBackend` enforce a REAL `NetPolicy` allowlist, given ADR-090 already found `--config` mode blocked by a rootfs-prep chain-ID gap for `pids`? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; implemented, WSL compile-verified, awaiting red-team + project-owner sign-off). Two findings change ADR-090's picture: (1) `ctr run --cni` is real but functionally broken for Kata specifically — source-verified lifecycle-ordering bug, containerd's CNI setup runs AFTER task-CREATE (`run.go`) but Kata's own network-endpoint discovery + VM boot both run synchronously DURING task-CREATE (`virtcontainers/api.go`'s `CreateSandbox()`), confirmed by contrast against containerd's own CRI plugin which sets up CNI BEFORE `controller.Create()` — the order Kata actually needs (`docs/research/2026-08-24-containerd-ctr-run-cni-kata-ordering.md`); (2) `ctr images mount <ref> <target>` (`cmd/ctr/commands/images/mount.go`, not fetched by ADR-090's own session) solves the rootfs-prep gap entirely — it computes the internal chain ID itself and mounts a real rootfs at a caller-chosen path, zero chain-ID discovery needed. So this backend now does what containerd's CRI plugin does, manually: `ip netns add` + `cnitool add` (a real, separately-installed CLI, NOT bundled with `ctr` — new deployment precondition) BEFORE `ctr run --config`, joining the pre-populated netns via the spec's own `linux.namespaces`. `NetPolicy::allowlist` hostnames are resolved ONCE at `create()` time via the SAME `agentengine::sandbox::resolve_and_validate()` `HostEgressProxy` (ADR-011) itself uses — reused, not re-implemented — then a default-deny `nft` ruleset inside the fresh netns permits only the resolved `IP:port` pairs. A real correctness gap found during this design (not copied from an existing pattern): the guest's OWN DNS resolution of an allowlisted hostname could legitimately return a different IP than the host-side resolution, which the IP-pinned firewall rule would then unhelpfully block — closed via a generated `/etc/hosts` bind mount pinning each hostname to the exact firewalled IP. **Disclosed residual, not hidden**: pinning is create-time-only (not per-request like `HostEgressProxy`) — NOT a rebinding/SSRF hole (the firewall stays pinned to the originally-validated IP, admitting no new traffic on a later DNS change), purely an availability/correctness residual (a rotated legitimate IP silently loses connectivity until recreation). The hand-authored OCI spec (`build_oci_spec_json()`) matches containerd's own real default namespaces/capabilities/masked-and-readonly-paths/mounts/rlimits (`pkg/oci/spec.go`/`pkg/oci/mounts.go`, fetched+read this pass) — real parity with the convenience-flag path, not an invented posture; device-cgroup allowlisting beyond the base default-deny rule is deliberately not reproduced (Kata's guest `/dev` comes from the guest kernel, not host device-cgroup rules). Verification: WSL compile of the rewritten backend and all four Kata test binaries (three pre-existing + new `test_kata_backend_slice9_10_linux`, 5 cases including a live two-directional allowlist proof); no live containerd/Kata/`cnitool`/nftables deployment reachable this session, so every runtime-behavior claim is disclosed as compile-verified/logically-derived only. Residuals: `pids` (ADR-090) not reopened despite SLICE 9's rootfs-prep finding also applying there; `disk_bytes`/`net_bytes` (ADR-092) unaffected (this controls reachability, not byte volume); three new host dependencies (`cnitool`, CNI plugin+config, `nft`) named in `kata_backend.hpp`'s own header comment. |
| [094](ADR-094-execrequest-mediation-contract-and-cap-exec-gap.md) | Can `KataBackend` close its last named residual, `ExecRequest::source` "not yet Runner-mediated"? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; Finding 1 implemented + fully verified, Finding 2 investigated and deferred, awaiting project-owner sign-off). Split into two genuinely separate findings on investigation. **Finding 1**: tracing the real `execute_code` call path (`tools/cli_chat.cpp`'s `real_execute_code()`) confirms model-supplied code never reaches any `SandboxBackend::exec()` today — it routes through `MediatedPythonRunner`, a separate IPC-driven jailed worker. The "caller mediates, backend trusts" contract each of the three backends' `exec()` already implements is correct, settled architecture (008 §1b layer 2), not unfinished — but was independently restated three times, in `kata_backend.hpp`/`linux_native_jail_backend.hpp`/`native_jail_backend.hpp`, a real drift risk (the same "no per-backend divergence" shape ADR-087 already named for a different field). **Fixed**: consolidated into one canonical `008-Sandbox-and-Isolation.md` §2 paragraph (corrected from an initial draft's wrong-section proposal, §1b, caught by an independent adversarial red-team pass), with all three backend headers now pointing to it instead of re-describing it. Zero runtime code changed. **Finding 2**: the same red-team pass caught a real error in the first draft's own analysis — an initial claim that `cap::Exec` (`capability.hpp`) was scoped to *nested* exec only (008 §4's table) and therefore irrelevant was WRONG; `007-Capability-and-Trust-Model.md:59` independently confirms `Exec<profile>` = "create a sandbox of a profile," a top-level host-side gate `authorize_spec()` never checks anywhere, for any backend — the same shape of gap ADR-087 already fixed once for `mounts`/`net`, reproduced one layer earlier. **Deliberately NOT implemented this pass**: wiring it hit a genuine open question (`sandbox_backend_registry.hpp`'s `register_hardware_isolation_backend(name, ...)` shows backend identity is a host-chosen registration string, not a fixed enum — no `sandbox_profile` enum exists anywhere in the tree, contradicting `cap::Exec::profile_name`'s own "matches `sandbox_profile`'s enumerators" comment) — shipping a check against a guessed granularity (backend-type vs. registration-name) would be decorative, not real, containment. Disclosed as a real, open follow-on (design draft §7 items 4-5), not silently dropped. Verification (Finding 1 only, Finding 2 has no code to verify): WSL build of both edited Linux targets clean, full Windows build clean, full `ctest` 247/247. |
| [095](ADR-095-kata-backend-slice-11-config-pipeline-pids-disk-net.md) | Can `KataBackend` close its last three unenforced `ResourceLimits` axes — `pids` (ADR-090), `disk_bytes`/`net_bytes` (ADR-092) — via a project-owner-authorized full redesign/rewrite reusing ADR-093's `--config` pipeline? | **Proposed** (2026-08-24; design → independent red-team (8 findings, 5 BLOCKING) → fix → implement → WSL compile-verified + one real live-run-verified fix, awaiting project-owner sign-off). Reopens ADR-090/ADR-092's own negative decisions — both correct when written, both made stale by ADR-093's unrelated `ctr images mount`/netns-nftables findings. `pids`: same-shape `linux.resources.pids.limit` addition `memory_bytes` already has. `net_bytes`: a NAMED `nft` quota object shared across the netns's `output` AND a new `input` chain — total bidirectional bytes, entirely host-side. `disk_bytes`: a backend-owned, loop-device-backed, fixed-size ext4 filesystem as a real overlay's writable half — enforcement is a real guest-kernel `ENOSPC`, not a heuristic; `fallocate -l` reserves the full size on the HOST eagerly at `create()` time, a deliberate multi-tenant-safety choice, not an oversight. **Independent red-team pass (fresh reviewer, no session context) found 8 issues, 5 BLOCKING, all fixed before implementation**: (1) no `lower_dir` tracked separately from `rootfs_dir` would leak a containerd snapshot every `disk_bytes > 0` cycle — fixed, `Instance::lower_dir` always tracked, always the real unmount target; (2) the pre-existing `cleanup_partial()` lambda already ran `remove_all` BEFORE its own unmount call, which would corrupt a live writable overlay once one existed — fixed, reordered; (3) `fallocate -l` was mischaracterized as lazy/sparse (verified against `fallocate(2)`: it's eager) — disclosure corrected, mechanism kept; (4) the drafted `nft add quota` command omitted mandatory `{ }` braces, a real syntax error that would have shipped `net_bytes` completely non-functional while its own proposed test appeared to pass — fixed; (5) **I2-relevant**: a pre-existing, capability-independent mount-validation opt-out (`authorize_spec()` skips its own check for a caller holding zero grants) combined with the new loop-mounted file to produce a live host-filesystem-corruption path reachable with ZERO capability grants — fixed, `create()` now unconditionally rejects any `MountSpec` host path targeting `/run/agentengine-kata`, independent of any grant (`kata_backend.workdir_mount_forbidden`); (6) `losetup -f`'s TOCTOU under concurrent `create()` calls, benign for corruption but real against the registry's own "tolerate concurrent calls" contract — fixed with a scoped `std::mutex`, not left open; (7)/(8) MINOR disclosure/wording corrections. **Verification**: no live Kata/containerd/`cnitool`/`nft`/`losetup` deployment reachable this session (confirmed via `git stash` baseline comparison that the 4 Kata test failures — `Permission denied` under `/run/agentengine-kata`, no root in this WSL environment — are 100% pre-existing, not a regression); the ONE finding provably testable without a live deployment (finding 5's workdir-mount-exclusion fix, which runs before any resource acquisition) is proven by REAL test execution this session (3 new cases in `test_kata_backend_slice2_linux.cpp`, all passing); everything else is WSL compile-verified only (full rebuild clean, `ctest` 156/160 — same 4 pre-existing failures, zero new ones); Windows build unaffected by construction (`AGENTENGINE_BUILD_KATA_BACKEND=OFF`, backend absent from that build graph). Residuals: `disk_bytes == 0`'s possible pre-existing read-only-rootfs gap named but not fixed (out of scope, would change behavior for callers who don't opt in); `MountSpec::quota_bytes` confirmed a separate, still-unenforced gap, not conflated with `disk_bytes`; `cpu_ms`, `exec_outcome_class::oom`, GPU passthrough, `cap::Exec` (ADR-094) all unchanged. |
| [096](ADR-096-session-sandbox-lifecycle-context-provider-wiring.md) | Does session→sandbox lifecycle wiring for Shell go through `SandboxBackendRegistry`/`SandboxHandle`, or through a `ContextProvider` composed via the existing `Ms...`/`ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>` mechanism? | **Proposed — design/red-team complete, `SandboxToolProvider` now implemented and tested (2026-08-25), still awaiting `Judged` sign-off.** `SandboxToolProvider` (`src/backends/native_jail/sandbox_tool_provider.hpp`) constructs `SessionShellSandbox` (real, Tier-1 this session) lazily on its own first `on_context()` call, contributing `run_shell`'s `ToolDescriptor` and assigning `EffectContext::sandbox_fs` in the same call — reusing `ContextProvider`'s mutable `EffectContext&` seam and `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>` (ADR-074) rather than routing through `SandboxBackend::exec()`/`SandboxHandle` (deferred, not rejected, until a second real backend needs to run Shell) — plus idempotent per-session scratch-directory creation and the digest-based subdirectory-naming defense-in-depth check (C8). Proven by `tests/test_sandbox_tool_provider.cpp` (19 checks: concept conformance + move-only proofs, lazy construction, `ctx.sandbox_fs` population, a real command run through the actual `invoke_tool()` pipeline with the write landing on the real host filesystem, reuse across a later round with no directory recreated, and direct coverage of the digest check against both synthetic and real `compute_digest()` output). Full project rebuild and the full `ctest` suite (252/252) green, zero regressions. Five independent red-team rounds preceded implementation, not one: round 1 caught a fabricated `CodeActRunnerBinding` "release-on-drop" claim (ADR-030 makes Python binding permanent for the process's life — Python's own session-lifecycle wiring is explicitly out of scope of this ADR as a result); round 2 empirically CONFIRMED by real MSVC compile that composing a non-copyable provider this way makes `AgentSession::fork_from()` fail to compile (not a runtime bug) for that session type — the design's central safety property; rounds 3-4 found, then independently re-verified the fix for, a real `clear_in_process_state()` re-`engage()` gap (session reuse after that call is a real, tested pattern in this codebase, contrary to an intermediate self-directed claim that it wasn't); round 5 corrected a double-hex-encoding error and an overclaimed "no path-splice filtering needed" framing in the per-session subdirectory-naming design. **Three of five red-team rounds found this line of work's own self-directed reasoning wrong or overstated, always in a "grep-confirmed" claim specifically.** Implementation independently re-corroborated C1/C3/C6/C8 by actually building and testing against them (no new error found) but did NOT re-touch C2/C4/C5/C7 at all — those still rest entirely on the original red-team verification, named as what a `Judged` review should scrutinize most. One small, precedented piece shipped even earlier, independent of the rest: `tool_pipeline.hpp::background_task()` resets `ctx.sandbox_fs = nullptr`, mirroring an already-Judged `report_progress` reset (ADR-060) exactly. Explicitly out of scope: routing through `SandboxHandle` (Design A, deferred); Python's own session-lifecycle wiring; a real connection from `SandboxBackendRegistry`'s resolved backend to `SandboxToolProvider`'s construction (traced to a structural reason it doesn't exist today — `check_sandbox_profile_availability()` discards the resolved backend, keeping only pass/fail — building one is not recommended speculatively); `wasm`'s tool-bridge path; and no host application yet actually composes `SandboxToolProvider` into a real, production `AgentSession` — only the test file does. Narrows, does not close, ADR-080's own "selection without consumption" residual. Full record: `docs/planning/session-sandbox-lifecycle-wiring-design-draft.md` (six revisions). |
| [097](ADR-097-agent-session-standing-effects-and-trust-helpers-extraction.md) | Which of `rt::AgentSession`'s ~ten responsibility clusters can be safely extracted into real collaborators, and which can't? | **Proposed — design (3 red-team rounds) and implementation both complete (2026-08-25), awaiting `Judged` sign-off.** A full read of the real code (not a line-count summary) found almost every "big" method touches almost all class state, and the interaction-resolution/`run_rounds()` cluster's call graph is a DAG (a red-team pass corrected an earlier "mutually recursive" overclaim) but still not worth extracting given `run_rounds()`'s own bug history and the ~15-parameter surface a free-function split would need. Three clusters WERE genuinely disjoint and got extracted for real: `StandingEffectRegistry` (`include/agentengine/rt/standing_effect_registry.hpp`) now owns standing-effect storage, the background-completion queue, and `schedule_wakeup_impl()`'s capability enforcement, with `AgentSession`'s six public methods in this area reduced to thin wrappers (same locking/authority/emission behavior, ADR-061 §20.5/§24.2 unchanged); I3 trust helpers (`force_tainted`/`filter_cross_provider_reasoning`) and the streaming-response drain loop moved to free functions (`include/agentengine/rt/agent_session_trust.hpp`) taking an `EmitFn` callback in place of a direct `emit_run_event()` call. Two independent red-team lenses (correctness, security) each independently found the same real, honestly-disclosed behavioral delta: batching `drain_ready()`'s erase-then-emit (vs. today's interleaved per-item shape) widens an already-accepted unlocked-reader race window from ~zero to up to N-1 iterations — judged acceptable as a bounded widening of an existing race class, not a new hazard. Verification: full project rebuild (240/240) and full `ctest` suite (252/252) green, **zero test files modified** — every public signature and observable behavior is unchanged. `agent_session.hpp` is ~170 lines smaller (3055→2884); the real win is that the standing-effects cluster and the I3 trust logic are now independently testable outside a full `AgentSession` fixture, not the line count. Full record: `docs/planning/agent-session-decomposition-design-draft.md` (two revisions, three red-team rounds). |
| [098](ADR-098-default-sandbox-backend-registry-wiring.md) | Should the four real `SandboxBackend` conformers get a real, tested factory that registers them into a `SandboxBackendRegistry`? | **Proposed — design (3 red-team rounds) and implementation both complete (2026-08-25), awaiting `Judged` sign-off.** `SandboxBackendRegistry` itself was already real, tested infrastructure, but grepping the whole tree found `register_backend()` was called from nowhere except test files. This ADR closes that half with `build_default_sandbox_registry()` (`src/sandbox/default_sandbox_registry.hpp`/`.cpp`, new `agentengine_default_sandbox_registry` CMake target): registers native-jail (Windows/Linux, always, `strict_eligible`), wasm (only when `AGENTENGINE_WITH_WASM` was ON at configure time, `strict_eligible`, with a compile-time `static_assert` enforcing it can never outrank native-jail's strength), and kata (only when `AGENTENGINE_BUILD_KATA_BACKEND` was ON, via `register_hardware_isolation_backend()` — not a manual `named_only` argument, closing a real landmine the security red-team caught). A real constraint the naive version gets wrong: the four backends are separate opt-in CMake targets (wasm/kata both OFF by default, wasm pulling a real network-fetched dependency), not just platform-split, so this needed its own conditionally-linked target with new `AGENTENGINE_HAVE_WASM_BACKEND`/`AGENTENGINE_HAVE_KATA_BACKEND` feature-detection macros — the first of their kind in this codebase. Three independent red-team rounds (correctness/CMake, security I2/I3, scope/overclaim) preceded implementation; real findings folded in: a `WasmBackend` namespace-qualification bug that would not have compiled, the `register_hardware_isolation_backend()` fix above, and a missing Windows `wasmtime.dll` `PATH` test-property. One self-caught correction during implementation: the red-team's own "make the feature macros `PRIVATE`" suggestion turned out wrong once the real test needed to see them too — reverted to `PUBLIC`.
