Verified two ways on this session's real environment: full default-config build+`ctest` (253/253, `AGENTENGINE_WITH_WASM=OFF`) and, per explicit project-owner go-ahead, a full `-DAGENTENGINE_WITH_WASM=ON` pass with a real network fetch of the pinned wasmtime release (256/256), confirming the wasm branch actually compiles and behaves as designed rather than only by code inspection; build config reverted to the project default afterward. Also fixed as a byproduct: `naming_lint.py` surfaced that ADR-097's own already-committed `EmitFn`/`DrainedCompletion`/`StandingEffectRegistry` were missing required suppression comments — added. Kata's CMake wiring stays code-inspection-only, not build-verified (no Linux+Kata environment available), named plainly as "compiled by no one this pass," not equivalent to verified. Explicitly, honestly out of scope, not attempted: **no production caller exists** — grepping the whole tree found exactly one non-test `register_agent<A>()` call site anywhere (`tools/policy_reachability_fixture.hpp`, a CI dev tool); `tools/cli_chat.cpp`, the closest thing to a real host in this tree, never touches `Agent<>`/`register_agent<A>()` at all. This ADR makes backend *selection* real and tested; it does not make backend *consumption* real for any execution path — narrows, does not close, the same "selection without consumption" residual ADR-080/ADR-097 both already named. Full record: `docs/planning/default-sandbox-registry-wiring-design-draft.md` (two revisions, three red-team rounds). |
| [099](ADR-099-identity-native-sandbox-worktree-capability-model.md) | Does the mandatory session/sandbox/checkpoint stack get a clean-slate identity-native `Grant<T>`/`IdentityAuthority`/`Ledger`/`SandboxSession` model, or stay built on `CapabilitySet`/`AppendLogStore`/`WorktreeObjectStore`? | **Proposed — design accepted; no production code merged.** Design A (`mandatory-session-worktree-design.md`, retrofit onto `CapabilitySet`/`AppendLogStore`) was rejected after four revisions and four independent red-team rounds each finding a genuine structural gap (no identity field on `CapabilitySet` grants, a closed-variant extension tax, a `fork_from()` sync/`SpawnCostBudget` async mismatch, an ownership-shape conflict). Design B (`identity-native-sandbox-worktree-design.md`, accepted) is a clean-slate `Grant<Payload>`/`IdentityAuthority`/one coroutine-native `AsyncQuota<T>`/`Ledger<Store>` stack, identity-scoped from construction, backed by an unusually deep prove phase (§20–§35) including two independent adversarial code-review passes that each found and fixed real bugs (two silently-diverging `Ledger` implementations; 10 further findings including a silently no-op'd spender-identity check, a merge-branch leak, a rollback path-traversal gap, and a Docker-probe shell-injection vulnerability — all fixed and re-verified live). Every primitive exists only as standalone, compiler-verified C++23 under `docs/planning/proofs/`, never linked into `include/agentengine/`. Explicitly deferred to future ADRs of their own: **A3** (concrete execution-surface technology) and **A9** (real `AgentSession`/`ContextProvider`/`Tool<>` integration) — per explicit project-owner direction, both get designed fresh on this design's own primitives, not designed around reusing the three real, already-shipped, uncoordinated mechanisms already occupying this space (`SandboxToolProvider`/ADR-096, `CodeActRunnerBinding`/ADR-030, the zero-consumer `SandboxBackendRegistry`/ADR-080/098); reuse-vs-replace against those is an implementation-time decision, named here for the future implementer, not a design-time obligation. **Update**: A3 now has real progress (design record §36) — a fresh `ExecutionSurface` concept + `DockerExecutionSurface` + `SandboxRuntime::run()`, proven live end-to-end against a real Docker daemon through three rounds of independent adversarial review (each round found and fixed real bugs — a quota-bypass letting commands run for free, container move-semantics leaks, a reintroduced ACL-batch-persist bug — converging clean on round 3); still not wired to any real `SandboxBackend`/`AgentSession`, and whether the concept generalizes past Docker is unverified. **A9 also now has real progress (§37)**: `MandatorySandboxProvider`, composed as `AgentSession`'s real `HistoryProviderT`, makes the mandatory sandbox binding work against the REAL, unmodified `fork_from()`/`clear_in_process_state()` statements — round 1 of adversarial review found the initial design structurally unsound (a shared mutable "prepared fork" slot any incidental copy could corrupt, via `AgentSession::history_provider()`'s real mutable accessor) and it was fully redesigned (every copy-assignment now self-contained, no shared mutable state); round 2 found and closed one new gap the redesign introduced (a self-copy failure path that could wipe an already-bound session). Proven live against Docker, 9 checks. Still NOT established: §1 item 2's stronger "a session with no execution capability still owns a branch" requirement (the current mechanism's unbound state owns no branch at all — named, not solved) and generalization beyond the one real conformer. Residual, stated plainly: neither this ADR nor ADR-096/098 has been Judged yet, so two independent, unjudged, security-critical sandbox designs currently coexist in this repository with no ADR reconciling them. **A10 (§39, 2026-08-27)**: a real-world-use-case research pass (separate from the earlier attack-focused external validation) found this design's biggest gap wasn't a security defect — every actively-developed coding agent surveyed (Claude Code, Cursor, GitHub Copilot's coding agent) ships git-worktree-per-task isolation as its primary mechanism, and this design had the primitives proven but zero agent-callable path to them. `TaskBranchSandbox` (start/run/commit/discard) closes it at the same standalone-prove-phase bar as everything else here: three independent red-team rounds, one fatal finding (an unsynchronized handle table, corroborated by all three) fixed, 13/13 checks against live Docker, zero regressions across every other consumer of the shared `SandboxRuntime` primitive it extended. Still no production wiring, no capability-decl design, not Judged. **Update, §40 (2026-08-27)**: a fresh reconciliation pass tracing A8's ACL-cap call sites against A10's own calling pattern found A8's cap was a PERMANENT, non-evictable ceiling with no escape hatch (worse than its original "not tuned" framing) and closed it for real — the cap is now a per-instance constructor parameter, plus a real, principal-gated `mark_digest_shared()` escape hatch that exempts genuinely shared content from the cap entirely, not just from the denial. Also closed A10's own remaining disclosed gap: a rejected `commit_task_branch()` no longer strands the caller's handle at the lower-level A7 API — it now reclaims and re-surfaces the branch under the same `handle_id` automatically. Both fixes independently red-teamed (security/I2-I3/concurrency for A8, correctness/concurrency for A10) with zero fatal findings; one dormant, disclosed residual (`mark_digest_shared()` lacks `HostSandboxSelection`-style I3 defense-in-depth, moot with no production caller). Full regression across all 16 affected probes, zero regressions. **Update, §41 (2026-08-27)**: A10's own capability-decl gap (finding 4) is also closed, at the design level — `cap::decl::TaskBranch`/`TaskBranchCommit` (`docs/planning/proofs/task_branch_tool/task_branch_capability.hpp`), a real, two-tag declaration mirroring production `cap::decl::*` exactly, deliberately kept in prove-phase code rather than extending the real, closed `Capability` variant for a feature with no real caller yet. Proven for real including an honestly-diagnosed negative result: driving these tags through a real `Tool<>`'s `declared_capabilities()` does not compile today, for two precise reasons (one self-inflicted-but-fixable ADL/namespace gap, one genuinely unavoidable without production-code changes — the closed `Capability` variant has no `TaskBranch` alternative). Independently red-teamed, zero fatal findings, two wording corrections folded in. **Update, §41.1 (2026-08-28)**: a second round proved the two-tag GATING BEHAVIOR itself, not just the tags' types — `task_branch_capability_enforcement.hpp`/`probe_task_branch_capability_enforcement.cpp` mirror the real `CapabilitySet::contains()`/`bind()`/`subsumes()` and the real `tool_pipeline.hpp` step-4/7 loop over a local two-alternative variant, 12/12 checks pass, including the "commit-only grant is genuinely inert" claim now executed rather than asserted. Independently red-teamed again, zero fatal findings; two real, pre-existing wording/citation errors in the original `task_branch_capability.hpp` (a wrong `cap::NetListen` fieldless-precedent citation, a wrong function attribution for `Background<MaxConcurrent>`'s live-count check) found and fixed. **Update, §36.5 (2026-08-27)**: per explicit user direction, designed (not built) a fresh, OCI-standard second `ExecutionSurface` conformer — `ctr`/containerd-based, bind-mount instead of Docker's copy-in/copy-out, reusing this codebase's own real `KataBackend` OCI experience rather than Docker-CLI-specific mechanics. One design-only red-team round found a real ordering question against `SandboxRuntime::run()`'s fixed step sequence — **empirically proven benign same day**: `containerd`+`runc` provisioned into WSL2, a real checked-in probe (`docs/planning/proofs/execution_surface/probe_bind_mount_ordering_hazard.sh`) confirmed the hazard for real (no corruption, no leakage, clean orphaning) against a live containerd 2.2.2/runc 1.4.0 deployment. **Update, §42/§43 (2026-08-28)**: four more punch-list items closed in parallel, each independently red-teamed, no fatal findings. §42: `SandboxRuntime`/`MandatorySandboxProvider` gained a real, quota-gated `reset_to_turn()` (closing the design's own disclosed "no rollback method" gap) — a red-team round found the first version had no `AsyncQuota` at all (a real I8 resource-exhaustion gap), fixed with `AsyncQuota<ResetCost>`; 12 checks against live Docker across both layers. §43.1: A9 now proven against the REAL `agentengine::rt::AgentSession` (not `FakeAgentSession`), 10/10 checks. §43.2: that pass found a real, previously-undetected `Ledger::create_root_branch()` naming collision (same-owner root branches silently overwrote each other) — fixed with a backward-compatible optional `disambiguator`, proven not to disturb the real crash-recovery reattachment mechanism it was in tension with (all 4 durability probes re-verified unchanged), 16-file compile sweep clean. §43.3: A10's two-tag gating logic (§41.1) now proven through a real dispatch pipeline — a rejected commit call is structurally unable to reach the mutating verb at all, 6 checks live; still narrows, does not close, the real-`Tool<>` gap. §43.4: `ContainerdExecutionSurface` — the second conformer designed above — now has real, standalone C++ (16 checks against live containerd/runc), with a real design correction folded back into the draft (C3's "`reject_chars()` reused verbatim" claim does not transfer to a `posix_spawn`+argv-only path); not yet integrated with the real `Ledger`/`SandboxRuntime` stack. Full record `docs/planning/oci-execution-surface-design-draft.md`. |

| [100](ADR-100-adr-096-098-sandbox-layering-reconciliation.md) | Does ADR-096 (`SandboxToolProvider`) actually conflict with ADR-098 (`build_default_sandbox_registry()`), and if not, what real gap does reconciling them surface? | **Proposed — design complete, three independent red-team rounds complete (2026-08-27); the immediate mitigation this pass surfaced is built and proven the same day.** Per explicit project-owner direction to reconcile these two before further implementation in this space. **The first draft got the causal story wrong**, claiming Python reaches its real OS-level jail (`NativeJailBackend::create_python_worker()`) via `SandboxBackendRegistry`, and proposing `SandboxToolProvider` become a registry consumer the same way — the architecture-fit red-team round found this false against the real code: `MediatedPythonRunner` reaches `NativeJailBackend` via a plain constructor-injected `NativeJailBackend&`, never the registry, and `ADR-080` §4/§7 already states (as "Finding O," written before ADR-096/098 existed) that both Python and Shell bypass `SandboxBackendRegistry` entirely. **Corrected finding: there is no bridge to build between ADR-096 and ADR-098 — `ADR-080` already reconciled them.** What the pass actually surfaced, worth more than the reconciliation itself: `run_shell` (ADR-096's `SandboxToolProvider`/`MediatedShellRunner`) has zero OS-level containment where `execute_code` has one, reached by Python via direct dependency, never the registry; and a security-lens red-team round independently found a live, present-day, unmitigated wall-clock/iteration DoS gap in the mediated-shell evaluation loop (`mediated_shell_grammar.hpp` bounds size/tokens/nesting but not loop iteration or wall-clock time — a 20-level-nested loop with 3 items per level is 3^20 ≈ 3.49 billion body executions from well under 1,000 tokens), reachable via ordinary model-supplied `run_shell` tool-call content — `RunShellTool`'s `FsRead`/`FsWrite` capabilities bound path scope only and do not offset it. **Closed same-day**: `evaluate_statement()` (`mediated_shell_dispatch.{hpp,cpp}`) now checks a real `std::chrono::steady_clock` deadline once per statement — the one funnel every statement, top-level or nested-loop-body, passes through — and `MediatedShellRunner` gained a `wall_clock_budget` constructor parameter (default 10s, provisional, matching `output_discipline.hpp`'s own disclosed-not-final posture); on expiry the internal fail-fast error is translated into a real `ExecOutcome{klass: timeout}`, matching exactly how `NativeJailBackend`'s own watchdog classifies a wall-clock kill. Proven by `tests/test_mediated_shell_runner_wall_clock_timeout.cpp`: a positive control (no false positives) plus the real 3^20-body-execution exponential-blowup script, killed within a measured, bounded real wall-clock time under a 20ms/50ms budget. Full regression: 11 pre-existing shell/sandbox tests 100% green, zero regressions. **Still deferred, its own future design→red-team→prove→judge cycle**: a `create_shell_worker()`/`exec_shell_session()` pair on `NativeJailBackend` mirroring `create_python_worker()`/`exec_session()`'s real shape, taking a direct `NativeJailBackend&` (matching the one real precedent) rather than the registry's type-erased `create`/`exec`/`destroy` trio, which structurally cannot carry it — this is what would close Shell's remaining OS-level-containment gap (no AppContainer/Job Object at all); the wall-clock fix closes only the specific unbounded-loop DoS shape. Narrows ADR-099 §8's "three uncoordinated mechanisms" residual by exactly the ADR-096↔ADR-098 relationship; `CodeActRunnerBinding` (ADR-030) and the `Grant<T>`/`Ledger` relationship remain untouched. Full record: `docs/planning/adr-096-098-sandbox-layering-reconciliation-design-draft.md` (two revisions — the fatal first-draft error is kept on the record, not silently replaced, since the correction is itself the load-bearing finding). |

| [101](ADR-101-sandbox-backend-tree-refinement-reconciliation.md) | Does the real, production `SandboxBackend` concept need widening to carry tree-materialization verbs, or does a refined, additive concept — reconciled with `ExecutionSurface`/`SandboxRuntime` via a generic adapter — close the gap without touching the locked contract? | **Proposed — design accepted; six independent red-team/verification rounds complete (2026-08-28), all against real, compiled, live-Docker-run code; no production code merged.** Rejects widening `SandboxBackend` itself (would fragment three already-shipped conformers); accepts `TreeCapableSandboxBackend<T>` = the real, unmodified `SandboxBackend<T>` **+** additive, capability-gated, handle-scoped `reset`/`drain_to`/`try_destroy`, one real conformer `DockerSandboxBackend` wrapping the already-proven `probe::DockerBackend`, connected to the real production `SandboxBackendRegistry` via `register_hardware_isolation_backend()` (`named_only` — structurally excluded from `Strict` resolution regardless of declared strength). An architecture-fit red-team round found this alone created a THIRD, uncoordinated tree-materialization vocabulary alongside `SandboxBackend`/`ExecutionSurface` — closed by `TreeBackendExecutionSurface<Backend>`, a generic adapter making ANY `TreeCapableSandboxBackend` conformer a real `ExecutionSurface`, proven live end-to-end through the actual, Ledger-integrated `SandboxRuntime::run()` (mirroring `probe_execution_surface.cpp`'s own two-turn persistence-proving shape). **Three separate self-inflicted regressions across this one ADR's own fix rounds, each caught only by a genuinely independent verification round, not the same reasoning that produced the fix**: (1) the first capability-gate fix for `reset()`/`drain_to()` (originally reachable with NO authorization at all) itself introduced a path-traversal bypass, fixed by reusing `authorize_spec()`'s own `has_dot_or_dotdot_component()` guard, re-verified against 12 adversarial path strings; (2) the reconciliation adapter's `reset()` silently orphaned a real container on every transient `destroy()` failure after the first turn (`SandboxBackend::destroy()`'s locked `void` return gives no way to know if cleanup succeeded) — fixed by adding `try_destroy()`, a checkable twin now required by the concept itself; (3) the first fix for a klass-fidelity bug (a non-ok `exec()` outcome — timeout/oom/crash/policy_violation/escape_attempt/ask_pending — silently reported as fabricated `exit_code=0` success) made `run()` return a `result<>` error instead, which `SandboxRuntime::run()` treats as "nothing was attempted" and refunds `run_quota` for — reopening the exact "run for free" bug class `RunCost` exists to prevent; corrected to return a value (`exit_code=-1` sentinel, real class in `stdout_text`), proven live with a synthetic `FakeCrashingBackend` conformer confirming `run_quota` is consumed (97→96), not refunded, for a real crash outcome. Cumulative real, live evidence across the three checked-in probe files: 67 passing checks against a real, running Docker Desktop daemon. Explicitly out of scope: no production wiring into `build_default_sandbox_registry()`; `DockerExecutionSurface`/`DockerSandboxBackend` remain independent, undeduplicated Docker wrappers (this ADR unifies the CONSUMPTION path, not the two conformers' own implementations); `SandboxRuntime`'s own unconditional-commit-on-non-ok-klass behavior (an already-shipped, unexamined gap this design's own positive control surfaced, not caused); the `exit_code=-1` sentinel's cross-conformer ambiguity with `DockerExecutionSurface`'s own pre-existing "never launched" use of the same value (not currently exploitable, no fully-generic caller exists yet); a second `TreeCapableSandboxBackend` conformer (containerd-shaped or otherwise) to confirm the refinement itself generalizes past Docker. |

| [102](ADR-102-identity-native-sandbox-implementation-phase-1.md) | Do the identity-native sandbox/worktree design's (ADR-099) core primitives — `IdentityAuthority`/`Grant<T>`/`AsyncQuota<T>` (Phase 1), `Ledger<Store>`/`BranchHandle`/`Checkpoint` (Phase 2), `ExecutionSurface`/`SandboxRuntime`/`DockerExecutionSurface` (Phase 3), `MandatorySandboxProvider`/`RunCommandTool` wired as a real session's `HistoryProviderT` (Phase 4), then `tools/cli_chat.cpp` wiring (Phase 5) — port cleanly into real production `include/agentengine/`, does the real `agentengine::trust::Principal` name collision get resolved by reuse or a distinct name, and does the first real host integration actually work? | **Proposed — Phases 1-4 plus the `cli_chat.cpp` slice of Phase 5 and closing the `fork_from()` session-serialization gap complete and independently red-teamed (2026-08-28); authorizes exactly that, not the whole design or the rest of Phase 5.** `Principal` collision resolved by a DISTINCT name (`IdentityHandle`, not reuse) — the two answer genuinely different questions (007's per-request identity vs. a durable, ancestor-tracked authority-subject), bridged via a typed `IdentityAuthority::adopt(Principal const&)`. **Phase 1** (`trust/identity_authority.hpp`, `rt/async_quota.hpp`): one independent red-team round found and fixed a real MUST-FIX — `adopt()` keyed its bridging map by `Principal::id` alone, ignoring `tenant_id`, a real 018 §6 cross-tenant-isolation violation (two tenants sharing an `id` string would silently merge into one identity, sharing quota/grants) — fixed with two-level `(tenant_id, id)` keying. **Phase 2** (`core/ledger.hpp`, `Ledger<Store>`'s real checkpoint/branch/merge system built directly on the already-shipped `WorktreeObjectStore`): a faithful, ~700-line mechanical port whose own FIRST correctly-modeled test (using a real `derive_child()` identity-descendant, not a second independent `mint_root()` — the two are separate ancestry relationships entirely, `IdentityAuthority`'s identity ancestry vs. `Ledger`'s own branch ancestry) surfaced a real MUST-FIX no prove-phase testing had ever exercised: after a legitimate sub-agent merges its work into a parent branch, the parent's own creator was permanently locked out of reading its own branch's new head — breaking the "orchestrator spawns a sub-agent, it merges back, the orchestrator resumes" flow this whole design exists to support. A first justifying comment for this exact flow falsely claimed it "matches" the real task-branch tool track (A10) — independently re-checked and found A10 sidesteps the whole scenario by using only one identity throughout, never actually exercising it. Fixed: `merge()` now also grants the parent branch's own creator a real ACL root on the merged tree digest. A second, independent verification round confirmed the fix live via a forced-revert test (temporarily disabling it reproduced exactly the two new checks failing, nothing else) and found one further disclosed-not-fixed residual: the fix is tree-digest-level only, not blob-content-level, for content the child alone wrote. **Phase 3** (`sandbox/{execution_surface,docker_execution_surface,real_io_filesystem,sandbox_runtime}.hpp` — `ExecutionSurface`/`SurfaceRunOutcome`, `DockerCliBackend`/`DockerExecutionSurface`, `RealIoFileSystem`, `SandboxRuntime`/`SandboxRunOutcome`/`RunCost`/`ResetCost`; deliberately NOT a `SandboxBackend` conformer and NOT wired to any real backend, ADR-099 §7's own scope boundary carried forward): a faithful port, compiling clean on the first attempt, of four previously-standalone prove-phase files composed together for the first time in production. One independent red-team round found and fixed a real SHOULD-FIX no prior round (five on the prove-phase original, one each on Phase 1/2) had caught: `merge_into()`/`discard()` mutated `branch_` via `Ledger::merge()`/`abandon()`'s pass-by-value parameter without taking `SandboxRuntime`'s own `exclusivity_` lock first, unlike every sibling method — a real interleaving hazard once a future phase wires a concurrent caller, fixed by adding the lock both places. The same round also caught that `DockerExecutionSurface`'s own leak-residual disclosure overclaimed its trigger as "requires an actual process crash," when in fact any transient `docker rm -f` failure during an ORDINARY (non-crash) destructor call leaks the container just as silently — corrected in place; the underlying leak itself remains a disclosed, unfixed residual. Registering Phase 3's own vocabulary also surfaced and fixed a real, previously-unnoticed LATENT GAP from Phase 2: `027-Vocabulary-and-Naming.md`'s combined "`X` / `Y`" table rows (`MergeResult`/`MergeConflict`, `BranchCost`/`StorageBytes`) were never actually recognized by `tools/naming_lint.py`, whose table-row regex only captures the first backtick name per row — fixed by splitting every combined row in the table, Phase 2's included, into one row per name. **Phase 4** (`sandbox/mandatory_sandbox_provider.hpp` — `MandatorySandboxProvider<Surface>`/`RunCommandTool`, composed for the first time as a REAL, production `agentengine::rt::AgentSession`'s actual `HistoryProviderT`, mirroring `tools/cli_chat.cpp`'s own real, shipped `ToolDeclaringHistoryProvider` pattern, deliberately NOT `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>`, which has zero real production consumers anywhere in this codebase): the port itself was faithful and compiled clean, but an independent red-team pass EMPIRICALLY DISPROVED this port's own central safety argument for skipping the prove-phase original's ASan-hardened `block_on()` driver — the argument ("I1, one session one executor, plus `invoke_tool()`'s sequential dispatch mean nothing here ever contends") was true but incomplete: `AsyncQuota<RunCost>`/`AsyncQuota<BranchCost>`/`AsyncQuota<StorageBytes>` are legitimately SHARED across sibling `SandboxRuntime` instances (an ordinary "one budget for a family of sessions" pattern this phase's own test demonstrates), so two sibling sessions running on genuinely different threads DO genuinely contend on that shared quota's own `AsyncMutex`. A targeted repro against the real, unmodified `AsyncMutex`/`task<T>` reproduced the naive drive loop breaking mutual exclusion 5/5 runs (a genuine, reproducible coroutine-frame use-after-free). Fixed by porting the prove-phase original's own ASan-hardened driver for real, into a NEW shared production primitive, `include/agentengine/rt/block_on.hpp` — re-verified by a second, independent positive-control test (`test_rt_block_on.cpp`) reproducing the identical contention shape against the fix, 5/5 rounds clean. One further SHOULD-FIX disclosed, not fixed: `AgentSession::fork_from()` is itself unguarded by `session_mutex_`, a structurally identical gap to Phase 3's own `merge_into()`/`discard()` finding one layer up — not live today (no real production caller of `fork_from()` exists anywhere in this codebase). Registering Phase 4's own vocabulary surfaced a second instance of Phase 2/3's own combined-table-row lint gap (fixed again) and found the naming-lint tool's own scope comment is stale (its real scope is namespace-based and does cover `rt/`, confirmed the hard way when `BlockOnState` was correctly flagged). **Phase 5** (`tools/cli_chat.cpp` slice only — `run_command` wired into the REAL, actual CLI host for the first time this whole design has ever been reachable by a real user running a real binary): bring-up hit a real MUST-FIX found the hard way — a shell-wrapper-masked build failure (checking a trailing `tail`'s exit code instead of `ninja`'s own) hid a genuine compile error: `Ledger`'s own `MergeConflict`/`MergeResult` (Phase 2) silently collided, byte-for-byte on the fully-qualified name, with an unrelated, already-shipped production type in `core/worktree_merge.hpp` (025 §4's own branch-merge mechanism) — no build across Phases 2-4 had ever `#include`d both headers in one translation unit, so the redefinition stayed latent the whole time; Phase 5's own wiring is what first combined them. Fixed by renaming to `LedgerMergeConflict`/`LedgerMergeResult`. A second, purely mechanical MSVC limit (`C1128`, too many template instantiations for one `.obj`) was fixed with `/bigobj`, scoped to the one target. A real, live smoke test against a genuine OpenRouter-backed model (not a scripted fixture) confirmed `run_command` works end to end — the model correctly discovered and called it, a real Docker container executed a real command, and the result was independently verified from the raw request/response dump, not merely the model's own narration — the first time any part of this four-phase effort has been driven by a genuine, unscripted model. That same smoke test also CONFIRMED (not merely disclosed) a third trigger for Phase 3's already-named Docker container-leak residual: this CLI's own deliberate `std::_Exit(0)` (a pre-existing, well-justified fix for a real CPython thread-affinity crash) skips `DockerExecutionSurface`'s destructor on every ordinary exit, not only a crash — disclosed inline, not fixed. An independent red-team round confirmed the rename is complete (full-tree grep found no other Phase 1-4 name collision), the new wiring's I2/I3 posture is clean, and the lifetime-ordering fix (`cli_ledger`/quotas declared before `actor` so `BranchHandle`'s destructor never dangles on an early-return path) is correct across every path — and, despite mis-attributing its own cause to a nonexistent silent regression (it was actually reading a stale pre-fix binary's dump from the SAME masked build failure above), correctly identified a genuine, real test-coverage gap: the exact "embed `MandatorySandboxProvider` in another `ContextProvider` and merge its contribution outside skill-scoping" composition `cli_chat.cpp` uses had zero automated coverage anywhere. Closed with a new, Docker-independent test, `tests/test_mandatory_sandbox_provider_composed.cpp`. **Closing `AgentSession::fork_from()`'s own session-serialization gap** (the same structural finding Phase 3/4 each disclosed but did not fix): `session_mutex_` made `mutable` and `fork_from()` now acquires `source.session_mutex_` for the whole copy, driven synchronously via `block_on()` so the function's own signature and every call site stay unchanged. Proven with a real two-thread race (`test_rt_agent_session_fork_from_serialization.cpp`) whose status as a genuine positive control was empirically verified via a forced-revert (fails 3/3 without the fix, passes with it). A dedicated independent red-team round — warranted given this touches the single most heavily-used class in the codebase — found and disclosed (not fixed) a real, empirically-confirmed new hazard the fix itself introduces: calling `fork_from()` reentrantly, from code already holding `session_mutex_` on the same thread (e.g. a future `agent.spawn`-style tool closure), now self-deadlocks instead of the impossible-before-this-fix "cannot deadlock at all" — confirmed with a 100%-reproducible repro, not reachable through any real call site today. A real process incident also occurred during this same work and is disclosed for the record: an independent red-team subagent's own cleanup step reverted `tests/CMakeLists.txt` via git rather than undoing only its own scratch additions, silently discarding seven of this session's own legitimate test registrations — caught via a test-count drop (286→279), fully recovered with no test source content lost, full suite re-confirmed green. Real evidence across all five phases plus the fork_from() fix: full project rebuilds (306/306 targets including `agentengine_cli_chat`, 378/378 including the full test suite) and full `ctest` suites (286/286) green across twelve independent runs, zero regressions (`test_native_jail_backend_windows` flaked twice and `test_rt_workflow_supervisor_merge_on_join`/`test_rt_spawn_cost_budget` each flaked once under `-j4` parallel execution across the whole effort — all confirmed passing in isolation and on a clean full re-run every time); seven new real tests (`test_identity_authority_grant` 14 checks, `test_ledger` 47 checks, `test_sandbox_runtime` 10 checks, `test_mandatory_sandbox_provider` 6 check groups, `test_rt_block_on` 3 checks, `test_mandatory_sandbox_provider_composed` 3 check groups, `test_rt_agent_session_fork_from_serialization` 2 check groups — three of the seven requiring and exercising a REAL Docker daemon) plus two new `try_compile()` negative probes, all passing. Explicitly out of scope, named for later work: `ContainerdExecutionSurface`/ADR-101 promotion, Windows/Linux parity, and adding real reentrancy protection to `fork_from()`/`AsyncMutex` before any real caller can reach the newly-disclosed self-deadlock hazard. **Second Phase 5 slice (2026-08-28): `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>` becomes a real production consumer for the first time anywhere in this codebase.** Before this slice, both `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>` itself and `SandboxToolProvider` (ADR-096, the one real conformer this codebase ships specifically to compose through it) had zero real production callers — every use was a unit test driving `on_context()` directly. New: `tests/test_composed_sandbox_providers_live.cpp` proves `ComposedContextProvider<SandboxToolProvider, MandatorySandboxProvider<DockerExecutionSurface>>` works as a real `AgentSession`'s `HistoryProviderT`, driven through a real `start_run()` round for the first time ever, executing both a native-jail `run_shell` call and a Docker-backed `run_command` call in the SAME composed session (15 checks, real Docker daemon); `tools/sandboxed_shell_chat.cpp` is a new, small, real CLI binary — the first user-reachable host wiring of `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>` anywhere in this codebase — reusing the already-shipped, previously-uncalled `ComposedQuickstartSessionBuilder`/`Bundle::ask()` machinery rather than hand-rolling a new REPL driver, and exiting via ordinary `return 0;` (not `cli_chat.cpp`'s `std::_Exit(0)`) so `DockerExecutionSurface` reclaims its container on every ordinary exit. An independent red-team round found NO MUST-FIX after a real build, a real run against a live Docker daemon, AND a real clang-ASan-instrumented run of the same test (the strongest available check for the "bind `MandatorySandboxProvider` before composing it — there is no accessor back to the concrete instance once `ComposedContextProvider::engage()` type-erases it" lifetime claim this whole slice's design rests on) — two real SHOULD-FIX disclosure gaps were found and fixed same day: the CLI tool's own container-leak-closed claim overreached (said nothing about Ctrl+C, which this whole codebase has no `SIGINT` handler for anywhere, so it bypasses destructors identically to `std::_Exit(0)`) and a `Principal` with an empty `tenant_id` had no comment warning against reuse in a real multi-tenant host (Phase 1's own central fix was specifically about tenant-scoped identity collisions). Full suite: 287/287 passing, zero regressions, the first fully flake-free run in this whole multi-phase effort's history. **Third Phase 5 slice (2026-08-28): ADR-096 C2's `fork_from`-becomes-compile-error claim becomes a durable, checked-in regression guard.** ADR-096 §6 had recorded this claim "empirically proven" via a throwaway MSVC probe compiled once, by hand, during that ADR's own red-team round, then discarded — no CI signal would have caught a future regression. New: `tests/compile_fail/sandbox_tool_provider_rejects_fork_from.cpp` (composes the real production `SandboxToolProvider` via `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>` and calls `fork_from()` — MUST NOT compile) plus a byte-identical positive control minus that call, wired into `tests/CMakeLists.txt` via this repo's own established `try_compile()` idiom (an 8th such pair). Real MSVC reconfigure confirmed both: the negative probe fails with `error C2280` at the exact `history_provider_ = source.history_provider_;` statement inside `fork_from()`, and the positive control compiles and links. An independent red-team round did real, executed verification (not just static reading) — including reproducing the error directly via `cl.exe` and, most valuably, temporarily flipping `ComposedContextProvider`'s copy-assignment from `= delete` to `= default` and confirming the gate then goes green for the wrong reason, proving it is genuinely load-bearing (then reverted) — and found two real SHOULD-FIX issues, both fixed same day: (1) the new files' and CMake block's comments wrongly attributed the compile failure to `SandboxToolProvider`'s own non-copyability, when `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>`'s copy-assignment is actually deleted unconditionally for ANY `Ms` (live-reproduced with a fully copyable dummy provider hitting the identical error) — reworded; (2) a real, previously-undisclosed bypass: `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>`'s move-assignment is not deleted, so `target.history_provider() = std::move(source.history_provider());` still compiles and still aliases live provider state across sessions through the public accessor — the exact hazard shape `core/session_builder.hpp`'s sibling `LazyComposedContextProvider` already disclosed for itself (findings 9/11) but that this plain `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>` had never carried the same disclosure for, despite ADR-074 making it move-only too. Fixed as a disclosure (matching the sibling type's own precedent), not a code change: `composed_context_provider.hpp` and both new test files now name it explicitly, with real follow-on work named (a non-assignable accessor shape, or an owning-session-identity check) but not attempted. Full rebuild clean and full `ctest` suite 287/287 passing, zero regressions (this slice adds zero new `ctest` targets by design — `try_compile()` checks run at configure time). |

| [103](ADR-103-sandbox-tool-provider-linux-parity.md) | Does `SandboxToolProvider`'s `MediatedFileSystemAdapter` (ADR-096, Windows-only since it shipped) port cleanly to Linux, reusing the already-Judged `open_within_mount_root` (ADR-014) containment primitive, without reopening the class of TOCTOU/mount-escape bug that primitive exists to close? | **Proposed — implemented and independently red-teamed (2026-08-28), real builds and real test runs on both platforms, including a genuine WSL2 Ubuntu Linux environment — the first time this repo's `SandboxToolProvider` chain has ever actually been built or run on Linux.** Scoping research found the real Windows-specific surface was narrower than "the whole native_jail chain": only `mediated_filesystem_adapter.cpp` has real Win32 API calls; the shell parser/dispatch/command-registry layer was already portable, and the hard part — path containment — was already done and Judged (`worktree_mount_fs_posix.hpp`, ADR-014 Phase C4). New: `mediated_filesystem_adapter_posix.cpp`, implementing all 10 `FileSystemAdapter` methods against that already-Judged primitive; `MediatedFileSystemAdapter`'s declaration became portable (`std::wstring root_` → `std::filesystem::path`), same one-declaration/two-`.cpp` pattern `compute_digest()` already uses; CMake `NOT WIN32` branch for `agentengine_mediated_shell_runner`; six test targets un-gated from `if(WIN32)` and made portable. `tests/test_mediated_shell_runner_hostile_corpus.cpp`'s SH7-SH10 block — a real, previously-shipped CRITICAL Windows security fix for a backslash-based mount-escape bug — needed genuinely different, both-real, platform-adapted expected outcomes (`#ifdef _WIN32`/`#else`), since the attack's premise is Windows-specific by construction (`\` has no separator meaning on POSIX). An independent red-team round, given real build/shell access on both platforms, compiled and ran real standalone repros against the actual built libraries rather than trusting comments, and found one real MUST-FIX: `usage()`'s original `accumulate_usage()` recursed one C++ stack frame per directory-tree level while holding two fds open per level for the whole recursive call — a real, guest-triggerable crash (`usage()` runs on every quota-capped `mkdir`/`cp`, and nothing bounds guest-created nesting depth), reproduced via a real 50,000-level-deep tree: `EMFILE` under default `ulimit -n`, a **segfault, twice, reproducibly**, under a raised one — exactly the shape `worktree_mount_fs.cpp::mount_root_usage()` (the Windows sibling this file claims to mirror) already documents avoiding via an explicit stack, not recursion. Fixed by converting to an explicit stack of mount-relative path strings, each re-verified via `open_within_mount_root` only when popped — at most one open directory handle at any time, matching the Windows sibling's own invariant, bounded by tree BREADTH not nesting depth — then independently re-verified via the identical 50,000-level-deep repro (clean `ENAMETOOLONG`, no crash) and a 500-level-deep repro (correct, successful `file_count`/`total_bytes`). Two further SHOULD-FIX platform-parity gaps found and fixed same day: `exists()`'s and `make_directory(parents=true)`'s error classification only recognized `ENOENT`, narrower than the Windows original's broader catch-all, empirically confirmed to diverge (querying through an intermediate regular-file component returns `ENOTDIR` on Linux, propagated as a hard error instead of the Windows-matching `false`) — both broadened to match. A real, pre-existing, unrelated gap was discovered as a byproduct and disclosed, not fixed: `include/agentengine/sandbox/real_io_filesystem.hpp` (ADR-102 Phases 3-4's `RealIoFileSystem`/`SandboxRuntime`) unconditionally `#include`s the Windows-only `worktree_mount_fs.hpp`, so `test_sandbox_runtime`/`test_mandatory_sandbox_provider`/`test_mandatory_sandbox_provider_composed` fail to even compile on Linux — never Linux-portable despite not being `WIN32`-gated in CMake, simply never discovered because nobody had attempted a real Linux build before. `remove(recursive=true)`'s own identical recursion-depth hazard, shared unchanged with the pre-existing Windows implementation, is disclosed but not fixed (real, contained, but genuinely separate follow-on work touching the already-shipped Windows file too). Real evidence: a real WSL2 build (six ported targets compile clean, pass 6/6, three separate runs); a full Linux project build via `ninja -k 0` surfacing exactly the three pre-existing, unrelated `real_io_filesystem.hpp` failures and nothing else; a full Linux `ctest` run excluding those three, 170/170 passing, zero regressions, run three times; a full Windows rebuild and the same six tests passing 6/6 there too, confirmed after every round of fixes. Explicitly out of scope, named for later work: `ContainerdExecutionSurface`/ADR-101 promotion; Docker-dependent consumers (`tools/sandboxed_shell_chat.cpp`, `test_composed_sandbox_providers_live`, the ADR-096 C2 compile-fail probes) untested on Linux (no Docker daemon reachable in this session's WSL2 environment); `test_mediated_shell_runner_no_process_creation`'s own Linux symbol list; the embedded-CPython Python-runner tests; and the newly-discovered `RealIoFileSystem` Linux-portability gap itself. |

| [104](ADR-104-real-io-filesystem-linux-parity.md) | Does `RealIoFileSystem`/`DockerExecutionSurface` (ADR-102 Phase 3) port cleanly to Linux — the gap ADR-103 §7 disclosed but deliberately left out of scope — without opening a POSIX-specific host command-injection hole in the process? | **Proposed — implemented and independently red-teamed (2026-08-29), real builds and real test runs on both platforms.** Attempting the mechanical fix (split `write_verified()`/`read_verified()` per platform, following ADR-103's own portable-declaration/two-`.cpp` pattern, reusing `open_within_mount_root`'s already-Judged POSIX half) surfaced a SECOND, un-predicted blocker: `docker_execution_surface.hpp`'s `run_capture()` calls Windows-CRT-only `_popen`/`_pclose` directly. Ported in place (`#ifdef _WIN32`/`#else`) to POSIX `popen`/`pclose` — but `popen` on Linux invokes `/bin/sh`, a structurally different shell from `cmd.exe`, so reusing the existing Windows character-blacklist verbatim would have been a silent command-injection regression, not a parity win. Two NEW, POSIX-specific defenses were written instead, keyed to how each value is actually embedded in the generated string: a POSITIVE ALLOWLIST (alnum + `. _ - / : @`, matching Docker's own reference-name grammar) for `image`/`host_path`/`container_path` (all interpolated UNQUOTED into the sh command line, where almost every non-alphanumeric character is meaningful); a narrow blacklist of exactly `` " $ ` \ \r \n `` for `command` (embedded inside escaped double quotes, where only those four characters retain POSIX shell meaning) — deliberately still permissive of `&|<>(){}`, which must reach the CONTAINER's own inner `sh`, preserving the Windows version's original intent. An independent red-team round, given real build/shell access on both platforms and the same "empirically prove, don't just read" brief as ADR-103's own round, found two real, EMPIRICALLY PROVEN issues, both pre-existing on Windows too (not introduced by this port): (1) neither the Windows blacklist nor the new POSIX allowlist anchored "must not start with `-`," so an all-safe-character value like `--privileged` passed cleanly and landed as a bare, unquoted token exactly where the real `docker` CLI parses its OWN flags — CWE-88 argument injection, proven against both the real `docker.exe` CLI (flag-parsing proceeds past arg-parsing to a daemon-dial error for `--privileged`, vs. immediate `unknown flag` for a bogus one) and the real, compiled `DockerCliBackend::create()` with a PATH-shimmed `docker` logging its own `argv`; not live-reachable today (the one production caller, `MandatorySandboxProvider::bind_sandbox()`, always hardcodes `image`/`container_path` and derives `host_path` from `std::filesystem::temp_directory_path()`), fixed anyway on both platforms via a new, shared `docker_cli_reject_leading_dash()` (rejects a leading `-` specifically, not "must start with alnum" — the first version of the fix was caught locally, before the red-team, breaking the real, legitimate `/workspace`-shaped `container_path`/`host_path` values that also flow through the same function); (2) `command`'s blacklist never rejected an embedded NUL byte, which silently truncates at the eventual `.c_str()` call into `popen` — proven, via a compiled repro, NOT exploitable for RCE against the one real caller (a NUL always lands inside the still-open `sh -c "..."` region, so the truncated string always has an unterminated quote and `dash` fails closed with a syntax error) but fixed anyway as defense-in-depth, once, in the shared `docker_cli_reject_chars()` helper for every caller on both platforms. Both fixes re-verified via a standalone repro (25/25 checks, up from 20/20 pre-fix) including explicit regression checks that `/workspace`/`alpine:latest` still pass. Real evidence: full Windows rebuild (287/287 targets) and full `ctest` (284/287 — the unchanged 3 pre-existing "REQUIRES a running Docker daemon" tests, confirmed via `docker version` that the daemon is genuinely unreachable on this host) both before and after the fixes, zero regression; full WSL2 Ubuntu rebuild via `ninja -k 0` (zero `error:`/`FAILED:` lines — the first time `test_sandbox_runtime`/`test_mandatory_sandbox_provider`/`test_mandatory_sandbox_provider_composed` have ever compiled on Linux) and full `ctest` (171/173, both before and after fixes — the 2 failures traced to their real root cause: `docker` is not on `PATH` in this WSL2 distro at all, so `reset()` fails first and `run()` short-circuits before ever reaching the new guard, a purely environmental cascading failure, not a code defect); two standalone repros compiled and run against the real built libraries (a `RealIoFileSystem` POSIX I/O proof, 12/12 checks including a REAL symlink-escape rejection, not just lexical; the shell-guard proof above, with a positive+negative control pair proving the base defense is both real and actually load-bearing on this platform, not merely believed to be). Explicitly out of scope, named for later work: Docker-daemon-level runtime verification of the newly-compiling tests on Linux (no `docker` binary reachable in this session's WSL2 environment — the same class of gap ADR-103 §7 already disclosed, now additionally true for two more targets); `remove(recursive=true)`'s own recursion-depth hazard (ADR-103 §7, still open, different files); a full character-by-character re-audit of the Windows-side blacklist beyond the one leading-dash finding fixed live in this pass. |

| [105](ADR-105-sandbox-tool-provider-composed-linux-parity.md) | Was `SandboxToolProvider`'s remaining Windows-only build gate (`test_composed_sandbox_providers_live`, the two ADR-096 C2 compile-fail probes, `tools/sandboxed_shell_chat.cpp`) a real platform constraint, or a stale leftover from before ADR-103 achieved parity? | **Proposed — implemented, verified, and independently red-teamed (2026-08-29), real builds and real test runs on both platforms; NO production code changed.** `SandboxToolProvider`'s own file-top comment claimed portability was achieved by ADR-103, but three build-graph items ADR-103 §7 itself named as blocked by "SandboxToolProvider's own current platform scope" were never un-gated to match — investigation confirmed the whole `agentengine::mediated_shell_runner` dependency chain has in fact been portable (real `NOT WIN32` CMake branch) since that pass; the gates were simply never revisited. Empirically un-gated one target at a time against a real WSL2 Ubuntu build with a live Docker daemon: `test_composed_sandbox_providers_live` built, linked, and **passed against real Docker on Linux for the first time**, no code change needed. The two ADR-096 C2 `compile_fail` probes (proving `AgentSession::fork_from()` fails to compile when `SandboxToolProvider` is composed into a session) needed one real fix beyond un-gating: their `try_compile()` dependency list had hardcoded Windows-only sources (`mediated_filesystem_adapter.cpp`, `worktree_digest.cpp`, `worktree_mount_fs.cpp`, linked `bcrypt`) — given a real `NOT WIN32` deps list (the already-existing POSIX twins, no extra link library), with the `try_compile()` calls themselves de-duplicated to run unconditionally. Both now print "ADR-096 C2 compile-fail proof: OK" on a real Linux `cmake` configure. `agentengine_sandboxed_shell_chat` was un-gated the same way first, to test the hypothesis — but failed to LINK for a real, UNRELATED reason: `tools/sandboxed_shell_chat.cpp` pulls in `QuarantineSecretStore::quarantine()` (a core turn-boundary secret-quarantine feature, `trust/secret_quarantine.hpp`), which calls `agentengine::trust::hmac_sha256` — a symbol that exists only inside the `WIN32`-only `agentengine_capability_token` library (ADR-005's own deliberate BCrypt/CNG scope, never given a Linux twin). Re-gated `WIN32`-only, with the comment corrected to name the real reason. That gap turned out WIDER than this one target: a full `make -j12 -k` build surfaced the identical `hmac_sha256` linker error in `test_session_builder` (an old, long-existing test with zero connection to `SandboxToolProvider`) — confirmed pre-existing and unrelated via `git stash`ing this ADR's entire diff and reproducing the identical failure against the untouched tree; this had simply never been exercised before because this repo's Linux build directory had never been configured with `AGENTENGINE_WITH_HTTPS=ON` until this investigation turned it on. Full Linux `ctest` (207 tests, first-ever run with HTTPS on): 202/207 passed; the 5 failures — `test_provider_egress_address_policy` (a real, NEW, DISCLOSED finding, out of this ADR's scope: `G3: an octal-encoded address is not a dotted quad` fails, a potential SSRF-adjacent parsing discrepancy between ADR-016's two address resolvers, also newly exposed by turning HTTPS on for the first time) and 4 `test_kata_backend_*_linux` (confirmed environment-caused: this WSL2 distro has no working Kata/KVM runtime, an already-recorded gap) — all pre-existing, reproduced identically with this ADR's changes stashed out. An independent, fresh `general-purpose` red-team round reproduced every claim directly against the real repo (not by re-reading this ADR's prose), traced the CMake `if`/`else`/`endif` nesting hunk-by-hunk to confirm it stayed balanced, explicitly hunted for and ruled out a "third reason" `sandboxed_shell_chat` might fail beyond `hmac_sha256`, independently reproduced the `git stash` claim itself, confirmed zero new authority reachable (I2/I3: only the two `CMakeLists.txt` files changed, no `src/`/`include/` diff), and found two real, previously-undisclosed stale top-of-file comments (`tests/test_composed_sandbox_providers_live.cpp` and `tools/sandboxed_shell_chat.cpp`, both still asserting the same stale "SandboxToolProvider's own platform scope" claim this ADR corrected everywhere else) plus an inherited `CONVENTIONS.md` misquote repeated across ADR-103/104/105 (the real text is "Isolation parity is a gate, not a goal.", not "...not identical shape") — all fixed as a direct result of this red-team round. Windows: full rebuild (323/323 targets) and full `ctest` (287/287, 100%), zero regressions. Explicitly out of scope, named for later work: an `hmac_sha256` Linux port (ADR-005's own deliberate scope, a distinct security-critical crypto-primitive question — a from-scratch RFC 2104 HMAC-SHA256 construction on the existing `worktree_digest_posix.cpp` SHA-256 primitive, verified against RFC 4231's known-answer vectors, is the likely shape); the ADR-016 egress-policy octal-address finding; `ContainerdExecutionSurface`/ADR-101 promotion; the Windows-side shell-injection blacklist re-audit (ADR-104 §7). |
| [106](ADR-106-document-extraction-catalog-candidate-license-substitution.md) | 009 §7's "Document extraction" candidate names poppler/mupdf; does a locked MIT license let either ship? | **Proposed — design phase only, no red-team/implementation yet.** Checked each project's actual license directly: poppler is GPL, mupdf is AGPL-or-commercial — both copyleft, both incompatible with statically vendoring into this project's MIT-licensed binary the same way wasmtime/mbedTLS/CPython already are. **Decision: PDFium (BSD-3-Clause, Google) replaces poppler/mupdf** for the PDF text-layer half of this catalog row — permissive, composes cleanly with MIT, and (unlike a bespoke parser) is a real, security-fuzzed decoder for input this catalog row itself frames as hostile. libxml2 (MIT) is unaffected. Explicitly left open for a follow-up implementation ADR: the DOCX/PPTX/XLSX structural-parser third of this row (no permissively-licensed C++ equivalent to Docling/MarkItDown researched yet), vendoring mechanics, native-`Tool<>`-vs-WASM-plugin shape, and the real red-team pass hostile-PDF-parsing-in-process requires before anything ships — this ADR only settles the license question, matching the design-acceptance/implementation split already used for ADR-099→ADR-102. |

| [107](ADR-107-hmac-sha256-linux-parity.md) | Does `trust::hmac_sha256()` need a real Linux implementation, and if so, does giving it one accidentally widen ADR-005's deliberately Windows-only cross-process-token scope? | **Proposed — implemented, verified against RFC 4231, and independently red-teamed (2026-08-29), real builds and real test runs on Linux (WSL2 Ubuntu/gcc).** Closes the gap ADR-105 §7 found and disclosed but deliberately did not fix: `hmac_sha256` had an implementation only on Windows (`src/trust/hmac.cpp`, CNG/BCrypt), built exclusively inside the `WIN32`-only `agentengine_capability_token` library (ADR-005's own deliberate cross-process-token scope) — but `trust/secret_quarantine.hpp`'s `QuarantineSecretStore::quarantine()`, a core, UNRELATED, turn-boundary secret-quarantine feature (ADR-068), also calls it, breaking every `AGENTENGINE_WITH_HTTPS`-gated Linux target touching `QuarantineSecretStore`. The session's own brief assumed `capability_token.cpp`/`capability_registry.cpp`/`bearer_token.cpp` were "already portable" — verified false by direct read: all three call `BCryptGenRandom` directly, a genuinely Windows-only concern. Rather than porting that whole library (widening ADR-005's own deliberate scope) or duplicating the HMAC construction a second time, split `hmac_sha256`/`constant_time_equal` into their own small, portable `agentengine_hmac` CMake library (`hmac.cpp` on `WIN32`; new `hmac_posix.cpp`, a from-scratch RFC 2104 construction, on `NOT WIN32`) that `agentengine_capability_token` now links instead of compiling `hmac.cpp` inline — leaving that library's other three, genuinely-Windows-only files completely untouched. The Linux HMAC construction reuses (not reinvents) `worktree_digest_posix.cpp`'s existing, Judged, from-scratch FIPS 180-4 SHA-256 implementation: factored its compression-function/padding logic out into a new shared header, `include/agentengine/detail/sha256_posix.hpp` (`sha256_raw()`), refactoring `compute_digest()` to delegate to it rather than reimplementing SHA-256 a second, independent time — the exact "two unaudited copies of the same primitive" risk `hmac.hpp`'s own history already warns against (ADR-021's red-team finding). Verified against RFC 4231's published known-answer test vectors, transcribed directly from the RFC text (`https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4231.txt`), not from memory, after an AI-summarized extraction attempt produced a garbled value the raw-text read caught: all 7 numbered test cases PASS byte-for-byte (including Test Case 5's published 128-bit truncated compare and Test Cases 6/7's key-longer-than-block-size `K'=H(K)` branch), plus 5 edge-case checks. A negative control (temporarily corrupting the ipad constant) confirmed all 7 vectors then FAIL, and the file was restored byte-identical (verified via `diff`) — this project's own standard, "a test that cannot fail proves nothing." That exact vector set became a new, permanent, dedicated regression test, `tests/test_hmac_sha256.cpp` (portable, wired unconditionally for both platforms — prior coverage of this primitive was only INDIRECT, via `capability_token`/`bearer_token`/`secret_quarantine` tests). Found and fixed, as a byproduct, a real pre-existing bug independent of this ADR's own scope: `test_session_builder` had never linked anything providing `hmac_sha256` at all, on EITHER platform, despite its own B7 case ODR-using it through `QuarantineSecretStore` — a one-line missing-dependency fix. `test_secret_quarantine`/`test_rt_agent_session_quarantine_tool` un-gated from `WIN32`-only (now build and pass on Linux). `agentengine_sandboxed_shell_chat`'s `WIN32` gate removed entirely — confirmed `hmac_sha256` was the ONLY reason it couldn't build on Linux (every other dependency already portable per ADR-103/104/105, zero `_WIN32`/`windows.h` references in the file itself). An independent, fresh `general-purpose` red-team round, briefed with explicit git-safety instructions after ADR-105's own red-team once accidentally discarded an uncommitted diff via `git checkout --`, found and fixed ONE real bug: `hmac_sha256`'s `std::vector<std::byte> inner_buf(kBlockSize + data_len)` had no overflow guard on the size computation — surfaced via GCC's `-Wstringop-overflow=` at `-O2` (invisible at the project's default `-O0`), a latent heap-buffer-overflow-class defect, not practically exploitable (`data_len` near `SIZE_MAX` cannot correspond to any real addressable buffer) but fixed anyway with an explicit `contract`-class error return rather than left as reasoning-only UB; re-verified byte-identical output on every real input before/after. The same round independently re-derived all 7 RFC 4231 vectors from the primary source AND cross-checked them (plus 10 additional self-generated vectors covering the 64/65-byte key boundary, 1MB data, and the SHA-256 padding-branch boundaries) against Python's own independent `hmac`/`hashlib` implementation — all matched; independently hashed 9 more inputs spanning both padding boundaries through the refactored `compute_digest()` and cross-checked against `hashlib.sha256` — all matched, confirming the refactor changed nothing; performed its own line-by-line timing-side-channel analysis (verdict: every branch depends only on public lengths, never secret byte values — no side channel); confirmed zero capability/authority logic embedded (I2/I3); confirmed the excluded `native_jail`/`kata`/`execution_surface` directories were untouched and no uncommitted work was lost. Full Linux `ctest` (207 tests): **206/207 passed**, both before and after the red-team's fix — the one failure is the SAME pre-existing, already-disclosed, unrelated `test_provider_egress_address_policy` `G3` octal-address finding ADR-105 §7 named. **WINDOWS SIDE SINCE VERIFIED (2026-08-29, same day, by the coordinating session)**: this ADR was originally authored without Windows toolchain access — closed the same day once merged into a session with real MSVC access: full Windows rebuild (348/348 targets, zero errors) and full `ctest` (**288/288, 100%**, including the new `test_hmac_sha256` and every target this ADR un-gated), zero regressions on either platform. Renumbered from its original ADR-145 to ADR-107 during merge — developed in parallel with, and independently of, `ContainerdExecutionSurface`'s own ADR-145 promotion, which claimed that number first. `agentengine_capability_token`'s own three `BCryptGenRandom`-based files remain deliberately un-ported to Linux, ADR-005's own distinct design question, not this ADR's. |
| [108](ADR-108-execution-surface-orphan-reap.md) | Does a real, adversarially-testable fix exist for the container-orphan residual both `DockerExecutionSurface` and `ContainerdExecutionSurface` have long disclosed, without widening either conformer's own authority (I2)? | **Proposed — implemented, verified against both live daemons, independently red-teamed (2026-08-29).** Adds `DockerCliBackend::reap_orphans()` and `ContainerdCliBackend::reap_orphans()`, adapted from `Ledger`'s own orphan-branch precedent but shaped as garbage-collection (`Ledger::abandon()`'s shape), not resumption — nobody in-process ever held a reference to a crashed container, so there is nothing to hand back. `DockerCliBackend::create()` now names every container `ae_des_<pid>_<seq>` via `docker run --name` (previously unnamed); `ContainerdCliBackend`'s existing `ae_ces_<pid>_<seq>` id scheme (ADR-145) was already discoverable. `reap_orphans()` lists the daemon's own container table, keeps only prefix-matching names with a parseable pid, and destroys the ones whose pid is confirmed dead via a fails-closed liveness check (POSIX `kill(pid,0)`/`ESRCH`; Windows `OpenProcess`+`GetExitCodeProcess`) — every ambiguous outcome resolves to "alive, don't touch." Deliberately NOT called automatically from any constructor/reset()/destructor (a Delegated Decision Seam — reaping touches OTHER processes' containers by definition). New `tests/test_docker_orphan_reap.cpp` (cross-platform, no `WIN32` gate) and 8 new checks appended to `tests/test_containerd_execution_surface.cpp`, both with real positive AND negative controls (a live-pid container survives, a confirmed-dead-pid one is reaped, a non-prefixed one is never touched). An independent, fresh `general-purpose` red-team round found **3 real issues, all fixed same day**: (1) `parse_orphan_pid()` parsed into a 64-bit `long` but `process_is_alive()` casts to a 32-bit `pid_t`/`DWORD` — a decimal run fitting in `long` but exceeding `INT32_MAX` silently truncated on the cast, and the red-team empirically proved the truncated value can read back as a dead pid even though no real pid ever reaches that range, letting `reap_orphans()` on a shared daemon destroy a container it never created (a direct I2 violation) — fixed by rejecting any parsed value outside `(0, INT32_MAX]` before it ever reaches a liveness check, with new regression checks added to both test files; (2) adding mandatory `--name` to `docker run` introduced a real, undisclosed collision regression — a process whose orphaned container is still occupying `ae_des_<pid>_1` causes a LATER process that reuses that same pid to fail its own first `create()` call outright, since the 0-based per-process counter recomputes the identical name — fixed by seeding the counter from a wall-clock nanosecond timestamp instead of 0; (3) `ContainerdExecutionSurface::seq_` (pre-existing, per-instance, unchanged by this ADR's own diff) shares the identical 0-based collision class and was never actually made to "mirror" the Docker fix as this ADR's own design section claimed — fixed by seeding it the same way. Two minor test-hygiene findings (a fixed literal negative-control container name risking cross-run collision; one unused local) were also fixed; one theoretical vacuous-pass edge case was assessed genuinely benign and left as-is. Post-fix re-verification against real daemons: `test_docker_orphan_reap` 11/11 on both Windows and WSL2/Linux Docker; `test_containerd_execution_surface` 28/28 on WSL2/containerd; full Windows `ctest` 289/289 (100%); full Linux `ctest` 207 total with only the 4 pre-existing, environment-caused Kata image-pull failures and one confirmed `-j 4` parallel-execution flake in an unrelated egress test (re-run standalone: `ALL PASS`) — zero real regressions. **BOTH remaining residuals SINCE CLOSED (2026-08-29, same session, on explicit user request)**: (1) the pid-reuse race is now NARROWED via a real `check_process_identity()` mechanism — a per-process-instance "start key" (`/proc/<pid>/stat`'s `starttime` on POSIX, `GetProcessTimes()`'s creation time on Windows) embedded alongside the pid in the naming scheme, layered on top of the existing, unchanged `process_is_alive()` gate — proven via a new, direct positive control (same live pid, deliberately WRONG start-key, confirmed reaped) added to both test files (14/14, 31/31); a tiny, irreducible TOCTOU window remains, disclosed not eliminated. (2) `tools/sandboxed_shell_chat.cpp`/`tools/containerd_shell_chat.cpp` now both run a best-effort, non-fatal `reap_orphans()` sweep at startup, verified end to end (not just at the unit level) against both live daemons by manually planting an orphan-shaped container and confirming the actual tool binary reaped it. This verification pass also surfaced two new, honestly disclosed findings, neither blocking: a real WSL2 `/mnt/d` 9p cross-filesystem stale-build artifact (an incremental `cmake --build` silently skipped recompiling one source despite a genuinely newer mtime — worked around by force-deleting the object file, a real gotcha for future WSL2 work against a `/mnt/*`-hosted tree, unrelated to this ADR's own code), and a genuine multi-pid-namespace-sharing-one-daemon hazard specific to Docker Desktop's WSL2 integration (a container created in one OS pid namespace can be reaped by a concurrent `reap_orphans()` call running in a DIFFERENT pid namespace pointed at the same shared daemon — observed directly when the Windows and WSL2 `test_docker_orphan_reap` runs executed concurrently, both pass cleanly in isolation; `ContainerdCliBackend` is immune, only ever reachable from one pid namespace here) — a real, disclosed, deliberately-out-of-scope residual, distinct from the pid-reuse-over-time race this pass fixed. Full post-follow-on re-verification: Windows `ctest` 289/289 (100%); Linux `ctest` 214 total, only the same 4 pre-existing Kata failures (the earlier egress-test and `test_sandbox_runtime` flakes did not recur). |
| [109](ADR-109-kata-backend-rootfs-writable-layer-fix.md) | Does `KataBackend`'s own core claim — "a real Kata sandbox starts successfully" — actually hold once a real VM, guest agent, and containerd are in the loop, after a dozen ADRs' worth of design that (per its own provisioning runbook) had never once run against a live deployment? | **Proposed — implemented, verified against a REAL Kata Containers 4.1.0 (cloud-hypervisor) deployment, independently red-teamed (2026-08-29).** This session provisioned real Kata Containers in its own WSL2 dev environment (real `/dev/kvm`, unlike the previously-blocked Proxmox-LXC CI-runner candidate) specifically to run this whole test suite for real for the first time ever. Found and fixed the actual defect via systematic empirical bisection (not guessing): `KataBackend::create()`'s `disk_bytes == 0` default case gave `rootfs_dir` a plain, READ-ONLY bind mount of `ctr images mount`'s own read-only target — and Kata's Rust guest agent (`runtime-rs`) does NOT auto-create a missing mount-point directory before mounting something there (unlike `runc`). `busybox:latest` ships only `/dev` as a pre-existing top-level directory, so every one of `build_oci_spec_json()`'s own default mounts (`/proc`,`/sys`,`/run`,`/dev/pts`,`/dev/shm`,`/dev/mqueue`) failed guest-side `create_container` with a bare ENOENT, for EVERY default `create()` call, with any image that doesn't ship all of them. Fixed by making `rootfs_dir` ALWAYS a real overlay with a writable upper layer (loop-device-backed ext4 when `disk_bytes>0`, unchanged; a size-capped tmpfs otherwise) and pre-creating every declared mount-point directory (plus an empty `/etc/hosts` file when network access is granted) into it before invoking `ctr run --config`. `destroy()`'s teardown unified correspondingly. An independent, fresh `general-purpose` red-team round found **2 real issues in this SAME DAY's own fix, both fixed before landing**: (1) BLOCKING — the first version used an UNSIZED tmpfs for the no-quota writable layer, whose Linux kernel default (50% of physical RAM) silently reopened a resource-budget question `docs/planning/kata-backend-config-pipeline-pids-disk-net-redesign-draft.md` had explicitly scoped `disk_bytes==0` OUT of and deferred to separate, owner-decided follow-on work — a real, guest-reachable, zero-capability-gated, up-to-50%-of-host-RAM write primitive (since the tmpfs is shared into the guest via virtiofs, entirely outside the guest's own memory cgroup) that composed across concurrently-created instances, and a real I8 regression versus the ACTUAL prior behavior (a read-only bind mount enforced a budget of literally zero writable bytes) — fixed by capping the tmpfs at `size=4m`, re-verified all four Kata suites' own results unchanged; (2) a host-side directory-traversal gap in the new mount-point pre-creation loop — `fs::path::relative_path()` (empirically verified, not assumed) does NOT strip embedded `..` components, and `authorize_spec()` only rejects a `.`/`..` `guest_path` component when the caller holds a `cap::SandboxMount` grant (every existing call site holds none), so an unvalidated `MountSpec::guest_path` could make the new `fs::create_directories()` call create real directories OUTSIDE `rootfs_dir` on the host — fixed via an unconditional `has_dot_or_dotdot_component(guest_path)` rejection mirroring `targets_own_workdir()`'s own existing unconditional treatment of `host_path` for the identical risk class. A real-but-minor `/etc/hosts`-as-directory type-detection gap (a bare `fs::exists()` doesn't distinguish file vs. directory) was also found and fixed via `fs::status()`. Verification against the live deployment: `test_kata_backend_linux` 8/8 ALL PASS (was 0 before this fix — every case failed at `create()`); `test_kata_backend_slice2_linux` 22/22 ALL PASS, including real, first-ever-live proofs of a genuine bidirectional virtiofs bind mount, a `memory_bytes` cap really resizing the VM (measured), an `fds` cap's `RLIMIT_NOFILE` genuinely propagating to a LATER `ctr tasks exec` call (SLICE 7's own previously-disclosed open question, answered for real), and a `wall_ms` cap genuinely bounding a real 10-second guest sleep; `test_kata_backend_abuse_corpus_linux` 25/26 (1 separate, real, first-time-surfaced containment-timing finding, disclosed not chased down); `test_kata_backend_slice9_10_linux` 12/14 after also fixing, as a byproduct, one real pre-existing test-sequencing bug (a disk-quota "positive control" case raced against a PRIOR case's own uncleaned failed-`dd` output, not a fresh budget) — the 2 remaining failures are both `cnitool`/CNI-plugin-dependent, and CNI was never installed in this environment, matching the provisioning runbook's own explicit "needed only for NetPolicy::allowlist tests" scoping, not a code defect. Also fixed, as a second byproduct: two stale test error-code assertions in `test_kata_backend_slice2_linux.cpp` (`kata_backend.net_allowlist_unsupported`, a Slice-2-era code SLICE 10/ADR-093 superseded with `kata_backend.net_capability_required` — never caught before because this assertion had never run against a live deployment). Full project `ctest`: zero regressions outside the Kata suite itself, on either platform (every touched file is Linux-only). |
| [110](ADR-110-kata-backend-heartbeat-virtiofs-writeback-residual.md) | Is ADR-109 §8's disclosed-not-diagnosed heartbeat-empty finding really `virtio_fs_cache = "auto"` write-back timing, and if so, what closes it without weakening the containment proof? | **Proposed — root cause reasoned from the active Kata config and documented FUSE writeback semantics, fix applied (2026-08-29), NOT re-verified live.** Confirmed the live, installed Kata clh config actually sets `virtio_fs_cache = "auto"`, whose own doc comment ("close to open consistency") describes Linux FUSE writeback-cache semantics: a `close()` triggers a `FLUSH` request but does not itself force synchronous writeback — actual writeback follows the kernel's ordinary dirty-page timer (`dirty_writeback_centisecs`, default 5s). `wall_ms=800` plus a 1-second re-read window are both comfortably inside that timer's interval, so ADR-109's observed `v1=v2=empty` is consistent with the guest's dirty heartbeat pages never having been swept to virtiofsd at all — a proof-mechanism timing artifact, not evidence about the workload's liveness. Fix: added `sync` immediately after each heartbeat write in both Case 1 and Case 2's guest scripts (`tests/test_kata_backend_abuse_corpus_linux.cpp`), forcing host-visibility synchronous with the guest write; also corrected the file's own top-of-file disclosure comment, stale since ADR-109 landed (it still claimed "not run live" despite ADR-109's own 25/26 live run). **Separately found, same pass: a real, blocking `ctr images mount` environment defect** — containerd's `io.containerd.mount-manager.v1.bolt` plugin registers a bind-mount record but never creates its source directory under `/run`, failing every `KataBackend::create()` call with ENOENT/ENODEV. Isolated via a minimal, AgentEngine-code-free repro; ruled out as transient by reproducing after a `containerd` restart, a `/run/containerd` wipe, AND a full `wsl --shutdown`; ruled out as Kata-specific or general-containerd-broken since plain `ctr run --rm busybox echo hello` (containerd's normal snapshot-mount path) and a raw `mount -t overlay` both succeed cleanly — the defect is isolated to the standalone `ctr images mount` subcommand specifically, the exact mechanism `create()` depends on for Kata's custom `--config`-mode rootfs (SLICE 9's own reasoning for not using the normal RPC path). This blocked re-verifying this ADR's own fix and blocks the entire Kata suite from running live in this session, materially larger than the residual this ADR set out to close — disclosed, not chased down further (containerd 2.2.1 plugin internals are out of scope here). No production code touched — `tests/test_kata_backend_abuse_corpus_linux.cpp` only. |
| [111](ADR-111-ledger-merge-asyncquota-gate.md) | `Ledger::merge()` performs real object-store I/O (3 tree loads, a `put_tree`, ACL mutation, a snapshot persist) with zero `AsyncQuota` gating, an I8 gap named by ADR-102 Phases 2/3/4/5 and never fixed -- is the fix as simple as "mirror `commit()`/`branch_from()`'s restructure", or does `merge()`'s 8 early-return paths make it a real trap? | **Proposed — implemented, independently red-teamed (found and fixed one real MUST-FIX issue same day), verified (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild + `ctest` clean outside 5 pre-existing, unrelated, environment-caused failures; NOT yet Linux-verified.** Added a dedicated `MergeCost` Kind tag (`include/agentengine/core/ledger.hpp`, alongside `BranchCost`/`StorageBytes`, matching the class's own one-tag-per-verb convention -- merge()'s cost is a fixed per-call expense, neither byte-proportional like `StorageBytes` nor the same budget as `BranchCost`'s "how many branches" question). `merge()` now consumes 1 `MergeCost` unit via `co_await quota.try_consume(1, requested_by)` BEFORE `mutex_` is ever taken, with its entire existing 8-early-return-path body (every finding, every comment, unchanged) moved into a non-coroutine immediately-invoked lambda so the lock is never held across a `co_await` -- the exact restructure `commit()`/`branch_from()` already established. Every failure path inside the lambda refunds the unit. `SandboxRuntime::merge_into()` (`sandbox_runtime.hpp`) threads the new quota through -- its only real-tree caller turned out to be zero (grep-confirmed: no production code or test calls it; only a separate, standalone prove-phase copy under `docs/planning/proofs/` has a same-named method), so the blast radius was smaller than the signature change suggested. **MUST-FIX red-team finding (fixed same day):** the quota-exhaustion path's first version copied `branch_from()`'s own quota-check shape verbatim -- but unlike `branch_from()`'s by-const-reference `parent`, `merge()`'s `child` is taken BY VALUE, so leaving it unresolved on this one early-return path meant `BranchHandle::~BranchHandle()`'s own `maybe_queue_abandon()` would silently schedule the branch for REAL ERASURE on the next unrelated `reap_pending_abandons()` call -- genuine data loss, not the "untouched" behavior this ADR's own first draft claimed. Fixed by registering `child` into `orphaned_from_restart_` and marking it `resolved_` on quota exhaustion too, matching every other rejection path's own contract exactly (safe: no `co_await` inside the brief lock scope this fix adds, so the lock-across-suspension hazard doesn't reopen). `tests/test_ledger.cpp` extended, not just patched to compile: case [4] now asserts a successful merge consumes exactly 1 unit; case [5] now asserts a REJECTED (conflict) merge refunds it back to the exact same `remaining()`; case [12] (corrected alongside the fix -- its first version asserted the WRONG behavior) mints an identity with a real branch/storage quota but a `MergeCost` quota of ZERO, proving the merge is refused closed with `async_quota.exhausted`, and that the child branch becomes a real, reclaimable orphan (`orphaned_branches()` contains it AND `reclaim_orphaned_branch()` genuinely succeeds), matching case [5]'s own contract rather than the "left fully untouched" claim the first version wrongly asserted. Full `ctest`: 246/251 (98%), the 5 failures independently confirmed Docker-daemon-dependent (Docker Desktop not running on this machine) or an unrelated Python-worker/matplotlib gap -- none exercise `merge()`/`merge_into()` at all. A second review pass caught `MergeCost` missing its required `027-Vocabulary-and-Naming.md` row (`tools/naming_lint.py`'s own CI gate) and a minor qualification inconsistency, both fixed same day; that same pass surfaced two PRE-EXISTING, unrelated vocabulary gaps from ADR-145 (`ContainerdCliBackend`/`ContainerdExecutionSurface` had no rows at all), also closed while there. `naming_lint.py` now reports clean. Explicitly NOT done: no Linux verification, and `merge_into()` still has no real first caller anywhere in the tree. |
| [112](ADR-112-ledger-merge-blob-content-acl-grant.md) | `merge()`'s own parent-owner ACL grant is tree-digest-level only (disclosed, unattempted since ADR-102 Phase 2) -- the parent branch's owner can list the merged tree's structure but not fetch the actual bytes of a blob the child alone wrote.
