Is "the orchestrator resumes" now fully restored, or still only structural? | **Proposed — implemented, verified (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild + `ctest` clean at the same 246/251 ADR-111 established; NOT yet independently red-teamed or Linux-verified.** Extends the SAME existing tree-level grant block in `merge()` with a per-entry loop: for every entry in the merged tree, grants `parent_state.created_by_id` root access to that entry's own digest too (`tree_acl_`/`blob_acl_` per `entry.is_tree`) -- identical reasoning to the existing grant (the parent owner already owns the branch; this isn't widening authority to a stranger, it completes the same category of grant one level down). Deliberately BEST-EFFORT: a per-entry grant hitting `ledger.acl_root_cap_exceeded` does not reject an otherwise-successful merge -- a capped digest is left exactly as inaccessible as before, no regression. `tests/test_ledger.cpp` case [4] extended with a positive proof (`owner` can now `get_blob_safe()` the child's own blob, previously `blob_access_denied`) and a negative control (`unrelated` still refused the same blob -- the grant doesn't over-widen). Sanity-checked the test itself: temporarily disabled the fix, confirmed the new check fails exactly as expected, restored it. No new exported vocabulary -- `naming_lint.py` unaffected. |
| [113](ADR-113-sandbox-runtime-nonok-klass-commit-gate-analysis.md) | ADR-101 named but scoped out `SandboxRuntime::run()`'s own unconditional-commit-on-non-ok-klass behavior -- is it a live defect, and does it need a code fix now? | **Proposed — analysis only, no production code changed.** Confirmed the commit path itself has no gate: `run()` treats any `result<SurfaceRunOutcome>` value as "genuinely attempted" and commits unconditionally, and `Checkpoint` carries no field recording what the exec outcome was. But `SurfaceRunOutcome` (`execution_surface.hpp`) -- what the two real, production `ExecutionSurface` conformers (`DockerExecutionSurface`/`ContainerdExecutionSurface`) actually return -- is only `{exit_code, stdout_text}`, with no klass concept at all; both conformers confirmed by direct read to only ever produce a raw process exit code. The only thing that could ever hand a non-ok `exec_outcome_class` (timeout/oom/crash/policy_violation/escape_attempt/ask_pending) into this path is `docs/planning/proofs/execution_surface/tree_backend_execution_surface_adapter.hpp` -- prove-phase only, never promoted, itself gated behind still-unjudged ADR-101. Conclusion: the gap is real but currently dormant, unreachable by anything shipped. Rather than widen `SurfaceRunOutcome`/add a commit-gating branch against a caller that does not exist yet (speculative generality this project's own conventions reject), recorded as an explicit PROMOTION GATE: any future promotion of `TreeBackendExecutionSurface` or any other klass-carrying `ExecutionSurface` conformer MUST resolve this (klass representation, whether a non-ok outcome commits at all, whether `Checkpoint` needs a distinguishing field) as part of that same promotion ADR, with its own red-team round, not silently inherited as a pre-existing gap. No code changed; no new vocabulary; `merge_into()`'s own separate zero-caller residual (ADR-111) is unrelated and untouched. |
| [114](ADR-114-task-branch-tools-promotion.md) | `SandboxRuntime::merge_into()` has had zero real production callers since ADR-111 (`docs/planning/proofs/task_branch_tool/task_branch_sandbox.hpp`'s own A10 design was never promoted). Is promotion mostly mechanical, or does it need new design decisions first? | **Proposed — implemented, verified against a REAL Docker daemon (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild + `ctest` clean at 251/252 (the one failure is the same pre-existing, unrelated matplotlib/pandas gap ADR-111/112 already named). Independent red-team round completed same day (ADR-114 §7): found and fixed one real MUST-FIX and one real SHOULD-FIX; NOT yet Linux-verified.** Ported the prove-phase `TaskBranchSandbox`'s four verbs (`start_task_branch`/`run_in_task_branch`/`commit_task_branch`/`discard_task_branch`) directly onto `MandatorySandboxProvider<Surface>` (not as a separate sibling object -- it already owns everything the prove-phase design's own "main" parameter required) as four new `Tool<>` conformers, gated behind a deliberately separate, opt-in `bind_task_branch_tools(AsyncQuota<MergeCost>&)` call -- `bind_sandbox()`'s own existing signature is completely untouched, so every existing caller (3 production tools, 4 test files) compiles and runs unmodified. CAPABILITY-GATING DECISION (the one real, non-mechanical question): mirrors `RunCommandTool`'s own zero-`Capabilities<...>`-ceiling precedent rather than promoting the prove-phase design's own two-tag `CapabilitySet` gate into the real, closed `Capability` variant (19 alternatives) -- real, security-critical surgery this design line has consistently declined absent a real caller to design it against; disclosed as follow-on work, not silently skipped. `tests/test_task_branch_tools.cpp` (new) proves start/run/commit with a real merge into the parent's own branch (MergeCost consumed by exactly 1 unit), a best-of-N/sequential-conflict-then-reclaim sequence (the same handle_id survives a real rejection), discard's BranchCost refund, every fail-closed path, AND -- matching this design line's own established rigor bar -- the full sequence driven through the REAL, unmodified `session.start_run() -> invoke_tool()` pipeline, not just direct accessor calls. A real bug was caught WHILE WRITING the test, not after: the shared `ScriptedChatClient` test fixture never reset its own call-count between the test's three sequential script phases, silently misaligning which scripted tool call each phase actually drove -- caught because the specific content-arrived-on-parent-branch check failed, not because anything crashed; fixed in the test fixture itself. Confirmed (git-stash-scoped) that both an unrelated pre-existing `ctest` failure and an ~~unrelated pre-existing MSVC `C1128` (`agentengine_cli_chat`, `/bigobj`) build limit reproduce identically on the unmodified tree~~ (**corrected by ADR-122**: that comparison was against a stale, orphaned `.vcxproj` in a drifted build directory, not a real defect -- `agentengine_cli_chat` builds clean). `027-Vocabulary-and-Naming.md` gets 12 new rows (4 tools + 8 Args/Reply types); `naming_lint.py` reports clean. **SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (§7), BOTH FINDINGS CLOSED**: (1) MUST-FIX, live and I8/availability-relevant -- `Ledger::merge()` refunds its own `MergeCost` unit on every rejection, including a real conflict that only occurs after real, expensive tree-load+diff work; `commit_task_branch()`'s own reclaim-and-retry behavior let a caller repeat that real work indefinitely for a NET `MergeCost` cost of zero (empirically proven: 50/50 free rejected retries, each briefly holding `Ledger::mutex_`, a real cross-session availability lever) -- fixed by re-consuming 1 `MergeCost` unit at the task-branch layer after every successful reclaim (the underlying, already-tested `Ledger::merge()` refund contract is untouched for every other caller), with a clean, explicit discard-and-refund fallback if the re-charge itself can't be afforded. (2) SHOULD-FIX, real but not currently reachable by any production caller -- `bind_sandbox()`'s re-bind and both `operator=` paths silently `task_branches_.clear()`-ed without discarding active branches, permanently leaking their `BranchCost` units with no refund and no error -- fixed via a new `discard_all_active_task_branches_and_refund()` helper called before any state is reassigned. Three new test checks for both fixes, sanity-checked by reverting only the source fix (`git stash`-scoped) and confirming all 8 new assertions genuinely fail against the pre-fix code before restoring. Full re-verification: real-Docker tests clean, full rebuild zero errors, `ctest` unchanged at 251/252, no new vocabulary. |
| [115](ADR-115-linux-verification-adr108-114.md) | Every ADR from ADR-109 through ADR-114 disclosed "no Linux verification" -- does this branch's newest work actually hold up on a real, independently-obtained Linux checkout? | **Proposed — real verification pass, no production code changed.** Fresh `git clone` of this branch's exact HEAD into a NEW WSL2 Ubuntu 24.04 directory (the pre-existing `/root/ae` in that distro turned out not to be a git repository at all -- unclear provenance, left untouched), built with GCC 14.2.0 (the distro's own DEFAULT `gcc`/`g++` is 13.3.0, below this project's own 021 §5 floor -- a real, useful finding). **Full project build: 764/764 targets, zero errors** -- the first time this exact branch tip has been built end-to-end on Linux at all. **`test_ledger`: ALL CHECKS PASSED**, no daemon needed -- a complete, unconditional closure of ADR-111/112's own Linux-verification residual. **`test_containerd_execution_surface`: 31/31 checks passed** against a real, already-running containerd daemon, including ADR-108's full `reap_orphans()` positive/negative-control suite (the pid-reuse fix included) -- re-confirms ADR-145/108 end-to-end on a genuinely fresh environment. **`test_mandatory_sandbox_provider_composed`: ALL CHECKS PASSED**, no daemon needed, exercising this session's own ADR-114-follow-on `discard_all_active_task_branches_and_refund()` code path for real. 7 failures, all sharing one disclosed, environment-caused root cause: this WSL2 distro's `docker` on `PATH` resolves to the WINDOWS Docker Desktop binary bridged in via `/mnt/c/...`, which cannot reach a real daemon from the Linux side -- not a broken/absent installation, and materially different from ADR-105's own prior "docker not on PATH at all" finding. This narrows, but does not fully close, ADR-114's own Linux-verification residual: `MandatorySandboxProvider<Surface>`'s task-branch logic is now confirmed to COMPILE cleanly on Linux (new), and its underlying `Ledger`/`SandboxRuntime`/`reap_orphans()` machinery is fully re-proven under a real container runtime on this exact environment via the containerd path -- but `test_task_branch_tools.cpp` itself is hardcoded to `DockerExecutionSurface` and could not be run for real here. Installing a genuine Linux-native Docker daemon in this WSL2 distro was judged a bigger, separate environment decision, not made as a side effect of a verification pass. |
| [116](ADR-116-composed-context-provider-move-assignment-identity-check.md) | `ComposedContextProvider<Ms...>`'s move-assignment cross-session aliasing bypass (ADR-102 §48 finding 2, disclosed twice, never closed) -- is either of the two named remediations (a non-assignable accessor shape, or an owning-session-identity check) actually worth doing, and does closing it break the existing suite's own legitimate self-assignment/move-construction coverage? | **Proposed — implemented, verified (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild + `ctest` clean at 251/252 (same pre-existing matplotlib/pandas gap). Independent red-team round completed same day (ADR-116 §7): found and fixed two real MUST-FIX issues. **Linux-verified, ADR-121** (2026-08-30, same day): B20/B23/B24/B25 all pass on real GCC 14.2.0, including B25's own ABA scenario reproduced for real on a second, independent allocator (glibc, not MSVC's CRT).** Rejected the accessor-shape option (would touch ~30 files calling `.history_provider()`, almost all using an unrelated, non-composed `HistoryProviderT` this hazard never applied to) and the naive "just make `operator=` private+friend `AgentSession`" option (access control can't distinguish the accessor route from a bare standalone variable, so it would have silently broken `test_session_builder.cpp`'s own B21a self-assignment regression test with no legitimate substitute). Implemented the owning-session-identity check instead: `ComposedContextProvider` gains a private `owner_` tag and a private `bind_owner()` (friended to the `AgentSession` template, requiring `composed_context_provider.hpp` to `#include agent_session.hpp` directly -- the same core→rt dependency `session_builder.hpp` already has); `AgentSession::history_provider()`'s accessor stamps identity on every call via `if constexpr` (a true no-op for every other `HistoryProviderT`); `operator=` refuses -- a silent, disclosed no-op, not a crash (matching ADR-112 §2's own best-effort-disclosed precedent, since `operator=` is `noexcept` and structurally cannot return a `result<>`) -- only when both sides are tagged with two DIFFERENT sessions' own identities. Both sides untouched on refusal, not partially applied. `tests/test_session_builder.cpp`'s own B20 (which used to demonstrate the cross-session transfer succeeding) now proves the identical statement leaves BOTH sessions' own content completely unchanged instead. **SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (§7), BOTH FINDINGS CLOSED**: (1) MUST-FIX -- the original design tagged `owner_` only at the owning session's own accessor call and left it unset on every freshly move-constructed instance, so routing a move through ANY untagged intermediate variable (`auto smuggler = std::move(session1.history_provider());` then `session2.history_provider() = std::move(smuggler);`) bypassed the whole check in two ordinary-looking lines -- fixed by treating `owner_` as a provenance tag that follows the content through the move constructor AND `operator=` (propagated onto an untagged destination, never overwriting an already-tagged one), empirically confirmed via two new permanent regression tests (B23 two-hop, B24 three-hop through an `operator=`-populated relay) that both genuinely failed against the pre-fix code. (2) MUST-FIX -- a real, empirically-reproduced ABA hole from tagging with `AgentSession`'s own raw address (`void const*`): destroy a heap-allocated session holding an extracted, tagged provider, heap-allocate an unrelated new session, and the CRT allocator reliably handed back the identical freed address, wrongly matching the stale tag and leaking the first session's content into the second -- fixed by retagging with a new monotonic, process-wide, never-reused `std::atomic<std::uint64_t>` counter (`agent_session_detail::g_next_session_identity`) and a new `AgentSession::session_identity_ const` member, `owner_`'s type changed from `void const*` to `std::uint64_t`; verified via a new permanent regression test (B25) that reproduces the exact address-reuse precondition and confirms the leak is closed. Also corrected an inaccurate justifying comment (address stability claimed from a universal `make_unique` convention that isn't actually universal -- the real reason is `AgentSession`'s own non-movability via its `AsyncMutex` member, which was never the exploitable gap in the first place). Full re-verification: rebuild zero errors, `ctest` unchanged at 251/252, `naming_lint.py` clean, `test_session_builder` standalone run confirms B20/B23/B24/B25 alongside every pre-existing check. Updated the disclosure comments this residual was named in three times over (composed_context_provider.hpp's own top comment, both `tests/compile_fail/sandbox_tool_provider_rejects_fork_from*.cpp` files, `tests/CMakeLists.txt`'s own comment block, and ADR-102 §48/§51) to point here instead of re-disclosing it. No new exported vocabulary; `naming_lint.py` unaffected. |
| [117](ADR-117-task-branch-capability-variant-widening.md) | ADR-114 §2 named "the `Capability`-variant widening question" as real, deferred, security-critical follow-on work -- is it now worth doing, and can the prove-phase's own already-designed two-tag `cap::decl::TaskBranch`/`cap::decl::TaskBranchCommit` gate be promoted into the real, closed `Capability` variant without breaking anything depending on the old, ungated contract? | **Proposed — implemented, verified against a REAL Docker daemon (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild (zero errors) and full `ctest` clean, `naming_lint.py` clean. SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM: clean bill of health -- a fresh agent independently re-verified the exhaustiveness audit, re-ran the revert-and-restore sanity check itself, traced the `is_inert_for_text_derived_declassification()` polarity, confirmed `subsumes()`'s index guard is airtight, checked for a `Tool<>`-bypass path (found none reachable by any real caller), and checked for a serialization/ABI concern from appending two enumerators (found none) -- no fix needed, working tree left clean. **Linux-verified same day, ADR-118**: fast-forwarded ADR-115's own WSL2/GCC-14.2.0 checkout to this commit -- 610/610 targets rebuild clean, and the new double gate itself (both directions: granted -> allowed, withheld -> rejected with zero side effects) is proven COMPLETELY and unconditionally through the real `invoke_tool()` pipeline, independent of this environment's own pre-existing, already-disclosed Docker-CLI-reachability gap (which still leaves only the unrelated, pre-existing Docker-command-execution half of `test_task_branch_tools` unverified on Linux).** Promoted the prove-phase design verbatim (`task_branch_capability.hpp`'s own header comment had already specified the exact shape): `cap::TaskBranch{}`/`cap::TaskBranchCommit{}` (fieldless runtime markers, matching `cap::Entropy`/`cap::Elicit`'s own "no further parameter to narrow" shape) and `cap::decl::TaskBranch{}`/`cap::decl::TaskBranchCommit{}` added to `capability.hpp`; two new `Capability`-variant alternatives, two new `capability_kind` enumerators, and every genuinely exhaustive switch/`if constexpr` site over them updated -- `capability_kind_of()`, `capability_from_kind()`, `is_inert_for_text_derived_declassification()` (all `capability.hpp`), plus `policy_reachability.hpp`'s own `capability_kind_name()` (found by grepping the whole `include/` tree for every site that pattern-matches the variant/enum, not assumed). `StartTaskBranchTool`/`RunInTaskBranchTool`/`DiscardTaskBranchTool` now declare `Capabilities<cap::decl::TaskBranch>`; `CommitTaskBranchTool` alone declares both tags (a commit can only ever operate on a handle `start` produced, enforced by the tool's own ceiling, not by any relationship between the two kinds themselves). THE REAL FINDING (not merely mechanical): giving the tools a real ceiling makes this a genuine DOUBLE gate, not a straight swap -- before this ADR, `invoke_tool()`'s step 4/7 had nothing to bind (`declared_capabilities()` was empty), so the EXISTING dynamic opt-in gate (`is_bound()` + `bind_task_branch_tools()`) alone was sufficient to reach all four verbs through the real pipeline with NO capability grant at all; confirmed directly, not assumed, that `tests/test_task_branch_tools.cpp`'s own pre-existing real-pipeline section passed an explicitly EMPTY `CapabilitySet` and would now be rejected unchanged. A real, disclosed BEHAVIOR CHANGE to an API surface ADR-114 shipped the SAME DAY, deliberately made now rather than deferred again: ADR-114's own §5/§6 name zero real production callers anywhere outside that one test file, making this the lowest-risk moment this contract will ever be to change. Fixed the one file that needed it (`tests/test_task_branch_tools.cpp` section [6] now grants both tags) and added a NEW section [7] proving the fail-closed direction end-to-end: a session with the dynamic gate satisfied but an explicitly empty `CapabilitySet` gets `start_task_branch` rejected as a real `role::tool` error result (`start_run()` itself still completes normally -- the rejection is an ordinary error `ToolResult`, not a thrown/aborted run), no `handle_id` ever leaks into history, and zero `BranchCost` is spent (the rejection happens strictly before `call_sandbox()`). Sanity-checked the new test the same way this design line always does: temporarily reverted `StartTaskBranchTool`'s ceiling back to the ADR-114 zero-`Capabilities<...>` shape and confirmed all three of section [7]'s new assertions genuinely FAIL against that pre-fix code, then restored and re-confirmed a full pass. Searched the whole `tests/` tree for any hard-coded exhaustive count over `capability_kind`/`Capability`: none found; `agent_library_manifest.hpp`'s discovery registry checked directly and confirmed to need no new row (task-branch tools aren't represented there under any name today, pre-existing scope, not a regression). `RunCommandTool` itself is explicitly OUT of scope -- this closes only the ADR-114 §2 residual named for the task-branch tools, not a broader reconsideration of `RunCommandTool`'s own, separately-decided zero-ceiling design. No new exported vocabulary (the two new `cap::`/`cap::decl::` types follow the identical fieldless-marker shape `naming_lint.py` already accepts for `Entropy`/`Elicit` with no special-case entries needed). |

A REAL, NEW REGRESSION found and fixed while verifying, not merely anticipated: building `agentengine_sandboxed_shell_chat` after the change hit a genuine, ADR-119-caused MSVC `C1128` (COFF section-count limit) -- confirmed via `git stash` that the unmodified tree built clean -- fixed with the identical, already-established `/bigobj` remedy `agentengine_cli_chat` already uses (~~that file's own separate, pre-existing `C1128` was independently confirmed unrelated and unchanged, the same way~~ -- **corrected by ADR-122**: never a real defect, stale-build-artifact false positive). | [121](ADR-121-linux-verification-adr116.md) | ADR-116 (the `ComposedContextProvider` cross-session identity fix, including its own same-day red-team's tag-laundering and ABA fixes) landed on Windows/MSVC only and was never independently re-checked on Linux by any ADR since -- does the fix, especially the ABA-hardened monotonic-counter identity tag, hold on a real, independently-obtained GCC 14.2.0 toolchain and allocator? | **Proposed — real verification pass, no production code changed.** Fast-forwarded ADR-120's own `/root/ae-verify` WSL2/GCC-14.2.0 checkout to this branch's exact tip (`35fe8dd`) -- documentation-only at the code level, since ADR-116's own code had already ridden along during ADR-118's own earlier, wider fast-forward. `test_session_builder` is `AGENTENGINE_WITH_HTTPS`-gated (links `provider_http_client` transitively) -- reconfigured with that ON just long enough to build and run it (the same toggle-and-revert pattern ADR-120 established), compiles clean (one benign GCC `-Wself-move` warning on B21a's own deliberate self-assignment test, which MSVC has no equivalent for). **`test_session_builder`: ALL PASS**, including every ADR-116-relevant check: B20 (the original cross-session refusal), B23/B24 (the red-team's tag-laundering fixes, two-hop and three-hop), and -- the part most likely to be allocator-dependent and least safe to assume "probably also holds" -- **B25's own ABA scenario, genuinely reproduced on a SECOND, independent allocator (glibc, not MSVC's CRT)**: a destroyed session's heap slot really does get reused by a new, unrelated session on this platform too, and the monotonic-counter identity tag (not the original raw-address tag) correctly prevents the stale match from leaking content, confirmed by the test's own assertion rather than assumed to generalize from the original Windows-only finding. Every other pre-existing check in the file (B21a/b/c/d, B22) also passes unchanged. Closes ADR-116's own Linux-verification gap completely and unconditionally -- `test_session_builder` has no Docker/containerd dependency at all, so there is no environment-caused residual of its own. |
| [122](ADR-122-cli-chat-c1128-was-never-real.md) | ADR-114 and ADR-119 both hit MSVC `error C1128` building `agentengine_cli_chat` and both concluded, via `git stash`, that it was a real, pre-existing, unrelated large-translation-unit limit -- asked to actually fix it, is that true, or is something else going on? | **Proposed — root-caused and confirmed fixed with ZERO code changes.** The main Windows build directory this whole session's work used has `AGENTENGINE_BUILD_PYTHON_RUNNER=ON` but `AGENTENGINE_WITH_HTTPS=OFF` (confirmed via `CMakeCache.txt`) -- so `agentengine_cli_chat` (which needs BOTH) is never actually created by the current `CMakeLists.txt`'s own `add_executable()` call. Yet `cmake --build --target agentengine_cli_chat` still "worked" (produced a real C1128) because of a genuine, generalizable Visual-Studio-generator quirk: when a target is later excluded by a CACHE-VARIABLE toggle (not a source edit), CMake does not delete its previously-generated `.vcxproj` -- the file is orphaned on disk, and `--target <name>` resolves to it BY NAME with no check that it is still part of the live solution. Confirmed directly: the stale, orphaned `agentengine_cli_chat.vcxproj` had ZERO `/bigobj` occurrences, even though `CMakeLists.txt` has carried a working `if(MSVC) target_compile_options(agentengine_cli_chat PRIVATE /bigobj) endif()` block since ADR-114 itself -- the stale file predates that fix and was never regenerated to pick it up, because this directory's cache has excluded the target ever since. The `git stash` methodology both prior ADRs used was a real, sound comparison of the WRONG thing: it correctly answers "is this failure caused by my change" (no) while never asking "is this `.vcxproj` even what CMake would generate for the CURRENT configuration" (it wasn't). Reconfiguring with `cmake -DAGENTENGINE_WITH_HTTPS=ON .` (the directory already had `AGENTENGINE_BUILD_PYTHON_RUNNER=ON`) regenerates a FRESH `.vcxproj` -- confirmed 4 `/bigobj` occurrences where the stale one had zero. A from-scratch clean rebuild (intermediate directory and output binary deleted first, to rule out stale-object-file luck) **compiles and links `agentengine_cli_chat` with zero errors**, only the same pre-existing, unrelated warnings every other build of this file produces; ran the resulting `.exe` directly and confirmed it starts and exits cleanly with the expected missing-API-key message -- a genuinely functional binary, not merely a successful link. Corrected ADR-114's and ADR-119's own disclosure passages (strikethrough + pointer here, matching this session's established pattern) since both had asserted a real defect that was actually a stale-build-artifact false positive. Named a real, generalizable residual for future verification passes in this repo: a `--target` build succeeding or failing in a long-lived build directory tells you nothing about whether it reflects the CURRENT `CMakeLists.txt`/source tree unless the target's own gating cache variables are checked directly first. |
| [123](ADR-123-fork-from-reentrant-self-deadlock.md) | ADR-102's own fix for `AgentSession::fork_from()`'s cross-thread serialization gap introduced a disclosed-not-fixed hazard: a 100%-reproducible self-deadlock if `fork_from()` is ever called reentrantly, same thread, from inside an in-flight round on the session being forked from -- worth closing now, and how, given the two options the original disclosure named (broader `AsyncMutex` owner-tracking vs. a `fork_from()`-local guard)? | **Proposed — implemented, verified (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild (zero errors) and full `ctest` clean (285/286, same pre-existing unrelated gap), `naming_lint.py` clean. **Linux-verified, ADR-125**: section [3] passes completely on real GCC 14.2.0, a full, unconditional proof (no Docker dependency). SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (ADR-123 §7): a real gap found in the fix's own safety argument, disclosed rather than code-fixed this pass.** A third, cleaner option than either originally named: PURELY ADDITIVE owner-thread tracking on `AsyncMutex` itself -- a new `is_held_by_current_thread()` query, written only at the two places `held_` already transitions (`LockAwaiter::await_resume()`, `unlock()`'s two free-the-mutex branches), changing nothing for any of `AsyncMutex`'s many other real callers unless they explicitly opt in. `fork_from()` now skips re-acquiring `source.session_mutex_` when the calling thread already holds it -- I1 already guarantees no other thread can be touching `source` concurrently in that case. Neither of the two already-tested behaviors (`test_rt_agent_session_fork_from_serialization.cpp`'s own [1]/[2]) changed; a new section [3] proves the previously-deadlocking reentrant case now completes correctly, using a BOUNDED wait (not an infinite join) so a real regression fails the check instead of hanging the whole suite -- sanity-checked by reverting the fix and confirming section [3] genuinely times out (100%, not a rare race) before restoring. A real fixture-design lesson found the hard way: `AgentSession` is a CONCEPT-CONSTRAINED template, so a self-referential `ReentrantChatClient` fixture cannot name `AgentSession<ReentrantChatClient>` anywhere in its own declarative region (even as a bare pointer TYPE) while still incomplete -- fixed with a `void*` field, cast back to the real type only inside the deferred-compiled body of `chat()` itself. Full rebuild/ctest clean despite touching the two most foundational headers in the codebase (`async_mutex.hpp`/`agent_session.hpp`). **SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM finding (ADR-123 §7)**: the same-thread-throughout reentrant case is genuinely, completely fixed (re-confirmed by re-reverting and re-restoring the fix), and the `void*` fixture cast is well-defined -- but `owner_` is stamped ONCE, at lock acquisition, to whichever OS thread is physically running then, never re-stamped afterward; `rt/block_on.hpp`'s own file banner documents (and real `RunCommandTool`/`AsyncQuota` contention already exercises) that a coroutine's continuation can resume on a DIFFERENT OS thread than the one that suspended it. If `source`'s round hops threads inside `chat()` (a real, I/O-bound `ChatClient::chat()` is exactly this shape) before a reentrant `fork_from()` call, `is_held_by_current_thread()` false-negatives and the call self-deadlocks again -- empirically confirmed with a throwaway, uncommitted repro (forced thread hop via a custom awaiter, driven through the real `block_on()`; removed after use, `git diff --stat` confirmed a clean tree). Not reachable by any real call site today (same status as the ORIGINAL pre-ADR-123 hazard), and a general fix needs coroutine/round identity rather than OS-thread identity (checked directly: a `thread_local` marker would share the identical blind spot) -- judged out of proportion for a same-day round against the two most foundational concurrency primitives in the codebase, so the overstated safety-argument language in both `agent_session.hpp`'s `fork_from()` comment and `async_mutex.hpp`'s `is_held_by_current_thread()` comment was corrected to precisely scope the guarantee instead, rather than landing a speculative, unreviewed architecture change. Full rebuild/ctest re-run after the comment corrections: zero errors, 285/286 unchanged. |
| [124](ADR-124-task-branch-concurrent-dispatch-stress-test.md) | ADR-114 §5/§6 disclosed that `MandatorySandboxProvider`'s task-branch verbs were only ever exercised sequentially, never under genuine two-OS-thread concurrent dispatch on one instance, unlike `AsyncQuota`'s own `block_on()` fix (ADR-102 Phase 4) which got a dedicated stress test -- does `task_branch_mutex_` actually hold up under real concurrent dispatch? | **Proposed — a real, two-OS-thread stress test added and verified against a REAL Docker daemon; no production code changed. Full rebuild/ctest clean (285/286, same pre-existing unrelated gap), `naming_lint.py` clean. **Linux-verified, ADR-125**: section [1], the test's own core, Docker-independent claim, passes completely on real GCC 14.2.0 -- 8 concurrent threads, 5 rounds, correct accounting every time; sections [2]/[3]'s own Docker-dependent check remains unverified there, same pre-existing environment gap. SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (ADR-124 §7): clean bill of health -- this ADR's own `exclusivity_` explanation verified accurate; the real finding from the same round landed against ADR-123 instead.** `task_branches_` is genuinely per-instance, private state -- never shared across sessions the way `AsyncQuota` legitimately is -- so under I1 this mutex should never see real contention in NORMAL usage; the test's value is a genuine POSITIVE CONTROL, not closing a live, reachable bug. New `tests/test_task_branch_concurrent_dispatch.cpp`: [1] eight threads calling `start_task_branch()` concurrently (all succeed, exactly 8 distinct branches, exactly 8 `BranchCost` consumed); [2] `run_in_task_branch()`/`commit_task_branch()` dispatched concurrently on two independently pre-seeded handles (both genuinely execute against real Docker/Ledger state with zero cross-contamination, each post-condition confirmed via a real follow-up call); [3] concurrent `discard_task_branch()`/`start_task_branch()` (the discarded handle genuinely gone, the new one genuinely present, both confirmed via real follow-up calls). AN HONEST, DISCLOSED NEGATIVE RESULT from the usual revert-and-confirm-fail sanity check, found by actually trying it: removing `task_branch_mutex_` from `start_task_branch()`/`discard_task_branch()` entirely did NOT reliably reproduce a detectable failure across 8 total runs (2- and 8-thread variants) -- traced to `SandboxRuntime::spawn_child_branch()`'s own `exclusivity_` lock already serializing the dominant, slow work for most of these call paths, leaving too narrow a real-I/O-timing window to hit reliably. Disclosed as an EMPIRICAL LIMITATION of testing a `std::map` race against real I/O-dominated timing, explicitly NOT as evidence the lock is unnecessary -- concurrent unsynchronized `std::map` mutation is textbook UB regardless of empirical hit rate, and `task_branch_mutex_` remains correct by construction (code review, not empirical reproduction, grounds this ADR's own confidence). Both locks restored immediately after the investigation, confirmed via `git diff --stat` matching the committed state exactly. **SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (ADR-124 §7)**: independently re-read `spawn_child_branch()`/`start_task_branch()` and confirmed the `exclusivity_`-serializes-the-dominant-work explanation holds up exactly as described; reran the stress test against a real Docker daemon (all checks passed, unchanged) and the full suite (285/286, same pre-existing gap) -- no issue found in this ADR's own scope. |
| [125](ADR-125-linux-verification-adr123-adr124.md) | ADR-123's reentrancy fix and ADR-124's concurrent-dispatch stress test both landed on Windows/MSVC only -- does the fix genuinely close the deadlock on a real, independently-obtained GCC 14.2.0 toolchain and allocator, and does the stress test's own core claim reproduce there too? | **Proposed — real verification pass, no production code changed.** Fast-forwarded ADR-121's own `/root/ae-verify` WSL2/GCC-14.2.0 checkout to this branch's exact tip (`8ee9eef`). **Full rebuild: 340/340 targets, zero errors** -- both touched headers (`async_mutex.hpp`/`agent_session.hpp`) are used essentially everywhere, so this genuinely exercised the change against the whole tree. `test_rt_agent_session_fork_from_serialization`: **ALL CHECKS PASSED**, including the new reentrancy section [3] -- a COMPLETE, unconditional proof (no Docker dependency at all). `test_task_branch_concurrent_dispatch`: **section [1] -- the test's own core, Docker-independent claim -- PASSED completely**: 5 rounds of 8 genuine concurrent OS threads calling `start_task_branch()`, `task_branches_` correctly populated and `BranchCost` correctly accounted every round. Sections [2]/[3] partially ran: the one check requiring real container exec (`run_in_task_branch()`) failed on the same, already-diagnosed Docker-CLI-reachability gap ADR-115/118/120/121 all name, while every OTHER check in those sections -- including `commit_task_branch()` and the full concurrent `discard_task_branch()`/`start_task_branch()` pair, both pure Ledger operations -- passed cleanly. Full `ctest`: **183 total (182 baseline + this pass's own new test), 6 failures** -- the same 5 pre-existing failures plus `test_task_branch_concurrent_dispatch` itself (on the identical, single Docker-dependent check), zero regression anywhere else. Closes ADR-123's Linux-verification residual completely; closes ADR-124's for the part that matters (section [1]), leaving only the same pre-existing, disclosed Docker gap unclosed there. |
| [126](ADR-126-task-branch-durability-recovery.md) | ADR-114 §5/§6 disclosed, inherited unchanged from the prove-phase original's own finding 6: "`active_`'s table has no durability of its own -- a process crash mid-task-branch strands it from this tool surface's own verbs even in a durable-`Store` `Ledger` configuration." Fixable without inventing any new persistence mechanism, given the Ledger already durably tracks exactly what's needed? | **Proposed — implemented, verified (Windows/MSVC, Docker-independent), full rebuild (zero errors) and full `ctest` clean (286/287, same pre-existing unrelated gap), `naming_lint.py` clean. SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (§7): one real MUST-FIX found and fixed — the original `recover_orphaned_task_branches()` matched an orphan by name PREFIX alone and its own comment claimed a grandchild (a branch forked from another already-a-child branch) "would carry a DIFFERENT prefix and is correctly left alone," which was FALSE (`Ledger::branch_from()` names children unconditionally, so a grandchild's name DOES start with the same prefix) — fixed by additionally requiring no further `/` in the matched suffix, proven with a new Phase D ([5]/[6]) building a genuine grandchild via direct `Ledger::branch_from()` calls, reverting the fix line alone reproduced the exact expected `FAIL`s. The I2/cross-owner-authorization boundary itself (item (1)/(2) of the review) checked out clean under genuine adversarial construction (two-owner scenario, `Ledger::create_root_branch()`'s and `Ledger::branch_from()`'s exact naming rules traced directly) — a `/` can never appear immediately after a root's own numeric id from `create_root_branch()` alone, so no unrelated root or its children can ever collide with another root's own child-prefix. Re-verified 286/287 and a clean full rebuild after the fix. **Linux-verified, ADR-127**: a complete, unconditional pass on real GCC 14.2.0 -- no Docker dependency at all, so every phase, including the grandchild-exclusion regression, is fully proven there too.** No new persistence mechanism needed: `Ledger::branch_from()` names children deterministically (`"<parent>/child-<id>-<seq>"`), and `load_durable_state()` already restores every branch with no live handle anywhere (a crash or a clean exit, indistinguishable and both fine) into `orphaned_branches()` at Ledger construction time -- exactly the set `task_branches_` should contain. `bind_sandbox()` now calls a new `recover_orphaned_task_branches()`: prefix-match `orphaned_branches()` against this root's own deterministic child prefix, reclaim each via the ALREADY-PROVEN `SandboxRuntime::reclaim_orphaned_child()` (the same primitive `commit_task_branch()`'s own conflict-retry path already uses, independently re-checking ACL authorization per candidate), best-effort (one failed reclaim is not fatal to the others). A REAL, PRECISE SCOPE BOUNDARY found empirically, not assumed: the test's first version used `commit_task_branch()` to prove recovery and genuinely failed -- not with `task_branch_unknown_handle` (recovery broken) but with a real `ledger.merge_tree_load_failed`, tracing to a DIFFERENT, already-disclosed limitation `tests/test_ledger.cpp` itself already states (`durable_dir` persists branch/ACL bookkeeping only, never blob/tree CONTENT). Redesigned around `discard_task_branch()` (a pure branch-table erase, no content needed) for the core metadata-durability proof, and kept the original finding as a NEW, permanent, precise assertion: a second recovered handle's own `commit_task_branch()` call must fail with EXACTLY `ledger.merge_tree_load_failed`, not `task_branch_unknown_handle` -- distinguishing "recovery didn't work" from "a separate, already-disclosed gap" by evidence. New `tests/test_task_branch_durability_recovery.cpp` mirrors `test_ledger.cpp`'s own established "destroy + reconstruct against the SAME `durable_dir`" crash-simulation methodology (letting a `Ledger`/`BranchHandle` go out of scope normally is "at least as strong as a real crash," since a real process exit runs no destructors either), Docker-independent throughout via a `FakeSurface` stand-in. Sanity-checked by commenting out the new recovery call, rebuilding, and confirming all three core recovery assertions genuinely fail -- then restoring and re-confirming a full pass. Rebuilt and reran every other `bind_sandbox()` caller (`test_task_branch_tools`, `test_task_branch_concurrent_dispatch`, `test_mandatory_sandbox_provider`, `test_composed_sandbox_providers_live`, `test_mandatory_sandbox_provider_composed`) against real Docker where applicable: all pass unchanged, confirming the new call is a genuine no-op for the ordinary, non-crash case. `BranchCost` deliberately NOT re-charged on recovery (a fresh `AsyncQuota` after a restart has no memory of pre-crash spend -- correct given `AsyncQuota`'s own pre-existing, unrelated non-durability, not a new gap). ~~Root-branch recovery remains the caller's own, separate, already-disclosed responsibility (ADR-102's own "`bind_branch_only()` is real follow-on work")~~ **Closed by ADR-128**, not conflated with the narrower gap this ADR closes. |
| [127](ADR-127-linux-verification-adr126.md) | ADR-126's durability-recovery fix (including the same-day red-team's own grandchild-exclusion MUST-FIX) landed on Windows/MSVC only -- does recovery genuinely hold on a real, independently-obtained GCC 14.2.0 toolchain and filesystem, including the adversarial grandchild-orphan regression case? | **Proposed — real verification pass, no production code changed.** Fast-forwarded ADR-125's own `/root/ae-verify` WSL2/GCC-14.2.0 checkout to this branch's exact tip (`2425fcb`). **Full rebuild: 41/41 affected targets, zero errors**, `test_task_branch_durability_recovery` compiling and linking clean on the first attempt. `test_task_branch_durability_recovery`: **ALL CHECKS PASSED**, run directly -- a COMPLETE, unconditional proof (entirely Docker-independent via its own `FakeSurface` stand-in). Every phase held on real GCC/glibc/filesystem behavior: the destroy-and-reconstruct crash simulation, recovery of two genuine orphaned child branches, `discard_task_branch()` on a recovered handle succeeding through the normal tool surface, `commit_task_branch()` on a second recovered handle correctly reaching real merge logic and failing on the precise, already-disclosed content-durability gap (not `task_branch_unknown_handle`), an ordinary discard's own `BranchCost` refund, AND the same-day red-team's own MUST-FIX regression -- a genuine grandchild orphan (built via direct `Ledger::branch_from()` calls) correctly staying unrecovered while a true direct child still recovers normally. Full `ctest`: **184 total (183 baseline + this pass's own new test), 6 failures** -- the IDENTICAL 6 failures ADR-125's own pass already established (5 pre-existing Docker-CLI-reachability failures plus `test_task_branch_concurrent_dispatch`'s own single expected Docker-dependent check), zero regression anywhere else, `test_task_branch_durability_recovery` itself explicitly Passed. Closes ADR-126's own Linux-verification residual completely and unconditionally. |
| [128](ADR-128-root-branch-recovery.md) | ADR-126 §5 disclosed "root-branch recovery remains the caller's own, separate, already-disclosed responsibility" (tracing to ADR-102's own "`bind_branch_only()` is real follow-on work") -- `bind_sandbox()` still takes an already-resolved `BranchHandle`, so a host recovering a session after a crash has to hand-compute `Ledger`'s own root-name format and hand-sequence `reclaim_orphaned_branch()`/`create_root_branch()` itself. Automatable the same way the CHILD half was (ADR-126), without a new persistence mechanism or widened authority? | **Proposed — implemented, verified (Windows/MSVC, Docker-independent), full rebuild (zero errors) and full `ctest` clean (288 total, 1 failure, the established pre-existing pandas/matplotlib gap -- zero regression), `naming_lint.py` clean. SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (§7): one real MUST-FIX found and fixed** — `Ledger::reclaim_orphaned_branch()`/`abandon_orphaned_branch()`'s own authorization check was keyed to content-digest ACL membership, not true branch ownership: every never-committed branch (root or child, ANY owner) has its head tree at the digest of an empty `Tree{}` — content-addressed, hence IDENTICAL across owners — and `insert_acl_root_bounded()` adds every owner who has ever created any fresh branch into that SAME shared ACL entry, so any owner who had ever created their OWN root branch could reclaim a DIFFERENT owner's still-orphaned one using only their own identity. This directly falsifies the "cross-owner name/ACL mismatch is impossible by construction" claim below as a safety property (the narrower literal claim -- names never collide -- is still true). Empirically confirmed with a real, temporary probe (owner B, who had only ever created their own unrelated root branch, successfully reclaimed owner A's orphaned "root-A" via a direct `Ledger` call) before any fix; fixed by adding a private `is_branch_owner()` check against `BranchState::created_by_id` (the same field `reap_pending_abandons()` already treats as authoritative) in place of the digest check, with the identical ancestor-of-owner allowance `authorized_for()` already extends elsewhere. Re-verified: probe fails closed post-fix, `test_root_branch_recovery`/`test_ledger`/`test_task_branch_durability_recovery` all pass unchanged (every existing legitimate reclaim already reclaims with the branch's own creator identity), full rebuild zero errors, full `ctest` 288/288 minus the one pre-existing gap, `naming_lint.py` clean, and a `git stash`-based revert-to-confirm-fail cycle reproduced the exact original bug before the fix was restored. New `MandatorySandboxProvider::bind_root_branch()`: computes `owner`'s own deterministic root-branch name exactly the way `Ledger::create_root_branch()` itself does, checks `ledger.orphaned_branches()` for it, and reclaims (crash-recovery case) or creates (first-ever-bind case) accordingly before delegating into the existing, unmodified `bind_sandbox()`. A genuinely interesting, unplanned structural finding while designing this, not a new check added: root branches cannot suffer a cross-owner name/ACL mismatch through the public API BY CONSTRUCTION, since `create_root_branch(owner, ...)` derives both the name and the initial ACL grant from the SAME `owner.id()` in the same call. New `tests/test_root_branch_recovery.cpp`: [1] a fresh owner/disambiguator takes the create-path and the resulting binding is genuinely functional end to end (not merely "returns success"); [2] THE CORE CLAIM -- after a simulated crash (same "destroy + reconstruct against the SAME durable_dir" methodology ADR-126's own test established), a second call for the SAME owner/disambiguator reattaches to the SAME branch, with NO manual Ledger-level reclaim call anywhere in the test; [3] two different disambiguators for the same owner stay genuinely independent (positive control). A REAL, HONEST MISS FOUND DURING THE MANDATORY SANITY CHECK ITSELF, not merely a "the fix works" story: the test's first-draft check [2] proved recovery only via "the pre-crash task-branch handle is still discoverable," reasoning a wrongly-always-creating `bind_root_branch()` would leave it unrecoverable -- reverting the reclaim branch to force create-unconditionally and rebuilding to confirm the expected failure instead produced a full, UNEXPECTED PASS, because ADR-126's own child-orphan recovery matches by NAME PREFIX alone and the root's deterministic name is identical either way, making that proof blind to the exact distinction being tested. Root-caused (a "does the recovered state look right" proof is not automatically the same claim as "did the right code path run"), not patched around: added a second, direct differentiator (`reclaim_orphaned_branch()` erases the name from `Ledger`'s own `orphaned_from_restart_` set; `create_root_branch()` never touches it) -- re-ran the same forced-break, confirmed the NEW assertion failed exactly and only as expected, then restored and reconfirmed a full pass (`git diff --stat` matched the real fix exactly, no leftover artifacts). Honestly disclosed, not closed: a second `bind_root_branch()` call for the SAME owner/disambiguator while the first branch is still LIVE (not yet orphaned) reaches the create-path and silently overwrites it -- the EXACT SAME pre-existing hazard `Ledger::create_root_branch()` itself already has (ADR-102 §43.2), not a new one this method introduces; and the fuller ADR-099 §1 item 2 claim (an entirely never-bound session should still own a branch) remains genuinely out of scope -- a session-lifecycle question, not a durability one. **Linux-verified, ADR-129**: a complete, unconditional pass on real GCC 14.2.0 -- both `bind_root_branch()` itself and the red-team's own `is_branch_owner()` cross-owner fix hold, neither has any Docker dependency in the tests that exercise them, `test_root_branch_recovery` and `test_ledger` both confirmed passing directly. |
| [130](ADR-130-content-durability-conformer.md) | This session's own prior design-research pass (`docs/planning/content-durability-conformer-design-draft.md`, not an ADR) recommended porting the prove-phase `probe::FileWorktreeObjectStore` into production, closing the "content durability" half of the gap ADR-102/126/128 each named and declined to close. Does that port actually work, and does it hold up under that document's own named gate criteria (§5), executed for real? | **Proposed — implemented, verified (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild (zero errors, 317 targets) and full `ctest` clean (291 total, 1 pre-existing failure, zero regression), `naming_lint.py` clean. SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (§7): one real MUST-FIX found and fixed** — both cross-process tests' `std::system()` self-relaunch quoting (`test_content_durability_cross_process.cpp`, `test_identity_durability_precondition.cpp`) used a Windows-`cmd.exe`-specific double-quote-wrap workaround whose own comment claimed it was also a harmless no-op on POSIX `/bin/sh -c` — FALSE, reproduced directly against a real `sh -c` with the exact command strings both files build: the extra wrap collapses the executable path and every argument into one bad, nonexistent command name (`No such file or directory`, exit 127), because it shifts POSIX quote-state parity by one. This would have made both tests fail unconditionally the moment this design line's own next Linux-verification ADR ran them, not merely stay unverified. **Fixed** by making the extra wrap `#ifdef _WIN32`-only in both files, verified via the same direct `sh -c` reproduction (the fixed form parses into the correct four arguments) since no Linux build of this repo was available this round; Windows behavior reconfirmed unchanged (both tests rerun three times, unaffected). Also closed gate item 4's own named coverage gap (the same-digest concurrent-write sub-case `test_content_durability_concurrency.cpp` never exercised): a new, permanent `[1b]` section (16 real threads, barrier-synchronized, 3 MiB same-digest race, checked directly against on-disk bytes) found this SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION — even under a deliberately sabotaged `put_blob()` with its atomic-rename discipline and mutex both removed, content-addressing's own "same digest implies byte-identical content" invariant plus Windows file-handle-follows-object semantics across a rename prevented any corruption across ~6,000 racing attempts; the new check's own teeth were confirmed by a separate, unrelated sabotage (writing only half the bytes) that it correctly caught. Everything else checked out clean: `FileWorktreeObjectStore` port fidelity and the `unique_ptr<mutex>` claim re-traced and confirmed; `decode_tree()` adversarially probed with six crafted/truncated on-disk files (all failed closed with `worktree.tree_decode_failed`, zero crashes, a positive control still decoded correctly); I2/I3 re-traced across every `store_` access site in `ledger.hpp` with no bypass found; the `[2c]` concurrency hazard and the identity-precondition proof both reran deterministically. Still NOT Judged — no Linux verification yet (unchanged residual; the round found and fixed a Linux-blocking defect but did not itself run on Linux).** A REAL, MATERIAL SCOPE BOUNDARY found while building this, not assumed going in: `Ledger<Store>` is genuinely store-agnostic via its own template parameter, but `SandboxRuntime`/`MandatorySandboxProvider` -- the production tool-surface layer this whole session's task-branch line actually lives in -- are hardcoded to `Ledger<>` (the default in-memory store) throughout, NOT `Store`-generic despite the design draft's own gate item 2 assuming `bind_root_branch()` could be used for the proof. This ADR does not attempt that integration (a real, separate, larger follow-on templatizing the two most heavily-verified files in this session's own design line) -- it proves content durability genuinely works at the raw `Ledger<Store>` level instead, where the "pure Store substitution" claim is actually true. New `agentengine::FileWorktreeObjectStore` (`core/file_worktree_object_store.hpp`): a real port of the already-proven, standalone prove-phase prototype -- same loose-object storage shape, same atomic-rename write discipline, same path-traversal gate, `unique_ptr<mutex>` indirection explicitly RE-CHECKED (not assumed) as still load-bearing given `Ledger`'s own constructor unconditionally moves its `Store` parameter. THREE new, real tests, each closing a distinct gate item with EXECUTED evidence: `test_content_durability_cross_process.cpp` -- a genuine TWO-REAL-OS-PROCESS proof (one executable self-relaunches via `std::system()` to play both roles) that a `merge()` on branches recovered entirely from real disk, in a genuinely different process than the one that committed the content, succeeds (not `ledger.merge_tree_load_failed`) and the merged content is byte-exact through the ACL-gated `get_blob_safe()` path, closing gate items 1/2. `test_content_durability_concurrency.cpp` -- real concurrent OS threads prove content writes are safe across independent store instances (200/200 byte-exact), while GENUINE concurrent `Ledger` construction (not merely sequential multi-instance use, proven separately as a negative-result baseline first) reliably loses exactly one of two branches from the durable record -- the SAME, pre-existing, UNCHANGED metadata-bookkeeping race `ADR-128` §2's own "still-live double-bind" disclosure already names, confirmed neither worsened nor fixed by adding durable content, closing gate item 3 with real reproduction rather than restated disclosure. `test_identity_durability_precondition.cpp` -- a real, adversarial, two-process proof of gate item 6 (the design draft's own most consequential named risk): with `IdentityAuthority` configured WITHOUT a durable dir in either process, an unrelated "attacker" process's own honestly-minted identity (id 1, since fresh in-memory allocation always restarts at 1) genuinely reads a DIFFERENT owner's real secret content through the production `get_blob_safe()` path -- a live, working leak on current code, not hypothetical; configuring `IdentityAuthority::bootstrap()` with the SAME durable identity directory in both processes correctly, genuinely closes it (attacker id 2, `ledger.blob_access_denied`). A real, honest (if informal) `MergeCost` measurement for gate item 4, via a temporary probe built, run once, and deleted afterward (the same pattern this session's own red-team rounds already use): 200 `merge()` calls, in-memory 6.63ms vs. file-backed 10.42ms -- a 1.6x, +3.8ms/call delta, disclosed as a single-machine Debug-build number, not a rigorous cross-platform benchmark.
