SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM: two further real defects found and fixed, one a genuine regression this ADR's own first draft introduced.** (1) A shared, digest-derived temp filename made the new cleanup-on-failure logic actively unsafe under concurrent same-digest writes -- one racing writer's failure-path cleanup could delete a SIBLING writer's still-in-flight temp file, reproduced directly (`test_content_durability_concurrency`'s own `[1b]` section genuinely failed/hung). Fixed with a real per-call-unique temp-filename suffix (`next_temp_file_suffix()`, mirroring `docker_execution_surface.hpp`'s own `g_next_container_seq` pattern), independent of digest collisions -- verified stable across 8+ consecutive runs after the fix. (2) A deeper, previously-undiscovered cross-platform gap: two independent `FileWorktreeObjectStore` instances racing a rename onto the SAME digest-named path for identical content genuinely fails on Windows (`MoveFileExW` denies a rename onto a destination another rename just populated, unlike POSIX's silently-atomic-replace semantics this code assumed) -- confirmed via a dedicated probe (833/1000 racing renames succeeded outright, 167 hit exactly this "access denied" path, 0 corruption). Made the new hard-failure behavior a real regression for `create_root_branch()`'s own always-identical empty-tree case (`[2c]`, 7/10 runs failing after fix (1) alone). Fixed via readback-and-recompute on a rename failure: content-addressing guarantees that if the destination now holds bytes matching this digest, a sibling writer's rename already won with byte-identical content, so this call correctly reports success instead of a spurious error -- deliberately verifies CONTENT, not bare `exists()`. Verified stable across 10+ consecutive runs of the full `[1]`/`[1b]`/`[2]`/`[2b]`/`[2c]` suite after this fix. (3) A separate, pre-existing dedup-check gap found while re-tracing the store's own logic: bare `exists()` treated any filesystem object -- including a directory -- at the digest path as "already have this blob," silently skipping the write; fixed by switching to `is_regular_file()`, proven via a directory-landmine probe now failing closed correctly. |
| [138](ADR-138-posix-mount-escape-cleanup-parity.md) | The Windows-side ADR-014 mount-escape fix in this same PR (`worktree_mount_fs.cpp`) unwinds a just-created object through its own handle when a creating `CreateFileW` disposition plants something outside `mount_root` before the containment check runs -- but the identical bug, reachable via POSIX `open()` with `O_CREAT` following a dangling symlink to a location outside the mount (no shell access needed -- git tracks symlinks), was never closed on the POSIX sibling (`worktree_mount_fs_posix.cpp`, reached by `mediated_filesystem_adapter_posix.cpp`'s own `write_file()`). Close it, matching the Windows fix's intent? | **Proposed -- implemented (Linux-only file, could not be compiled/executed on this Windows session). SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM: static-only review found and closed one real, DETERMINISTIC logic bug in the first draft (not merely a theoretical race).** The first draft's `O_EXCL`-probe-then-fallback-reopen emulation (POSIX has no single-call equivalent to `CreateFileW`'s `ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS` atomic signal) left `created_new_object` unconditionally false for the `EEXIST` fallback-reopen branch -- but `EEXIST` only proves SOME entry already sits at the final path component, not whether that entry is a symlink to something that already exists (fallback creates nothing) or a DANGLING symlink (the fallback's own `O_CREAT` is exactly the call that creates a new object at the target, single-threaded, no race required). The escape was still correctly rejected to the caller, but the newly-planted file was silently left on disk -- the exact residual this whole ADR exists to close, still open for this one deterministic path. Fixed: `stat()` (follows symlinks) the pre-resolution path before the fallback reopen -- success means the target already exists (must NOT be treated as "created," or the unwind would delete an arbitrary pre-existing file reached through the symlink, a real, WORSE bug the alternative "always treat as created" fix was considered and rejected for); `ENOENT` means dangling, and `created_new_object` is set accordingly. **Real compilation/execution verification still outstanding** -- named explicitly as this ADR's own residual for a dedicated Linux-verify pass. |
| [139](ADR-139-docker-run-capture-timeout-and-cap.md) | `docker_cli_detail::run_capture()` -- used by EVERY `docker` CLI invocation this codebase makes, including model-facing `run_command` via `exec()` -- had no wall-clock timeout and no output-size cap, unlike its sibling `ctr_cli_detail::run_argv()` (containerd_execution_surface.hpp), which explicitly cites CLAUDE.md's machine-safety rule for both. A non-terminating contained process (`tail -f`) hung the calling coroutine forever while holding `SandboxRuntime::exclusivity_`. Give it real parity with its sibling? | **Proposed -- implemented, verified FOR REAL against a live Docker daemon (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild (zero errors) and full `ctest` clean (293 total, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure, zero regression), `naming_lint.py` clean.** Complete rewrite of both platform arms: POSIX now uses `posix_spawn` + a real pipe + a `poll()`-based bounded read loop with a 30s deadline/1 MiB cap, mirroring `ctr_cli_detail::run_argv()`'s own proven shape; on timeout, real `SIGKILL`+`waitpid`. Windows now uses `CreateProcessA` + an anonymous pipe + a `PeekNamedPipe`-based bounded read loop. **A real bug found and fixed by the implementer BEFORE red-team**: the first Windows draft wrapped `command` in the Microsoft CRT argv-quoting algorithm before handing it to `cmd.exe /c`, reasoning by analogy to `_popen`'s own internals -- WRONG: `cmd.exe`'s own `/c`-remainder parsing does not apply CRT-style backslash-quote unescaping, so re-quoting a `command` that already contained its own literal `"..."` (e.g. `docker exec <id> sh -c "<cmd>"`) silently corrupted every quoted argument, breaking `test_sandbox_runtime`/`test_docker_orphan_reap`/`test_mandatory_sandbox_provider`/`test_task_branch_tools`/`test_composed_sandbox_providers_live`/`test_task_branch_concurrent_dispatch` identically. Fixed by removing the re-quoting entirely (`"cmd.exe /c " + command` raw, matching what `_popen` actually does and this file's own pre-existing, already-tested shell-quoting discipline) -- all six tests pass clean after. **SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM found and fixed one further real, reproducible Windows-only resource leak**: `TerminateProcess()` on timeout only killed `cmd.exe`, not its real child (`docker.exe`) -- Windows has no `exec()`-style process-image replacement, so a killed `cmd.exe` left the actual Docker client (and the containerized process it was driving) running forever, proved live against a real daemon (`docker top` showing a leaked `tail` process after a "successful" 5s-timeout return). Fixed with a Windows Job Object (`CREATE_SUSPENDED` + `AssignProcessToJobObject()` before `ResumeThread()`, `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`) -- `TerminateJobObject()` now kills the whole process tree, verified via the same live-Docker probe showing 0 leaked processes after the fix, all six tests still passing. |
| [140](ADR-140-kata-backend-mount-precreate-symlink-escape.md) | `KataBackend::create()`'s SLICE 12 mount-point pre-creation loops (`/proc,/dev,/sys,...`, every caller-supplied `MountSpec::guest_path`, `/etc`+`/etc/hosts`) call `fs::create_directories()`/open an `ofstream` against paths under `rootfs_dir`, whose LOWER layer is an arbitrary, untrusted OCI image -- both follow symlinks by default, so a malicious image shipping e.g. `/etc` as a symlink to an absolute host path would write outside `rootfs_dir` on the real host. The existing lexical `.`/`..` rejection on `guest_path` doesn't cover a symlink. Close it, mirroring `real_filesystem_adapter.cpp`'s own sibling guard? | **Proposed -- implemented (Linux-only, KVM/Kata-gated file, could not be compiled/executed on this Windows session, and this session has no live Kata deployment either way). SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM: full static trace, CLEAN BILL OF HEALTH, no bug found.** New `escapes_rootfs_via_symlink()` lambda (walk to longest existing ancestor, canonicalize, verify containment against a once-computed `rootfs_canonical`), called before all three real `create_directories`/`ofstream` call sites, failing `create()` closed via the existing `cleanup_partial()` pattern on escape. Red-team confirmed by hand: the ancestor-walk terminates correctly, `fs::canonical` failure fails closed, `lexically_relative()` correctly rejects the naive sibling-prefix false-positive class (`/tmp/rootfs` vs `/tmp/rootfs2`), all three real call sites are guarded, the one unguarded `ofstream` (`workdir/"hosts"`) is correctly out of scope (not under the untrusted `rootfs_dir`). One minor gap noted, not fixed: no explicit disclosed TOCTOU-window comment between the check and the act, unlike ADR-138's own POSIX fix -- judged low real risk (a statically extracted image layer, not a live racing process), recorded as a residual. **Real compilation/execution verification still outstanding**, named explicitly for a dedicated Linux-verify pass. |
| [141](ADR-141-async-quota-child-share-double-spend-fix.md) | A final-review pass found `AsyncQuota<Kind>::try_consume()` let a child registered via `allocate_child_share()` ALSO spend directly against the PARENT quota using its own identity, checked only against the parent's whole `remaining_` pool -- not against how much was actually allocated to that child -- an unbounded draw. `release_child_share()` then blindly re-credited the caller-supplied amount unconditionally, with no knowledge of what had already flowed out through either channel. Together: mint unlimited quota. Not yet reachable by any real caller -- fix now, before one exists? | **Proposed -- implemented, verified (Windows/MSVC), full rebuild (zero errors) and full `ctest` clean (293 total, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure, zero regression), `naming_lint.py` clean.** `try_consume()` is now owner-only -- the direct-parent-spend channel for a registered child is removed entirely; a child must spend through the separate `AsyncQuota` object `allocate_child_share()` returned. `release_child_share()` now takes that object BY VALUE (moved, consumed) and credits back exactly its own real, mutation-tracked `remaining_`, never a caller-supplied number. `tests/test_identity_authority_grant.cpp` sections [8]/[9] rewritten to match. Sanity-checked at the interface level: reverting `async_quota.hpp` alone causes the rewritten test to genuinely fail to COMPILE against the old two-argument signature (`error C2660`), confirming the fix is load-bearing, not cosmetic. Blast-radius check: zero real production callers of the affected paths exist yet, confirmed via grep -- a genuinely risk-free-to-fix-now primitive. **SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM (ADR-142) found two further real gaps in this exact redesign** -- see that ADR's own row. |
| [142](ADR-142-async-quota-child-share-followon-redteam.md) | Does ADR-141's owner-only/move-only redesign hold up under further adversarial probing -- specifically, what happens on a SECOND `allocate_child_share()` for an already-live child identity, and what happens when the SAME child identity legitimately holds shares from TWO independent root quotas and a caller releases one against the wrong parent? | **Proposed -- real, reproduced adversarial findings, both fixed and re-verified same day. Full rebuild (zero errors) and full `ctest` clean (293 total, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure, zero regression), `naming_lint.py` clean.** (1) **Confirmed real**: a second allocation to an already-live child silently clobbers `children_[child.id()]`, permanently stranding the first share's quota -- reproduced (allocate 40, then 25 more to the same child before releasing either; the ledger only ever remembers 25, the first share's 40 units become permanently un-releasable and un-spendable). Fixed: `allocate_child_share()` now refuses a second live allocation outright (`async_quota.child_share_already_live`). (2) **Confirmed real**: `release_child_share()` matched only by `child.id()`, so the same child identity legitimately holding shares from two independent root quotas let `quota_x.release_child_share(std::move(share_from_quota_y))` succeed -- crediting the WRONG parent while stranding the other's own ledger entry. Fixed: a new `origin_mutex_` field, stamped with the allocating parent's own stable `mutex_.get()` at allocation time (stable across any later move of the parent, for the same reason this file's own `unique_ptr` indirection exists at all), checked by `release_child_share()` before touching the ledger (`async_quota.release_wrong_parent`). Both verified via a standalone adversarial probe: confirmed genuinely broken against a temporarily reverted baseline, confirmed closed post-fix, confirmed the legitimate release paths still work correctly afterward; probe deleted after use. Disclosed, not closed: neither new error code has PERMANENT regression-test coverage yet -- the adversarial probe that found and verified both fixes was temporary, not promoted into `tests/test_identity_authority_grant.cpp`. |
| [143](ADR-143-linux-verification-adr136-142.md) | ADR-136 through ADR-142 landed on Windows/MSVC in one consolidated final-review session -- two of the six touched Linux-only files (`worktree_mount_fs_posix.cpp`, ADR-138; `kata_backend.cpp`, ADR-140) had never even been COMPILED before, let alone run. Do all six actually hold on a second, independent compiler/OS, and do the two never-compiled files even build? | **Proposed -- real verification pass. Found and fixed ONE further real, previously-undiscovered bug (in ADR-138's own file, unrelated to and predating both that ADR and its own red-team round) -- every other claim held with zero source changes.** Fast-forwarded ADR-135's own `/root/ae-verify` WSL2/GCC-14.2.0 checkout to this branch's exact tip (`e5baf56`, an 18-file, 1650-insertion fast-forward from `2a84e8d`) -- one leftover, uncommitted local diff found first (the ADR-135 WIFEXITED fix, already shipped upstream through a different path), confirmed byte-identical via `git show` before being discarded, per this repo's own git-safety discipline. **ADR-138 (`worktree_mount_fs_posix.cpp`), the genuinely new claim**: full rebuild, 81/81 steps, zero errors, no source change needed for the create-and-unwind fix itself; the existing escape corpus passes clean. A dedicated, temporary, targeted probe (built, run, then deleted, zero leftover diff) directly proved both properties the file's own red-team round could only reason about: a dangling symlink to outside the mount is rejected AND its newly-created target genuinely unlinked; a symlink to an EXISTING outside file is rejected WITHOUT deleting it. **That same probe's third check surfaced a real, previously-undiscovered bug**: `O_TRUNC` is destructive at `open()`-time (an unconditional kernel side effect before this function's own containment check runs) -- a symlink to an EXISTING outside file had its real content silently wiped to zero bytes even though the call was correctly rejected as an escape. Predates ADR-138 entirely (the original, pre-ADR-138 design had the identical exposure), reachable through the same `write_file()` path (`append=false` is this codebase's own default write mode). **Fixed in this same pass**: `O_TRUNC` stripped from every `open()` call, applied via a real `ftruncate()` only AFTER containment is confirmed safe -- behavior-preserving for every legitimate caller (POSIX guarantees offset 0 either way). Verified: the probe's own content-preservation check, which failed before, passes after; full escape corpus and whole `ctest` suite re-run clean, zero regression. **ADR-140 (`kata_backend.cpp`)**: `AGENTENGINE_BUILD_KATA_BACKEND=ON` toggled on, `agentengine_kata_backend` built 4/4 steps, zero errors/warnings -- the new symlink-escape guard compiles clean on GCC, a genuinely new claim; reverted after. **No live Kata/containerd deployment available to exercise `create()` against a real malicious image -- this pass narrows but does not fully close ADR-140's own residual.** Everything else held with zero source changes: `test_identity_authority_grant` (ADR-141/142) all checks passed; `agentengine_cli_chat` (ADR-136) builds clean; `test_content_durability_concurrency` (ADR-137, including both of its own red-team follow-on fixes) ALL CHECKS PASSED, 10.4s+ real time confirming the full `[1b]`/`[2]`/`[2b]`/`[2c]` suite genuinely ran, not skipped. Full `ctest`: 189 total (post-cleanup baseline), the IDENTICAL 6 pre-existing Docker-CLI-unreachable failures every Linux-verification ADR since ADR-125 has established, zero regression anywhere else. |
| [144](ADR-144-native-jail-and-kata-backend-decomposition.md) | After the `AgentSession` god-class survey found the class already decomposed as far as a prior, red-teamed design draft judged safe, the user asked for the next two largest files: `native_jail_backend.cpp` (1550 lines) and `kata_backend.cpp` (1459 lines). Neither had a prior decomposition draft -- could either be meaningfully split, and which parts would only look separable by method name without actually being separable by data flow (the explicit lesson carried over from the `AgentSession` pass)? | **Proposed -- implemented, verified for real on both platforms (Windows/MSVC with real AppContainer/Job Object execution; Linux/GCC-14.2.0 in the established `/root/ae-verify` WSL2 checkout), full rebuild (zero errors, zero new warnings) and full `ctest` clean on both platforms (identical pre-existing-failure baseline), `naming_lint.py` clean.** Both files turned out to have the exact same shape as `AgentSession`: genuinely self-contained utility clusters alongside one tightly sequenced, staged-construction core bound together by a rollback closure and/or multi-entry-point state mutation -- not safely splittable. `native_jail_backend.cpp`: switched from a local, DIVERGED duplicate of `widen()`/`narrow()`/`HandleGuard` to an already-existing sibling header (`native_jail_win32_helpers.hpp`, already used by two other files in this subsystem) -- the divergence was a real latent double-close risk, since the local `HandleGuard` was accidentally copyable where the shared header's version explicitly deletes copy and defines move. Also moved the nine `dispatch_*` HandleRelay methods (already PRIVATE STATIC members of `NativeJailBackend`, declared in the header) to a second `.cpp` of the same CMake target -- zero interface change, ordinary C++ practice. Left `create()`/`exec()`/`destroy()`, worker spawn, the watchdog, and `exec_session()` alone -- they mutate `PythonWorkerState`'s phase/deadline/alive fields from three separate call sites each, the same non-separable shape `run_rounds()` has. `kata_backend.cpp`: moved the generic subprocess spawn/drain primitive (`run_ctr()`), small string/id helpers, and the OCI runtime-spec JSON builder (~470 of 1459 lines, zero `KataBackend` member-state coupling) into a new header. Left `create()`'s staged-construction pipeline alone -- bound together by `cleanup_partial()`'s single rollback closure over ~10-13 locals, called from ~15 early-return sites, plus `Instance`'s own state handoff to `destroy()`; the rollback-plus-handoff IS the design, not an accident. **SAME-DAY INDEPENDENT RED-TEAM on each backend**: native_jail got a clean bill of health plus one genuine adversarial mutation test (temporarily reordering the port-range-before-cast check to reintroduce the pre-fix UB, rebuilding, and confirming the full test suite STILL passed -- adversarially proving the disclosed "these two safety-critical checks have zero dedicated regression coverage" gap is real, not merely asserted, then reverting), and two cosmetic fixes (a stray UTF-8 BOM the implementer's own edit introduced, confirmed absent from the pre-refactor HEAD via `git show`; a "ten" vs the actual "nine" dispatch-function miscount in a new comment). kata_backend got a clean bill of health with an independent, from-scratch WSL2 rebuild (byte-identity confirmed via `cmp`, not just `diff`, after an unrelated BOM/CRLF quirk gave a false diff result) reproducing the exact same clean build and the same pre-existing, disclosed `ctr images mount` environment failure (ADR-110 §4), not a new one. Disclosed, not closed: neither of native_jail's two safety-critical relay-handler checks has dedicated test coverage (adding it needs a `friend` declaration, judged a real encapsulation change out of scope for a "zero behavior change" refactor); `kata_backend_detail.hpp`'s anonymous-namespace-in-a-header shape is safe today (one consumer) but would silently duplicate state if a second `.cpp` ever included it. |
| [145](ADR-145-containerd-execution-surface-promotion.md) | Does `ContainerdExecutionSurface` (design-only per `docs/planning/oci-execution-surface-design-draft.md`, proven standalone 16/16 in the prove-phase tree) promote cleanly into real production code, as the second real `ExecutionSurface` conformer alongside `DockerExecutionSurface`? | **Proposed — implemented, verified, and independently red-teamed (2026-08-29), a real build and real test runs against a live containerd/runc deployment.** A mechanical port of already-red-teamed prove-phase code (`docs/planning/proofs/execution_surface/{containerd_ctr_backend.hpp,containerd_execution_surface.hpp}`) into `include/agentengine/sandbox/containerd_execution_surface.hpp`, following the exact naming/type-substitution convention `DockerExecutionSurface`'s own promotion (ADR-102 Phase 3) established: `probe::ContainerdBackend`/`ContainerdExecutionSurface` → `agentengine::ContainerdCliBackend`/`ContainerdExecutionSurface`, `probe::result`/`probe::error` → the real `agentengine::result`/`agentengine::error{failure_class,...}`, `probe::ExecOutcome` → `agentengine::SurfaceRunOutcome`. Header-only (no new CMake library target, matching `DockerExecutionSurface`'s own shape), Linux-only (`ctr` only meaningfully talks to a local containerd Unix socket), new `NOT WIN32`-gated `test_containerd_execution_surface` with no special opt-in flag (deliberately lighter-weight than `test_kata_backend_linux`'s `AGENTENGINE_KATA_SANDBOX_TESTS` gate — `ctr`/containerd is a comparably light dependency to `docker`, not a KVM/Kata deployment). Ported test adds one new check beyond the original 16-check prove-phase probe: `drain_to()` to a directory OTHER than the one bind-mounted (the general `ExecutionSurface` contract, previously implemented but never exercised) is now proven, not just implemented. Run as root (containerd's default socket permissions require it) against a real containerd 2.2.2 + runc 1.4.0 deployment: **18/18 checks passed**, the THIRD independent confirmation of the bind-mount-vs.-`SandboxRuntime::run()`-ordering-hazard finding being benign for this environment (after the original bash-script probe and the prove-phase C++ probe), now through the real production code path for the first time. An independent red-team round confirmed every `failure_class` choice correctly matches `docker_execution_surface.hpp`'s own convention, reproduced the 18/18 real-daemon run and the clean non-root `permission denied` failure independently, found no ODR issue from the header-only multi-TU inclusion, and found no injection bypass (tried `host_dir` containing literal `=`/`:`, both mount cleanly; the already-disclosed comma-grammar limitation reproduces exactly as documented). It also found two REAL, previously-undisclosed things: (1) two non-mechanical deviations from the prove-phase original beyond the claimed three substitutions — the move constructor/assignment now correctly moves/swaps the `ContainerdCliBackend` member (the prove-phase original silently dropped it, losing `exec_seq_`'s count on every move — a real, low-severity fix, not a regression, now disclosed accurately rather than left implied as "unchanged"), and the `not_reset` contract-error code string was sharpened from a generic to a per-class name matching `DockerExecutionSurface`'s own convention; (2) `host_dir_str` had no embedded-NUL guard unlike `id`/`image`/`command`, an inconsistency with this file's own "reject outright, never truncate" principle — **fixed same day** (`reject_embedded_nul()` now also guards it), re-verified 18/18 still passing. The red-team also confirmed and empirically reproduced (via a real SIGKILL test, cleaned up afterward) a genuine, **structurally unavoidable** residual missing from this ADR's own first draft: a container is orphaned indefinitely if the host process dies between a successful `reset()` and this class's own destructor running, because `ctr run --help` documents `--rm` as incompatible with the `--detach`/`-d` flag this conformer must use — unlike `DockerExecutionSurface`'s own (also imperfect) `--rm`-based attempt at a safety net, there is no CLI-level auto-cleanup available here at all; a real fix (persisting reclaimable instance ids, mirroring `Ledger`'s own orphan-branch/A7 design) is named, disclosed follow-on work. Full Linux `ctest` (208 tests, 207 pre-existing + this ADR's own new one): 202/208 passed, exactly the same 5 pre-existing ADR-105-disclosed failures plus this ADR's own new test failing cleanly as a normal (non-root) user with the documented permission-denied diagnostic — confirmed via `git`-diff-scoped review that no file this ADR did not touch behaves any differently. Windows configure re-verified clean and unaffected (no Windows target references the new Linux-only header). Explicitly out of scope at write time, later closed: wiring `ContainerdExecutionSurface` into any real, user-reachable session/tool surface. **SINCE CLOSED (2026-08-29, same session)**: `tools/containerd_shell_chat.cpp` (a near-verbatim port of `tools/sandboxed_shell_chat.cpp`, one template argument swapped) plus `tests/test_composed_containerd_providers_live.cpp` (same relationship to `tests/test_composed_sandbox_providers_live.cpp`) — the latter run as root against a real containerd/runc daemon, `ALL CHECKS PASSED`, the first real production use of `MandatorySandboxProvider<ContainerdExecutionSurface>` through the actual, unmodified `session.start_run() -> invoke_tool()` pipeline anywhere in this codebase. An independent red-team round `diff`'d both new files against their templates line by line and found the port genuinely faithful (every difference is exactly the template argument, string literals, and paths — zero silent logic changes), independently reproduced every empirical claim, verified the CMake link lists are correct by tracing what `sandbox_runtime.hpp` actually needs, confirmed zero new authority path, and independently reproduced the container-orphan residual for the composed case specifically via a real SIGKILL test (not worse than the standalone case). Full Linux suite: 207/213 (97%), the 6 failures being the same 5 pre-existing environment-dependent ones plus this new composed test's own expected non-root failure. Still explicitly out of scope, named for later work: the orphan-reclaim fix itself; re-verifying the ordering-hazard finding against a deployment filesystem other than WSL2's ext4-on-virtio. |
| [146](ADR-146-ci-containerd-permission-and-docker-daemon-contention.md) | CI's Linux job never ran `test_containerd_execution_surface`/`test_composed_containerd_providers_live` before this PR (they don't exist on `main` yet) and hit their known containerd-socket-permission requirement immediately; a follow-up run also showed 4 more live-Docker/containerd tests failing together under `ctest -j 4`, non-deterministically. Was the second failure a real logic regression, or daemon contention from running multiple live-daemon tests concurrently with no serialization between them? | Proposed — implemented. Split the Linux job's `Test` step so the two containerd tests run in a separate, `sudo`-elevated, narrowly-`-R`-scoped step (root confined to just those two, not the whole suite, to avoid flipping the escape-corpus tests that specifically assert a POSIX permission DENIAL). Diagnosed the second failure as daemon contention, not a regression (non-deterministic across two identical-code runs, one test's own POSITIVE control failing with no logic path that should depend on concurrent test state) — fixed by giving all 6 Docker-daemon tests a shared `RESOURCE_LOCK docker_daemon` and both containerd-daemon tests a shared `RESOURCE_LOCK containerd_daemon`, so CTest never schedules two same-daemon tests concurrently regardless of `-j`, without slowing the rest of the suite. Verified via CMake reconfigure + `ctest --show-only=json-v1` (all 6 Windows-visible locks confirmed registered) and `naming_lint.py`; real end-to-end verification is the next CI run on this PR, explicitly flagged as such (no containerd daemon or reproducible multi-core Docker-contention rig on this Windows session). |

Explicitly does NOT claim to test model propensity, scheming, or the eval-awareness confound the source papers name — no model is in this gate's loop; a future live-model gate needs its own ADR (model-version pinning, cost, flakiness governance, all currently undecided in this project). |
| [148](ADR-148-model-call-gateway-routing-and-sticky-tiers.md) | GitHub issue #16 named three findings against `ModelCallGateway` vs. Microsoft.Extensions.AI's routing/failover primitives (design draft: `docs/planning/model-call-gateway-routing-design-draft.md`).
